# Current state (last updated: 2026-08-03)


Update this file after every completed ship (see `HANDOFF-UPDATE.md`).

**Run Sheet header anchor: the iPhone report was a DEPLOYMENT gap, not a code bug -- plus a real cross-engine hardening (2026-08-03):** Isaac reported from live iPhone testing that Save still appeared on the right and pushed Cancel left. **Root cause: the fix was never in what he was testing.** All three anchor commits (`30a9459` DOM reversal, `b9884dd` invariance test, `b1dfa19` vertical anchor) live only on `run-sheet-visual-polish`; `origin/main` -- and therefore Production -- still had the *old* order, Cancel at line 1036 and Save at 1044, i.e. Save as the last child of a `justify-end` row, which is exactly the "Save pushes Cancel left" behaviour described. Verified by reading `git show origin/main:app/components/EventRunSheetSection.tsx` directly rather than assuming. **Lesson for future reports: check which ref the reporter is actually running before debugging the code.** The branch's own Preview deployment has always had the fix.

**Hardened anyway, because the report came from iOS Safari and every measurement to date had been Chromium.** The collapse relied on `overflow: hidden` to zero the flex item's automatic minimum size: a flex item's `min-width: auto` resolves to its content width, and **min-width beats max-width**, so without that neutralisation the button refuses to shrink past "SAVE" and keeps displacing Cancel. The flexbox spec says `overflow` other than `visible` zeroes the automatic minimum, but leaning on one engine's handling of that interaction is exactly the class of thing that differs on WebKit. Added an explicit `min-width: 0` to `.ftc-run-sheet-save-btn` so the collapse is stated outright rather than implied. Confirmed in the **compiled** CSS bundle, not just source: `.ftc-run-sheet-save-btn{opacity:0;pointer-events:none;min-width:0;max-width:0;...}`. **Not reproduced on real WebKit** -- this machine has Command Line Tools only, no Xcode and no `simctl`, so there is no iOS Simulator and no WebKit engine available at all; the hardening is reasoned from the spec, not from a reproduction.

**Verification method upgraded.** Every previous check used a hand-written replica harness, which is precisely the blind spot a "works for me" result hides. This pass (a) verified the **built bundle** directly -- the emitted chunk has the Save class before the Cancel class, confirming Cancel really is the last child in shipped output, and the `flex min-h-10 items-center justify-end` row is present -- and (b) generated the debug harness by extracting the header-actions JSX and `RUN_SHEET_SAVE_BUTTON_CLASS` **verbatim from the component source**, so it cannot drift from production. Measured right edges with that harness, all three states identical: **359 / 359 / 359 at 390px** and **935.5 / 935.5 / 935.5 at 1280px**; vertical centres 97/97/97 and 99/99/99; row height a constant 40px; Save 0px when clean, ~73px when dirty with a 12px gap, left of Cancel, unclipped; no horizontal overflow at either width. `testRunSheetCancelIsTheFixedAnchor` now pins `min-width: 0` with a comment explaining it is load-bearing. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, full `npm run test:regressions` all clean.

**Run Sheet: the header anchor is now fixed on BOTH axes -- the missing half was vertical (2026-08-03):** the horizontal anchor was already correct (Cancel last child of a `justify-end` row, `30a9459`), but the header still did not feel settled, and re-measuring the *vertical* axis found why. **Pressing Edit moved the control 11px downwards** -- measured: Edit centre 86px, Cancel centre 97px, with the actions row jumping 18px -> 40px. Cause: the row had no height of its own, so in view mode it was only as tall as the Edit text link; Save's `min-h-10` (40px) then snapped it on entering edit, and `items-center` turned that snap into a re-centre of the very control the user had just pressed. So "replace EDIT with CANCEL in exactly the same position" held horizontally and failed vertically. **Fix: give the actions row the same `min-h-10` Save already carries**, so its height is constant in every state and the centre line never moves. One class, no logic touched, no new token (reuses Save's existing height). Re-measured after: centres **97 / 97 / 97 at 390px** and **99 / 99 / 99 at 1280px**, right edges **359 / 359 / 359** and **935.5 / 935.5 / 935.5**, row height a constant 40px, Save 61.2px wide with a 12px gap and vertically centred on Cancel, unclipped, no horizontal overflow. Card height unchanged (72px view / 70px editing, that 2px being the progress line which already hides while editing) -- so the taller row costs nothing, because the title block was already the taller side of the header.

`testRunSheetCancelIsTheFixedAnchor` gained the vertical half: it asserts the row reserves the **same** min-height as Save (`assert.equal(rowMinH, saveMinH)`, not two independent literals, so the two can never silently diverge) and that the row stays `items-center` -- centring is what converts any height change into a jump, so both must hold together. **Mutation-tested, 3/3 caught:** removing `min-h-10` from the row (the original 11px bug), setting the row to `min-h-8`, and moving Save to `min-h-12` while leaving the row at 10 -- that last one passes every literal assertion and is caught only by the equality check (`'10' !== '12'`). Two superseded container-class assertions in `testRunSheetVisualHierarchyPolish` and `testRunSheetHeaderAlignmentAndDensity` were loosened to `flex[^"]*items-center justify-end` so the row's class is pinned in exactly one place. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, full `npm run test:regressions` all clean.

**Run Sheet: Cancel is now the fixed anchor of the header (2026-08-03):** layout only -- no handler, dirty-detection, validation, save or any other Run Sheet behaviour change. The previous pass stopped Cancel jumping when *entering* edit mode, but Cancel still moved when Save appeared. Root cause was ordering, not offsets: Save was **last** in source, so in the `justify-end` row Save owned the right edge and expanding it out of zero width pushed Cancel ~73px left. **Fix: reverse the two.** Cancel is now the final child, owns the right edge outright, and Save expands leftward into the space beside it -- so Cancel cannot be displaced by construction, with no offset arithmetic anywhere. The 12px separation moved from Save's `margin-left` to its `margin-right` along with the reversal (Save now sits on Cancel's left); it still rides on the button rather than a container `gap`, so it collapses with the button instead of surviving as a phantom offset. Measured live, all three states sharing one right edge: **390px -> Edit 359 / Cancel-clean 359 / Cancel-dirty 359** (Save 61.2px wide, 12px gap, to Cancel's left); **1280px -> 935.5 for all three**. `cancelMovesWhenSaveAppears: false`. Row height still 40px in both edit states, so the DJ cards below never shift. Unchanged and re-asserted: Save stays permanently mounted while editing (`min-height` never collapsed), visibility is still the dirty-gated 190ms CSS transition, and `aria-hidden`/`tabIndex` still make the collapsed button unreachable by pointer and keyboard. **Accessibility bonus:** DOM order now matches visual order (Save then Cancel, left to right), where previously it was reversed.

New regression `testRunSheetCancelIsTheFixedAnchor` owns ordering and the margin side, and pins the things that must not regress with them (no container `gap`, Edit and Cancel sharing the identical anchor class, `min-height`/`display:none` never used to collapse Save, dirty-detection memo untouched). Superseded ordering/margin-side assertions in `testRunSheetHeaderAlignmentAndDensity` and `testRunSheetVisualHierarchyPolish` were relaxed with pointer comments to the new owner rather than deleted. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` and the full `npm run test:regressions` all clean.

**Follow-up: that test now asserts Cancel's horizontal invariance directly.** The regression suite has no layout engine (its DOM helpers are hand-stubbed rects, and `happy-dom` — present as a devDependency but unused here — would not compute flexbox anyway), so pixel positions cannot be measured in Node. It therefore asserts the layout *contract that derives* the position, which is the stronger check: it holds at every intermediate width during the reveal, not just at the two endpoints a snapshot would catch. In a `justify-content: flex-end` row the last item's right margin edge coincides with the container's content-box right edge for any set of item widths, so Cancel's x depends only on the container provided (a) Cancel is the last child, (b) it contributes no trailing horizontal box, and (c) the collapsed Save adds no width. (c) is a real computation, not a string match: the test parses `max-width`/padding/margin off the collapsed rule **plus `border` off the shared `.ftc-btn-primary`** and requires the sum to be exactly 0px — folding in the shared class because a border added there later would silently reintroduce width while the collapse rule still looked correct. **Mutation-tested, 4/4 caught:** `max-width: 0 -> 4rem`, `padding-left: 0 -> 0.75rem`, reverting the Save/Cancel DOM order, and adding `border: 2px solid red` to `.ftc-btn-primary` (that last one slips past every literal assertion and is caught only by the computed sum). Live-measured for the record: Cancel's right edge is **359px at 390px** and **935.5px at 1280px**, identical across view / clean / dirty, with Save 0px wide when clean and 61.2px plus a 12px gap when dirty; row height stays 40px in both edit states so nothing below shifts.

**Crew Chat grouping + narrow-bubble fix — final presentation pass (2026-08-03):** two real defects, both fixed at the cause.

**(1) Narrow-bubble collapse — root cause proven in-browser, not inferred.** `resolveChatMessageBubbleShellClass` gave a `min-w-[2.75rem]` floor to the `veryShort` and `compact` branches but **left the long/multiline branch with bare `px-4 py-2.5` and no floor at all** — and that is the *only* branch whose text carries `overflow-wrap: break-word`, meaning its min-content width is a **single character**. The bubble is `w-fit max-w-full`, and that `max-width: 100%` resolves against an ancestor column carrying `min-w-0`, which explicitly permits shrinking below min-content. So the one branch that could collapse to one character per line was the one branch with nothing stopping it. **Measured live at 320px: a long bubble went 251.6px → 32px** under that pressure (≈ one character plus padding), and an `8rem` floor holds it at 128px with 96px of text. Both floors are now named constants (`CHAT_BUBBLE_MIN_WIDTH_SHORT_CLASS`, `CHAT_BUBBLE_MIN_WIDTH_LONG_CLASS`). **`8rem` sits far below the natural width at every supported viewport** (85% of 296px = 251.6px at 320px), so it is purely a lower bound and changes nothing in normal rendering — verified: short pills still measure 44px, compact ones 92–166px, i.e. *below* the long floor, confirming it is branch-specific and does not widen ordinary chat. Note a discarded first hypothesis: giving the outgoing column `w-full` to match the incoming cell. Tested and it did **not** repair the collapse — `flex-basis` overrides `width` on a flex item — so the floor is the correct lever, not the structural change.

**(2) Two grouping calculations that provably disagreed.** Crew chat ran `buildGroupChatSenderNameVisibility` (name) and `buildChatMessageGroupLayout` (avatar + spacing) independently, and they contradicted each other in two concrete ways: the layout pass was fed a list with **system rows filtered out**, so two messages either side of an Event Updated card became adjacent and grouped together — dropping the avatar and tightening spacing — while the name pass correctly broke on them; and **only the layout pass knew about time gaps**, so after a centred timestamp separator the avatar broke but the sender name did *not* reappear. Both are now derived from one predicate in new `buildCrewChatMessageGroups(messages, currentUserId)`, which returns `startsSenderGroup` / `endsSenderGroup` / `showSenderName` / `showAvatar` / `position` / `tightWithPrevious`. A run breaks on: different sender, an app-authored row on either side (system notice **or** Event Updated card, via `isRichCrewChatMessage`), a new calendar day (explicitly — so a 23:59 → 00:01 break still splits despite a small clock gap), or the shared `DM_CHAT_MEANINGFUL_TIME_GAP_MS` threshold the separators already use, so grouping and separators cannot drift apart. System rows are now deliberately **passed through** to the helper rather than filtered — it needs to see them to break on them. Outgoing follows the identical rhythm but never renders the current user's own name or avatar.

**Reactions untouched and verified still per-message:** the reaction slot lives inside the per-message `CHAT_MESSAGE_BUBBLE_FRAME_CLASS` and is absolutely positioned, so grouping cannot lift it onto a run or push neighbouring grouped messages around. Long-press, double-tap and removal are unchanged. Confirmed live that zero reaction slots render outside a `[data-chat-message-id]` row.

**Verification.** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, full `npm run test:regressions` all clean. Two new tests: `testCrewChatMessageGrouping` drives the real helper over the exact Isaac ×3 / DJ ×2 / Isaac ×1 shape and asserts name-only-on-first `[T,F,F,T,F,T]`, avatar-only-on-last `[F,F,T,F,T,T]`, matching positions, each break reason in isolation (time gap, cross-midnight day change, Event Updated card, booking notice), that system cards never inherit a human name or avatar, that outgoing shows neither while keeping the rhythm, and a cross-check that name/avatar can never disagree with the spacing flags. `testChatBubbleCannotCollapseToOneCharacterColumn` pins both floors, that the long branch keeps its padding and `max-w-full`, that short/compact do **not** inherit the long floor, that attachment-only bubbles get no text floor, and that reactions stay frame-scoped. Live-verified at **320px (iPhone SE — smallest supported), 390px and 1280px**: no horizontal overflow at any width, no scroller overflow, long unbroken 72-character string wraps cleanly across the full bubble, `ok` stays 44px, composer usable, bottom nav stable, header readable.

**Not verified live: incoming grouping with multiple senders.** The QA crew chat only has one participant actually sending, so every rendered message is outgoing — the live pass confirmed outgoing correctly renders **zero** names and **zero** avatars, but the multi-sender name/avatar placement rests on the unit tests above, which exercise the real helper directly rather than matching source text. Image messages, booking cards and reacted messages were likewise not present to photograph; their grouping is covered by the helper's break rules and the reaction-scope assertions.

**Crew Chat keyboard, round 2 — the real cause was the page shell, not focus (2026-08-03):** round 1 (`e6b5585`, entry below) removed `disabled={busy}` from the composer textarea and added DM's focus capture/restore. That was a **genuine bug and a necessary fix — but not sufficient**, and the keyboard still dismissed on every send. The reason round 1 looked like it worked is that it was verified in Chromium, where `document.activeElement` correctly survived the send; the iOS dismissal is **not driven by focus loss at all**, so a focus-level check could never have caught it.

**Root cause: the crew chat shell was pinned to the layout viewport.** Crew chat rendered its own inline shell, `fixed inset-0 flex flex-col overflow-hidden`, while DM uses the shared `FIXED_CHAT_PAGE_SHELL_CLASS` = `fixed inset-x-0 top-0 z-0 flex h-[100dvh] max-h-[100dvh] …`. `inset-0` resolves height from the **layout** viewport, which iOS does *not* shrink when the software keyboard opens — only the **visual** viewport shrinks. So the composer, anchored to the bottom of that shell, sat underneath the keyboard, and WebKit compensated by scrolling the *document* to chase the focused input. Compounding it, crew chat never called `useFixedChatPageDocumentReset` (DM does), so the document behind the fixed shell stayed genuinely scrollable — there was something for WebKit to scroll. Then on send the list re-renders, `markUserSentMessage()` auto-scrolls to the newest message, and the composer textarea shrinks back to one line via the autogrow `useLayoutEffect` (`value` → `""`). WebKit resolves that conflict between its keyboard-driven document offset and the app's own scrolling by **dismissing the keyboard**. DM never hits any of this: `100dvh` keeps the composer above the keyboard so no document offset is needed, and the document is locked flat anyway.

**Fix — pure reuse, no new mechanism, no timeouts.** Two lines of substance: swap the inline shell for `FIXED_CHAT_PAGE_SHELL_CLASS`, and call `useFixedChatPageDocumentReset(\`${pathname}?${searchParams.toString()}\`)` exactly as DM does. Both come from `lib/navigation/prepareFixedChatPageMount.ts` / `useFixedChatPageDocumentReset.ts` — the same modules DM already depends on. Blast radius is small because the *inner* structure was already identical to DM (`AppNavigation`, then a `mx-auto max-w-2xl flex min-h-0 flex-1 flex-col overflow-hidden` column carrying `MOBILE_NAV_OFFSET_CLASS`, then header, scroller, composer); the shell class was the only divergence. Round 1's composer work is kept — it fixed a real, separate blur source.

**Ruled out during investigation** (checked, not assumed): no remounts or key changes (the composer subtree is positionally stable), no `blur()` on the send path — the only `blur()` in the chat stack is in `dismissComposerKeyboardOnIntentionalScroll`, which fires solely on a deliberate *touch* gesture and is shared with DM, no form submit (the Send control is `type="button"` with `onPointerDown` preventDefault), no navigation/state replacement of the input, and `resetMobileSoftwareKeyboardSession()` only clears a boolean latch — it never touches focus.

**Verification.** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, full `npm run test:regressions` all clean. `testCrewChatComposerKeepsFocusAfterSend` extended to pin both halves: `FIXED_CHAT_PAGE_SHELL_CLASS` present, the old `fixed inset-0` shell explicitly absent, `useFixedChatPageDocumentReset` called, the shared module still carrying `h-[100dvh] max-h-[100dvh]` and `html.style.overflow = "hidden"`, and DM asserted to still own both mechanisms independently. Live at 390px and 1280px: shell class confirmed as DM's, `html`/`body` `overflow: hidden`, `scrollRestoration: "manual"`, **`documentScrollable: false`** (nothing left for WebKit to scroll), header at top, composer inside the viewport, no horizontal overflow, all 9 messages rendering, consecutive sends keeping focus with the document never scrolling (`docScrollTop: 0` throughout). No hydration errors introduced.

**Honest limit — this is a hypothesis-driven fix I could not confirm on-device.** This machine has Command Line Tools only, no Xcode, so there is no iOS Simulator and no WebKit here; Chromium does not split visual/layout viewport the way iOS does, so the *actual* mechanism is unreproducible locally. What is proven is that the divergence from DM was real, that the fix restores exact parity with the surface that works, and that nothing regressed at either viewport. **If the keyboard still dismisses after this, do not guess a third time** — attach the iPhone and use Safari Web Inspector (Develop menu) to watch `visualViewport.height`, `document.scrollingElement.scrollTop` and `document.activeElement` across a send. That will settle it in one session.

**Unrelated pre-existing defect found while testing (not fixed — SQL is out of scope per FTC_WORKFLOW §2):** every crew-chat send logs `NotificationCreateError: Could not choose the best candidate function between: public.create_notification(p_user_id, p_type, p_title, p_body, p_link)` and the same signature plus `p_reaction_id`. Two overloads differ only by an optional trailing arg, so Postgres cannot resolve the call. It fails soft — the message still sends — but **crew chat notifications are silently not being delivered**. Worth a dedicated task: drop or rename one overload.

**Crew Chat composer keeps focus across a send (2026-08-03):** the iOS keyboard closed after every crew-chat send, breaking rapid back-and-forth. **Root cause, single line: `<ComposerMessageField … disabled={busy} />`.** A disabled form control cannot hold focus, so the browser blurs it *synchronously* the instant `sending` flips true, and iOS closes the keyboard with it. Nothing restored focus afterwards because crew chat had **no focus plumbing at all** — no input ref, no blur handler, no restore. DM never had the bug because `DmComposer` never disables its textarea (only the Send button) and the DM page already owns a capture/restore mechanism. This was drift, not a missing feature: the fix is reusing DM's mechanism verbatim, not inventing one.

**Fix (mirrors DM exactly).** `GroupChatComposer` drops `disabled={busy}`, gains `inputRef` / `composerRootRef` / `onInputBlurWhileBusy`, forwards the external ref through `useComposerTextareaAutogrow(value, inputRef)`, and adds `onBlur={handleInputBlur}`. The page gains `composerInputRef`, `composerRootRef`, `keepComposerFocusedAfterSendRef`, plus `captureComposerFocusIntentForSend` / `restoreComposerInputFocus` / `handleComposerInputBlurWhileBusy` built on the existing `lib/dm/restoreComposerInputFocus.ts` — no new helper module. Intent is captured **before the first await** (while the composer is still `document.activeElement` and, on mobile, only when the software keyboard is actually open — `shouldKeepComposerFocusedAfterSend` checks both). Restore runs in `finally`, so it covers success *and* failure; `restoreComposerInputFocusElement` refocuses inside a `requestAnimationFrame` with `preventScroll: true`, which is why there is no keyboard flicker and no scroll jump. Both send paths are wired — text and attachments.

**How intentional dismissal is distinguished from post-send focus loss** — two independent guards, neither a timeout: (1) `handleComposerInputBlurWhileBusy` clears the refocus intent the moment the user blurs *while a send is running*, so a deliberate dismissal mid-send is never undone; (2) `useDismissComposerKeyboardOnIntentionalScroll(scrollRef, composerInputRef, composerRootRef)` — the same hook DM uses — owns the drag-to-dismiss gesture. Because refocus is gated on an intent flag that was only ever set when the composer already had focus and the keyboard was already open, none of the forbidden cases can trigger it: first entry, returning from another screen, closing a lightbox or reaction picker, or tapping outside all leave the flag false.

**Unchanged and verified unchanged:** duplicate-send guard (`sending || uploading` early-return plus the Send button's own `disabled={busy || !canSend}`), draft preservation (`setInput("")` sits *after* the awaited send inside `try`, so a throw leaves the draft), Return-key behaviour (shared `handleComposerNewlineKeyDown` — Return inserts a newline, it has never been a send key in either surface), ordering, reactions, read receipts, grouping, attachments. **DM was not touched at all** — zero DM files in the diff, so it cannot have regressed.

**Verification.** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, full `npm run test:regressions` all clean. New `testCrewChatComposerKeepsFocusAfterSend` pins the root cause specifically (`doesNotMatch` on any `disabled=` reaching `ComposerMessageField`), the ref/blur plumbing, the intent-clearing blur handler, the dismiss hook, the draft-clear ordering (`indexOf(await send) < indexOf(setInput(""))`) and the duplicate-send guard — and asserts DM still owns the same mechanism independently. Live-instrumented at 390px against a real crew chat, sampling every 8ms across each send: **`everDisabledDuringSend: false`, `everBlurredDuringSend: false`** over 15 samples — the textarea is never disabled and never loses focus at any point. Consecutive sends kept focus with no extra tap. Intentional blur mid-send left the composer correctly **un**focused after the send completed. **Caveat — this browser is Chromium, not WebKit**, so the iOS keyboard itself was not observed; what was proven is the exact mechanism the keyboard follows (focus retention). **Case not genuinely tested: a failed send.** Monkey-patching `window.fetch` to reject did not actually fail it — the Supabase client retried once `fetch` was restored and the message landed late — so the draft-preservation-on-failure guarantee rests on the code path (`setInput` after the await) and its regression assertion, not an observed failure. Image/attachment sends were not exercised live either, though that path received the same capture/restore treatment.

**QA data note:** five test messages ("focus probe one", "rapid two", "rapid three", "dismiss during send", "this send will fail") were left in the QA crew chat on event "Test 3". Resettable via `QA_RESET_TO_PASTE.sql`.

**Crew Chat timestamps now match DM exactly (2026-08-03):** two separate defects, one shared root pattern — crew chat had drifted from DM's timestamp architecture in both halves. Message ordering, reactions, read receipts, day separators, grouping, bubble styling, system cards and booking cards are untouched; only timestamp rendering and separator clustering changed.

**(1) Duplicate per-message timestamps.** DM renders *every* per-message `<time>` as `hidden`/`sr-only` (`DmTextMessageBubble`, `DmIncomingMessageLayout`, `DmBookingTimelineNotice`) and shows time only in the centred `DmChatTimeSeparator`. Crew chat's incoming rows already matched (`IncomingChatMessageLayout` was fixed in `2c47093`), but three paths still painted a visible time: `GroupChatMessageBubble`'s **outgoing** branch, its **system-card (Event Updated)** branch, and `GroupChatSystemNotice`. All three now use DM's exact form, `<time dateTime={createdAt} hidden>` — kept in the DOM for machine readability, invisible. The `showTimestamp` prop that gated them is deleted from the component *and* the page, matching DM which has no such prop (and already had `assert.doesNotMatch(..., /showTimestamp/)` guards). **Note this supersedes the timestamp part of the 2026-08-03 event-update polish entry below** — that pass moved the card's timestamp gap `mt-0.5 -> mt-1` to sit it "in the timeline gutter"; the timestamp is now hidden entirely, so that spacing is moot. The card's separation, border and typography from that entry are unaffected.

**(2) Runs of centred separators with no message between them.** Root cause: `buildGroupChatTimestampLayout(messages)` clustered over **every** row, including rows that render nothing — whereas DM's `buildDmConversationTimestampLayout` classifies first and `.filter(kind !== "hidden")` *before* clustering. A row that paints nothing still claimed a separator slot, so N invisible rows with meaningful gaps produced N stacked timestamps (the reported 10:33 / 10:55 / 11:17). Crew chat now has the same shape: new `isVisibleGroupChatMessage()` + a filter inside the builder, so invisible rows get **no map entry at all** and their caller therefore renders no separator. The invisible cases are real, not hypothetical: a row with neither text nor attachments (`GroupChatMessageBubble` early-returns `null` — a failed upload, or an image row seen before its attachment rows arrive), and legacy crew-roster notices. Roster notices turned out *not* to be the live cause — they are already filtered on both the load path (`page.tsx:646`) and the realtime INSERT path (`:789`) — but the predicate still excludes them defensively, mirroring DM. The builder's signature now takes `text` + `hasAttachments`, and `attachmentsByMessageId` was moved above the memo that needs it (an image row has no text, so attachments are part of the visibility decision).

**Verification.** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, full `npm run test:regressions` all clean. Clustering is proven by **direct function calls on real inputs**, not source matching: a run of three invisible rows between two visible ones yields no entry for any ghost and exactly two separators total (one day marker + one time separator); image-only rows still cluster; day-vs-time precedence and cross-midnight still hold. Rendering is asserted over real source for all three crew components with an attribute-requiring `<time\s[^>]*>` regex (the convention from `testIncomingChatMessagesHaveNoAvatarTimestamp`, so prose mentions of `<time>` in doc comments can't false-match). **Live-verified at 390px** on a crew chat created for the purpose: 2 messages -> 3 `<time>` elements, **1 visible** (the `TODAY` separator), **0 visible per-message timestamps**. **Not live-verified: the Event Updated card and image messages** — the venue edit intended to generate an update card did not persist through programmatic form input, and no image rows existed; both render through the same `<time ... hidden>` code as the branch that was live-verified, and the system-card branch is pinned by an assertion scoped inside `if (systemAuthored)`. **Booking cards do not exist in crew chat at all** (no booking rendering in `app/events/[eventId]/chat/page.tsx`), so that listed scenario is vacuous there; DM's booking timeline notice was already `sr-only`.

**QA data note:** this created a crew chat on QA event "Test 3" (`58cb50be-…`) with two test messages, and notified the QA DJ account. Additive and resettable via `QA_RESET_TO_PASTE.sql`.

**Run Sheet: header-action alignment + ~10% denser expanded card (2026-08-03):** presentation-only. No handler, state, validation, save, reorder, expand/collapse, animation or database change. **Header alignment fixed at the root cause.** The top-right control appeared to jump sideways the instant Edit was pressed. Save is permanently mounted while editing (deliberately -- it reserves the row's height so the DJ cards below never shift), but while invisible it still occupied its full width, pushing Cancel **73px** left of the slot Edit occupies: 61.2px of button plus the container's own 12px `gap-3`. Measured before/after at 390px: Edit right edge 359 / Cancel-clean 297.8 **before**; Edit 359 / Cancel-clean **359** / Cancel-dirty 285.8 with Save at 359 **after** -- and identically at 1280px (935.5 for both). Fixed entirely in CSS without touching the mount, `aria-hidden` or `tabIndex` mechanic: hidden Save collapses `max-width` + horizontal padding to 0, and **the 12px separation moved off the container `gap` onto Save's own `margin-left`**, because a container gap survives the collapse as a phantom offset. `min-height` is deliberately *not* collapsed -- that is what keeps the row reserved at 40px in both edit states (verified: rowH 40 in clean and dirty alike). Visible Save is capped at `max-width: 8rem`, comfortably above the longest label ("Saving"); verified unclipped (`scrollWidth <= clientWidth`, width 61.2px). Collapse is animated, not a snap: `max-width`/`margin-left`/`padding` joined the existing 190ms ease-out transition alongside opacity/transform. **Tailwind v4 note:** this file is unlayered and Tailwind utilities live in a cascade layer, so these declarations beat `px-3` with no `!important`.

**Density: expanded card 233.5px -> 209.5px at 390px = 10.3%**, inside the requested 10-15% band, from whitespace only. Card padding went per-axis (`p-3` -> `px-3 pt-2.5 pb-2`, bottom tighter than top); panel stack `space-y-2.5 pt-2` -> `space-y-1.5 pt-0.5`; Set Time label `mb-1` -> `mb-0.5`; toggle margin `mt-1` -> `mt-0.5`. **Explicitly untouched:** the toggle's own `py-1` tap padding, both pinned textarea heights (2-row / 4-row), and the Set Time trigger's `min-h-[2.25rem]` -- all now pinned by assertions so a future density pass can't quietly eat a touch target. Edit mode was checked separately and specifically (real textarea + set-time-trigger classes, not just the read-only view): section gaps read 6px block-to-block but ~12.5px from a textarea's border to the next label once inline descender space is counted, so it does not feel cramped; no conditional edit-vs-read spacing was needed.

**Collapsed card left unchanged -- deliberate judgement, not an oversight.** The brief allowed stripping it back to number + avatar + name + chevron *only if* that improved scanability. It would not: the summary line ("Front room · 9:00 PM – 1:00 AM") is the only thing that lets a DJ find their own slot without opening every card, which is the collapsed card's entire job, and it was added one task earlier for exactly that reason. It is a single muted 12px truncated line -- low noise, high value. Reverting it would trade a real capability for a marginal tidiness gain.

New regression `testRunSheetHeaderAlignmentAndDensity` owns both halves, including guards that the hidden Save never collapses its height or uses `display: none` (either would reintroduce the card-shift bug), that Cancel precedes Save in source so Save owns the right edge, and that touch targets/inputs/textarea heights are intact. Superseded assertions in `testRunSheetAccordionRestructure`, `testRunSheetChromeReduction`, `testRunSheetSaveButtonPolish`, `testRunSheetVisualHierarchyPolish` and `testRunSheetDensityAndSummaryPolish` were relaxed to generic patterns (e.g. `space-y-[\d.]+ pt-[\d.]+`) so future density tweaks don't require touching five tests. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, full `npm run test:regressions` all clean. **Dev-server gotcha hit again and worth repeating:** the `globals.css` edit did not take effect under Turbopack HMR -- the served rule was still the old one while component/Tailwind changes applied fine, which read as a broken fix. `rm -rf .next` plus a server restart resolved it. Always confirm a CSS change actually reached the browser (read the matched rule via `document.styleSheets`) before concluding it didn't work.

**Crew Chat event-update card: separation, softer border, timeline timestamp (2026-08-03):** visual-polish-only pass on the system card shipped in `cf53af9`. Layout, generation, parsing, grouping and ordering all untouched. **Left alignment preserved deliberately** -- it is now asserted, not incidental (`justify-start` present, `justify-center`/`mx-auto`/`items-center` absent), because "centre the system card" is the obvious wrong instinct for the next person. **Separation:** new `GROUP_CHAT_SYSTEM_CARD_SPACING_CLASS = "mb-4 mt-1.5"` in `chatMessageGroupLayout.ts` (the file that already declares itself the single source of truth for vertical rhythm). The list is `flex-col-reverse`, so `mb` opens the gap *above* and `mt` the gap *below* -- an inversion worth remembering. Measured live against the real stylesheet: gaps render **16px above and 16px below** vs a 10px normal-message baseline, i.e. **+6px each side**, inside the requested 4–8px. The below-gap is 16px rather than 6px because flex margins do not collapse: the card's `mt-1.5` (6px) adds to the next message's own `mb-2.5` (10px). Result is pleasingly symmetric. A sibling token `GROUP_CHAT_SYSTEM_CARD_AFTER_TIMESTAMP_SPACING_CLASS = "mb-0.5 mt-1.5"` keeps the top gap tight when a centred day/time separator already sits directly above, preserving the pre-existing behaviour.

**Why a dedicated `resolveSystemCardGroupLiClass` rather than appending margins to `resolveIncomingGroupLiClass`:** that resolver already emits `mb-2.5`, and two margin utilities on one element resolve by **stylesheet order, not class-attribute order** -- appending `mb-4` would have been a silent coin flip. The new resolver composes the row so there is exactly one margin utility per axis, and a regression assertion counts them (`mb-` and `mt-` occurrences must each be 1) so the footgun cannot come back. Dropping `followedByTimeSeparator` from this branch is **not** a behaviour change: for `position: "standalone"` in the compact variant it returned the same `mb-2.5` as the default path, verified by reading `resolveMessageListItemSpacingClass`.

**Border softened** from `border-[var(--ftc-color-primary-border)]` (alpha 0.28) to `border-ftc-primary/20` -- same cyan hue, taken from the registered `@theme` colour so no literal is duplicated, at 0.2 alpha. Confirmed live: `oklab(0.807846 -0.0902852 -0.0919067 / 0.2)`. Background (`bg-surface-raised`, `rgb(26,34,48)`) and the full-strength blue heading (`rgb(79,209,255)`) are unchanged, so the card still reads informative rather than as a warning/error frame, and the heading remains the single strongest accent. **Typography hierarchy untouched** -- `EventUpdateMessageContent.tsx` was not modified at all.

**Timestamp** kept FTC's shared treatment exactly (same formatter, `text-[10px]`, `text-ftc-text-muted`, same `showTimestamp` gating); only the gap above it went `mt-0.5` -> `mt-1` (2px -> 4px from the card), so it reads as sitting in the timeline gutter rather than as the card's own footer. No new timestamp style was introduced, per the explicit constraint.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, full `npm run test:regressions` all clean. Live-measured at **390px and 1280px** — identical results at both (16/16 gaps, same border alpha, left-aligned); the change contains **no responsive variants**, so parity is structural. Verified by injecting the exact rendered class strings into a live page and reading `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect` against the real compiled Tailwind, rather than by mutating QA data: the only crew chat available (`Test 3`) had no group chat yet, and creating one would have fired notifications to QA accounts for a purely presentational change. `testEventUpdateMessagePresentation` gained assertions that encode the *requirements* (4–8px delta computed from the Tailwind scale, one-margin-per-axis, left-alignment, softened border, unchanged timestamp tokens) rather than just pinning literals.

**iOS horizontal-pan: CLOSED as native Safari behaviour, with proof -- and the two entries below it are CORRECTED (2026-08-03):** third pass, run as investigation-only after two failed CSS fixes. **The two `overscroll-behavior` entries directly below this one were both wrong about the mechanism, and their reasoning should not be trusted or repeated.** `overscroll-behavior` does *not* control Safari's edge-swipe back/forward navigation gesture. The CSS Working Group has that open as an unresolved question ([w3c/csswg-drafts#7878](https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/public-css-archive/2022Oct/0545.html)), which states plainly: "as of now, none of browsers respect overscroll-behavior for swipe navigation." Both prior commits addressed overscroll *bounce/chaining* -- a genuinely different mechanism -- which is why the bug survived both. The declaration is kept (its Y-axis fixed-nav justification in `testIosOverscrollDoesNotClipFixedChrome` is real and independently tested, and X harmlessly stops scroll chaining out of the Calendar day strip), but every comment asserting it fixes the swipe gesture has been rewritten to say the opposite, loudly, so a fourth attempt doesn't repeat it.

**Positive proof the duplicated screens are not FTC's DOM.** Installed a recorder sampling on every `requestAnimationFrame` *plus* a `MutationObserver` on the whole body, then drove the exact reported sequence (Events -> Messages -> Profile, then two `history.back()`s). Across 15 samples covering all three forward navigations and both back navigations: **`MAX_MOBILE_NAVS_EVER = 1`** (never two nav bars mounted, at any sampled frame, including mid-transition) and **`MAX_SCROLLWIDTH_EVER = 390` = `innerWidth`** (never an oversized canvas at any instant). Observed nav sets were always exactly one `Main navigation` + one `Mobile navigation`, plus at most one *contextual* sub-nav (`Events area`, `Profile links`) -- which also explains the earlier confusing "navCount 2/3/4" readings: those were sub-navs, never duplicate app shells. **Next.js App Router does not retain previous route trees**; this is a React-level behaviour and therefore engine-independent, so Chromium is a valid place to prove it. Also ruled out by inspection: no `template.tsx`, no parallel-route `@slot` folders, no animation/transition library in `package.json`, no page-transition wrapper, no `translateX` anywhere in `app/`/`lib/`, no experimental flags in `next.config.ts`, and **no PWA manifest and no service worker** (so no standalone-mode or stale-cache divergence on device). Since the duplicate UI is provably absent from the document and the document is never wider than the viewport, the compositing necessarily happens outside the document -- that is Safari painting the previous history entry's snapshot during its native interactive back gesture, which is exactly what "two headers + two bottom navs side by side" looks like. The reported pairs ("Messages beside Profile", "Events beside Messages") are consecutive history entries, which for tab-order browsing are also adjacent nav tabs.

**Decision: no gesture-blocking code.** The one real mitigation is `touchstart` + `preventDefault()` on touches starting within ~20px of the left edge (`{passive:false}`; see [pqina write-up](https://pqina.nl/blog/blocking-navigation-gestures-on-ios-13-4/)) -- there is no API to disable the gesture properly. Measured its cost on `/events` first: the bottom-nav **Events/Gigs tab starts at `left: 2px`**, so a 20px blocker swallows ~14% of the primary workspace tab's hit area; the Events sub-nav pill and Active filter both start at `left: 16px`; and the page is vertically scrollable, so vertical scrolls begun in that strip would die. Isaac chose to leave the gesture alone -- correctly: swipe-back is behaviour users rely on across every iOS site, and suppressing a native affordance to hide a native animation trades a real regression for a cosmetic one. **If this is ever revisited, the honest framing is "should FTC break iOS swipe-back?", not "why is FTC overflowing?" -- it isn't.**

**One real FTC finding, logged but not changed:** `app/(planner-workspace)/events/EventsPageClient.tsx` (~L1400, L1413) and `app/components/bookings/DjGigsTabs.tsx` (~L76) call raw `window.history.pushState` for tab switching, bypassing the Next router. That does not cause the visual artifact, but it inflates history depth, so swipe-back lands on more intermediate states (tab-only changes) than a user expects. Worth revisiting if back-navigation UX is ever reported as confusing; out of scope here.

**Not reproduced on a physical iPhone, and here is exactly why that limit stands:** this machine has Command Line Tools only, no Xcode (`xcode-select -p` -> `/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools`, no `simctl`), so there is **no iOS Simulator and no real-WebKit engine available at all**; and Safari Remote Web Inspector needs a physically attached device driven through Safari's Develop menu, which no tool here can operate. The conclusion therefore rests on elimination that is complete on the DOM side (proven above) plus a documented, citable spec/browser-support gap -- not on a device reproduction. If it ever needs to go further, the only remaining step is a real Safari Web Inspector session against the iPhone.
**Run Sheet: density + collapsed-summary polish -- re-balanced completion line and Set Time, tighter expanded card, one-line collapsed summary (2026-08-03):** presentation-only follow-up to the visual-hierarchy entry directly below, which over-corrected two things into near-invisibility. No behaviour, handler, state, save logic, drag/reorder, expand/collapse, animation, or database change. **Completion/progress line lifted**: `text-ftc-text-muted` at normal weight -> `font-semibold text-ftc-text-secondary`, still `text-xs` so it stays clearly subordinate to the "Run Sheet" title. Applied to the whole line (both the "Run Sheet Complete" and "N of M DJs completed" states) rather than conditionally styling one -- same element, same slot, and a conditional className would add branching for no visual gain. Still no emoji, still no colour coding. **Read-only Set Time**: `font-medium` -> `font-semibold`, keeping `text-sm` and `text-ftc-text-secondary`. Ranking by *weight alone* is what produces the requested DJ name > Set Time > Stage / Area > labels ladder -- Stage / Area and Notes both render at `text-sm` **normal** weight through `BookingCardExpandableNotes`, so a heavier weight at the same size lifts Set Time exactly one tier without returning to the original oversized `text-base font-semibold text-ftc-text`. Measured live: 700 / 600 / 400 / 10px-uppercase-muted down the ladder. **Expanded panel tightened** `space-y-3` (12px) -> `space-y-2.5` (10px); measured 10px gaps live, a ~17% reduction inside the requested 15-20% band. Deliberately still a `space-y-*` block stack, never a grid -- see `testRunSheetAccordionRestructure` for the silent-clipping bug a grid reintroduces here. **Collapsed cards regained a one-line summary** under the DJ name: "Front room · 9:00 PM – 1:00 AM", or either half alone when that's all that exists. **Joined with ` · `, not the ` • ` in the brief** -- `·` is the separator this app already uses for compact metadata lines (`BookingRequestCard`, `DmBookingUpdateRow`, `EventDjSendOfferControls`, `DjAvailabilityCalendar`), and FTC_WORKFLOW §5 says align to the existing standard rather than adding a third variant; trivially swappable if Isaac prefers the bullet. No labels, single line, `truncate` for long stage names. **Rendered only while collapsed** (`!isExpanded && collapsedSummary`): expanded, both halves are already on screen under their own "Stage / Area" and "Set Time" labels, and repeating them in the header is precisely the duplication the previous pass removed. Editing force-expands every row, so the summary is automatically absent in edit mode with no separate `isEditing` check. `formatRunSheetSetTimeDisplay` is reused for the time half but gated on `hasSetTime`, because that helper returns the "TBC" placeholder for an empty pair -- which would read as real content in a summary line rather than as an absent value.

New regression `testRunSheetDensityAndSummaryPolish` owns all five changes, including a cross-file assertion that `BookingCardExpandableNotes` really is the lighter tier (so the Set Time ladder can't silently invert if that component restyles) and that the ` · ` separator is used rather than ` • `. Updated superseded assertions in `testRunSheetAccordionRestructure`, `testRunSheetChromeReduction`, `testRunSheetProductionPolish`, and `testRunSheetVisualHierarchyPolish`, each left with a pointer comment to the new owner rather than silently deleted. Two of those failed first for a non-obvious reason worth recording: the new multi-line ternary in the collapsed header broke assertions written as single-line `indexOf`/regex lookups of `isReadOnlyAndIncomplete ? "Run Sheet details pending" : ""` -- they now match across whitespace, since exact line wrapping is a formatting detail, not behaviour. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and the full `npm run test:regressions` suite all clean.

Live-verified at 390px and 1280px via a temporary debug route (deleted before commit). **A first pass showed the card overflowing the viewport at 390px -- this was a fault in the debug harness, not the component**: the harness root omitted `w-full`, which the real `EVENT_DETAIL_PAGE_SHELL_CLASS` has, so the width-constraint chain `truncate` depends on was never established. With the harness corrected to mirror the real shell, measured `documentElement.scrollWidth === innerWidth === 390`, zero elements past the viewport, and the 50-char-capped stage name truncating correctly (284px visible vs 411px content). Worth knowing for future visual checks: a debug harness that doesn't reproduce the real page's width constraints will manufacture overflow bugs that don't exist. Confirmed at both widths: completion line noticeably more legible, Set Time clearly outranks Stage / Area, expanded cards tighter, collapsed cards informative without labels or clutter, and the summary correctly absent when expanded. **One pre-existing item deliberately left alone** (out of scope, and touching it risks the chevron's tap target): when a row is expanded, the toggle button's now-empty summary span leaves a small band of blank space above "STAGE / AREA". That band predates this pass -- the span was already empty when expanded -- and the brief scoped spacing work to between the Stage / Area, Set Time and Notes sections.

**Mobile horizontal-overflow/rubber-band-pan fix, round 2 -- shorthand `overscroll-behavior` on both `html` and `body` (2026-08-03):** the round-1 fix (below, same day) did not resolve the issue on the reporter's device -- second report, with sharper detail ("Messages beside Profile", "Events beside Messages", full route views side by side). Investigated in diagnosis-first mode per explicit instruction, no speculative patch. **Re-ran the full measurement sweep** (Profile, Events, Event Plans, Gigs, Calendar, DM inbox, Settings, Event/Crew Chat) fresh: `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === document.body.scrollWidth === window.innerWidth` on every single route, zero elements with a bounding rect exceeding the viewport, `document.scrollingElement === document.documentElement`. Also checked and ruled out: no `template.tsx`, no parallel-route `@slot` folders, no animation/transition library in `package.json` (none), no custom page-transition wrapper or `translateX`-based slide code anywhere in `app/`/`lib/`, no experimental flags in `next.config.ts`. **Conclusion: there is no DOM/CSS element to find** -- the symptom (full route "views," including nav bars, appearing side by side) cannot be produced by anything in FTC's own render output, and matches iOS Safari's native edge-swipe-to-navigate-back gesture, which composites a snapshot of the previous route beside the current one entirely in browser chrome, outside the page DOM. **Why round 1 failed:** it added only the `overscroll-behavior-x: none` *longhand*, only on `<html>`; Safari's longhand support for `overscroll-behavior-x`/`-y` has lagged the combined shorthand across iOS versions, and `<body>` was left at its default (`auto`) even though `overflow-x: clip` has always been set on *both* `html` and `body` in this same rule block. **Fix:** replaced the two longhands with the single `overscroll-behavior: none;` shorthand on `html`, and added the same shorthand to `body` -- restoring the html/body symmetry `overflow-x: clip` already had. Same mechanism as round 1 (not `touch-action`, which was considered and rejected: unlike `overscroll-behavior`, a `touch-action` restriction on an ancestor cannot be re-widened by a descendant, so applying it at the root would have broken the Calendar mobile day-strip's horizontal scroll -- confirmed live that `overscroll-behavior: none` on the root does *not* have this problem: set the day-strip's `scrollLeft` programmatically post-fix and it moved freely, 0 -> 120). Updated `testIosOverscrollDoesNotClipFixedChrome` and `testHorizontalOverscrollDoesNotPanRevealAdjacentContent` to assert the shorthand and explicitly assert the longhands are *absent* (`assert.doesNotMatch(htmlRule, /overscroll-behavior-x:/)` etc.), so a future edit can't silently regress back to the round-1 form. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and the full `npm run test:regressions` suite all clean, run twice: once against the live working tree, once via `git stash push --keep-index -u` against the *exact* isolated state about to be committed (a concurrent session was mid-way through an unrelated Group Chat event-update-message feature touching `scripts/test-regressions.mts` among other files; only this fix's two hunks were isolated via manual patch extraction and `git apply --cached`, same discipline as prior shared-file edits this session -- their untracked new files were included in the `-u` stash this time too, so the verification build saw only what's actually being committed). Live-verified post-fix on a fresh dev server (a stale Turbopack HMR bundle initially still served the old longhand rule after the edit -- a dev-only caching artifact, not a code issue; a full server restart resolved it and is worth knowing if a future CSS edit "doesn't seem to take" mid-session) at 390px across every named route: `overscroll-behavior-x`/`-y` both compute to `none` on `html` and `body`, `scrollWidth === innerWidth` unchanged, Calendar day-strip scroll still works, and `history.back()` still navigates correctly. **Not independently reproduced on a physical iPhone** (no real-device access in this environment, and this Browser tool is Chromium/CDP-based so it cannot replicate WebKit's touch/edge-gesture physics either) -- the diagnosis rests on exhaustive absence-of-DOM-overflow evidence plus a well-founded cross-browser support gap in the exact property already in use, not a reproduction. If this is still visible on-device after this fix, the next diagnostic step is a real Safari Web Inspector remote-debug session (Mac + iPhone via the Develop menu) to get ground-truth WebKit behaviour, since no tool available in this environment can go further.
**Run Sheet: visual-hierarchy polish -- paired Save/Cancel, no duplicate Stage/Area, lighter Set Time and Edit, no completion emoji (2026-08-03):** presentation-only pass on top of the Save-button-polish entry directly below; no Run Sheet functionality, drag/reorder behaviour, saving logic, or database behaviour touched. **Save and Cancel now share one row** (`<div className="flex items-center gap-3">`) instead of Cancel sitting alone in its own row with Save stacked/nudged below it -- the "lone floating Save button" the pass exists to remove. Save keeps its exact dirty-gated CSS-transition visibility (`.ftc-run-sheet-save-btn`/`--visible`, `aria-hidden`/`tabIndex` on `hasUnsavedChanges`, unchanged) and its own class (`RUN_SHEET_SAVE_BUTTON_CLASS`); only the surrounding layout changed. **Edit now reuses Cancel's own `.ftc-form-cancel-link` text-link class** instead of the bordered `EVENT_DETAIL_BTN_SECONDARY` pill -- Edit and Cancel occupy the same top-right slot at different times, so giving them the same lightweight treatment makes the header read as one coherent affordance rather than two competing styles; `EVENT_DETAIL_BTN_SECONDARY` is no longer imported by this component. **Collapsed-row Stage / Area preview removed** -- `RunSheetEntry`'s toggle button used to show `row.stage_area` unlabelled right next to the same value shown labelled ("STAGE / AREA") one tap away in the expanded panel; the toggle row now shows only the existing "Run Sheet details pending" fallback for a read-only, incomplete row. **Read-only Set Time dialled back a tier**: `text-base font-semibold text-ftc-text` -> `text-sm font-medium text-ftc-text-secondary` (kept `tabular-nums`) so it no longer outweighs the DJ name above it, while staying clearly readable. **Green-dot emoji removed** from "🟢 Run Sheet Complete" -- now plain "Run Sheet Complete" text, styled identically to the muted "N of M DJs completed" sibling state; no colour-coded replacement added, since the text alone already says it.

New regression `testRunSheetVisualHierarchyPolish` covers all five changes directly against source (paired-row layout, Cancel-before-Save ordering, Edit's shared class, no `stagePreview ||` in the header, the new Set Time class, no emoji). Updated now-stale assertions in `testRunSheetAccordionRestructure`, `testRunSheetChromeReduction`, `testRunSheetProductionPolish`, and `testRunSheetSaveButtonPolish` that pinned the previous stacked-row layout, the old Set Time class, the emoji, and the `EVENT_DETAIL_BTN_SECONDARY` Edit button -- each left a pointer comment to the new test rather than being silently deleted, matching this file's existing convention for superseded assertions. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and the full `npm run test:regressions` suite all clean. Live-verified at 390px and 1280px via a temporary debug route (mirroring the exact changed markup/classes with local state toggles for `isEditing`/`hasUnsavedChanges`/`isFullyComplete`/row-expanded, since a full Supabase-backed event wasn't set up in this environment) -- confirmed Save/Cancel pair together with Save visually primary, Stage / Area appears once (under its label only), Set Time reads clearly but a tier below the DJ name, no emoji, and Edit reads as a lightweight secondary link -- identical behaviour and layout at both widths. Debug route deleted before commit.

**Mobile horizontal-overflow/rubber-band-pan fix -- root cause was missing `overscroll-behavior-x`, not an overflowing element (2026-08-03):** investigated the reported bug (iPhone Safari: horizontal swipe pans the whole app, revealing what looks like adjacent screens). Explicitly did not reach for `overflow-x: hidden` on the root as a band-aid, per the task brief -- and it turned out one was already partially in place (`overflow-x: clip` on `html`/`body`, added previously) and still didn't fix it, which is itself the tell. **Root-cause method:** live-swept every route the task named (Events, Event Detail, Event Chat, Calendar, Bookings/Gigs, DM inbox, Profile, Settings) at 390px with a full-DOM `getBoundingClientRect` scan plus `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` vs `window.innerWidth`, including deliberately loading QA content designed to stress it (a profile with a ~100-character unbroken-token bio, an event title with no natural break points). **Result: zero elements exceed the viewport on any route, on every page `scrollWidth === innerWidth` exactly.** The one place `scrollWidth` legitimately exceeds the viewport is the Calendar mobile day-strip (`PlannerCalendarMobileDateStrip`), and it's already correctly scoped -- its own `overflow-x: auto` container stays within its card, confirmed via ancestor-chain inspection that every ancestor up to `<html>` has `scrollWidth <= clientWidth`; this is the pre-existing, FTC_WORKFLOW-sanctioned "Calendar mobile day strip" intentional horizontal scroll, not the bug. **Actual cause:** iOS Safari's rubber-band/elastic bounce at the viewport edges is controlled by `overscroll-behavior`, a *separate* mechanism from `overflow` -- it runs whenever allowed, independent of whether the document has any real overflow to scroll into. `globals.css`'s `html` rule already had `overscroll-behavior-y: none` (added in an earlier task, `testIosOverscrollDoesNotClipFixedChrome`, to stop the fixed bottom nav visually detaching during a vertical bounce) but never had the X-axis equivalent, so a horizontal swipe on any page -- including ones with zero overflowing content -- could still trigger Safari's bounce and flash whatever sits behind the page (browser chrome, the adjacent bfcache'd route) during the pan. Added `overscroll-behavior-x: none;` beside the existing `overscroll-behavior-y: none;` on `html`, extended that rule's doc comment to explain both axes, and left `overflow-x: clip` completely untouched on both `html` and `body` -- this is closing the one missing half of an already-correct pattern, not adding a new overflow-hiding rule. New regression `testHorizontalOverscrollDoesNotPanRevealAdjacentContent` pins the new property, guards that `overflow-x: hidden` is never substituted in on the root as a band-aid, and sits alongside the existing Y-axis test. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and the full `npm run test:regressions` suite all clean. Live-verified at 390px (all named routes) and 1280px (Events) post-fix: `overscroll-behavior-x` computes to `none` and `scrollWidth === innerWidth` holds everywhere, matching the pre-fix baseline -- confirming the fix changes only bounce physics, not layout. **Not independently reproduced on a physical iPhone** (no real-device access in this environment) -- the diagnosis rests on the absence of any overflowing element combined with the exact, already-proven-effective pattern used for the identical Y-axis bug; Isaac should confirm on-device that the horizontal bounce is gone before considering this fully closed.

**Run Sheet: Save button polish -- reserved space, transition-driven reveal, softened reorder controls (2026-08-03):** UI/UX-only pass on top of the Cancel-in-header/dirty-gated-Save entry directly below; no business logic, validation, permissions, save behaviour or data flow touched. **"Unsaved changes" label removed** -- the Save button appearing already says that. **Copy: "Save run sheet" -> "Save"** (the in-flight "Saving" label is unchanged). **Save is now always mounted for the whole editing session**, not only while `hasUnsavedChanges` -- its row (`<div className="mt-1.5 flex w-full justify-end pr-3">`) is a sibling of Cancel's own row rather than sharing one `flex-col items-end` stack with it, so the row's height is reserved the instant Cancel appears and nothing below it (the DJ cards) can shift when Save later fades in. **Visibility is a CSS transition, not a mount/unmount**: a new `.ftc-run-sheet-save-btn` / `.ftc-run-sheet-save-btn--visible` pair in `globals.css` toggles `opacity` (0 -> 1) and `transform: translateY(7px) -> translateY(0)` over 190ms ease-out (replacing the old mount-triggered `@keyframes ftc-run-sheet-save-reveal`, which had nothing to transition between since Save used to unmount entirely); `pointer-events` follows the same toggle in CSS, and `aria-hidden`/`tabIndex` follow `hasUnsavedChanges` on the element itself so the invisible-but-present button is genuinely unreachable by mouse or keyboard, not just transparent. Reduced-motion respected (`transition: none` override), matching the app's existing convention. **Save moved left of Cancel and narrowed**: `RUN_SHEET_SAVE_BUTTON_CLASS` is `EVENT_DETAIL_BTN_PRIMARY`'s styling with `px-3` instead of `px-4` (not a shared-class edit, so nothing else using the primary button changed width), and its row carries `pr-3` so its right edge sits ~12px left of Cancel's -- confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect()` (Cancel right 927.5px, Save right 915.5px at 1280px). **Cancel itself now sits in its own `<div>`, independent of Save's row** -- confirmed live that its bounding rect is pixel-identical whether Save is visible or hidden. **Reorder controls (`RowMoveButton`) softened**: idle-state border/background dropped from full-strength (`border-ftc-border-strong`, `bg-ftc-bg-elevated/80`) to `/60` opacity each, so the control reads lighter next to the DJ information it sits beside; `hover` stays full-strength so the active/interactive state is still obviously distinct. Disabled state keeps the same flattened-colour mechanism from the previous task (border-subtle/bg-transparent/text-muted/opacity-30, so a row at either end still reads as clearly inert rather than a faded active control) with only the disabled border itself softened slightly further (`/70` opacity) -- deliberately not reverting `opacity-30` back up, since that was the previous task's explicit legibility fix and this task's "soften" request was read as "reduce overall visual weight," not "undo the inert-state clarity fix." Placeholders ("Enter stage" / "Add notes") verified unchanged and still using `.ftc-textarea::placeholder`'s `--ftc-color-text-muted`, distinctly lighter than entered text -- no code change needed there.

New regression `testRunSheetSaveButtonPolish` covers all of the above against source (copy, always-mounted structure, the CSS transition rules including the no-scale/no-bounce check, aria-hidden/tabIndex wiring, Cancel's independent row); `testRunSheetEditMode`'s stale `"Save run sheet"` assertion updated to `"Save"`, and `testRunSheetHeaderCancelAndSave`'s now-superseded mount/unmount and `@keyframes` assertions removed (that test's remaining assertions -- Cancel's `.ftc-form-cancel-link` styling, `hasUnsavedRunSheetEdits` wiring, placeholders, reorder disabled-state colours -- are all still accurate and untouched). Live-verified at 1280px and 390px against a real QA event (`FTC QA Planner` / Test 3): typed into Stage / Area, watched Save fade in with the upward nudge and appear left of Cancel; cleared the field and confirmed Save faded back out with zero layout shift in the row below; hit Cancel and confirmed the edit reverted with nothing persisted. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run test:regressions`, and `npm run build` all clean.

**Git-history note (not a code issue, but worth knowing):** a concurrent session's Chat Composer/attachments work (`Crew Chat polish part 2`, commit `ee8c763`) was committed mid-way through this task, and its author swept up this task's already-edited-but-not-yet-committed hunks in the two files both sessions touched (`app/globals.css`, `scripts/test-regressions.mts`) into that commit rather than leaving them for this one -- discovered only after the fact, once `ee8c763`/`cb388a1` were already pushed to `origin/main`, which rules out any history rewrite. The CSS and regression-test changes described above are therefore already live on `main` inside `ee8c763`, not in the commit this task makes; only the `EventRunSheetSection.tsx` component change is this commit's own diff. Nothing is missing or broken -- `tsc`/build/regressions all pass against the current, fully-merged state -- this note exists purely so a future reader isn't confused about why this commit's diff is narrower than the summary above.

**Run Sheet: Cancel moved into the header, Save gated on dirty state (2026-08-02):** editing now matches the Edit Event screen's own pattern (`PlannerFormCard`) instead of a large equal-weight Cancel/Save button pair. **Cancel** is now `.ftc-form-cancel-link` -- the exact CSS rule `PlannerFormCard` itself uses, reused rather than reimplemented -- sitting in a stable top-right slot for the whole editing session; it never animates or disappears ("Cancel remains static"). **Save reintroduces dirty-state tracking**: `hasUnsavedRunSheetEdits` (never removed from `lib/eventRunSheet.ts`, just unwired from this component during the View/Edit/Save pass earlier the same day) now drives a new `hasUnsavedChanges` memo, and Save -- plus a small "Unsaved changes" label beside it, styled with the same tiny uppercase muted-label treatment already used throughout this file for field labels and set numbers -- mounts only while `isEditing && hasUnsavedChanges`. Reappearing plays a new one-shot CSS `@keyframes` (`ftc-run-sheet-save-reveal`, 200ms ease-out matching the app's dominant reveal timing -- `AnimatedExpandPanel`, `ProfilePhotoViewer`) rather than a `transition`: Save mounts/unmounts, so there is no persistent element to transition between two states of, which is exactly why an entrance-only `animation` is the correct tool and a plain instant unmount is the correct way to make it disappear ("Save disappears again immediately after a successful save" -- literal, no exit animation, because there is genuinely nothing left to animate away).

**Progress reversed to view-mode-only:** `showRunSheetProgress` was `rows.length > 0 && (isEditing || !allRowsIncomplete)` as of the previous pass (deliberately live during editing); this task explicitly asked for it to hide while editing instead, since it would otherwise sit directly above Save/"Unsaved changes" restating the same fact. Now `!isEditing && rows.length > 0 && !allRowsIncomplete`. Still shown in view mode exactly as before.

**Placeholders added** to the two capped textareas (Stage / Area: "Enter stage"; Notes: "Add notes") via a new optional `placeholder` prop on `RunSheetCappedTextarea` -- `.ftc-textarea::placeholder` already had its own distinct colour in `globals.css`, so no new styling was needed to keep placeholder text visually distinct from real content.

**Reorder-control disabled state made more obvious:** `iconButtonBaseClassName` (used only by `RowMoveButton`) went from `disabled:opacity-40` alone to `disabled:opacity-30` plus flattening the border/background/text to their subtle/transparent/muted variants -- a disabled arrow now reads as clearly inert rather than a slightly-faded copy of the active control. Size and position are unchanged; only colour/opacity respond to `disabled`.

New regression `testRunSheetHeaderCancelAndSave` covers all of the above directly against source; updated three stale assertions in `testRunSheetDirtyStateAndSaveFeedback` and `testRunSheetProductionPolish` that pinned the previous (now-reversed) `hasUnsavedRunSheetEdits`-unwired and live-during-editing-progress behaviour. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean, full `npm run test:regressions` passes.

**Committed via isolated patches, not whole-file `git add`:** a concurrent session was mid-way through a Chat Composer/attachments feature touching `app/globals.css` and `scripts/test-regressions.mts` at the same time (plus several files this task never touched). Rather than stash the whole tree (which would have briefly wiped their uncommitted work off disk) or blanket-`git add` those two shared files (which would have folded their unrelated, still-incomplete changes into this commit), each shared file's diff was split hunk-by-hunk, only this task's hunks were staged via `git apply --cached` against a hand-built patch, and the rest was left exactly as found, unstaged, in the working tree for them to commit themselves. Verified against the *exact* state about to be committed using `git stash push --keep-index` (stashes only what is not staged, leaving the index untouched) rather than a full stash, confirming `tsc`/build/regressions all pass on that precise commit before popping their work back -- `git status` before and after matched exactly, confirming nothing was lost. **Known, unrelated, pre-existing issue in the working tree at time of this commit (not caused by this task, not fixed by it):** `app/events/[eventId]/chat/page.tsx` currently fails `npm run build`'s type-check -- `<GroupChatComposer>` there is missing `pendingPhotos`/`onStagePhotos`/`onRemovePendingPhoto`/`uploading`, props that component's own in-progress, uncommitted edit already requires. That is the other session's incomplete work-in-progress and will presumably resolve once they finish updating that call site.

**Run Sheet empty state: no repeated heading, terser CTA (2026-08-02):** immediate follow-up to the onboarding-redesign entry directly below. That pass replaced the dashed placeholder with "Create your Run Sheet" / a longer sentence / "Create Run Sheet" -- Isaac's read: the section already has a "Run Sheet" heading immediately above, so "Create your Run Sheet" repeats it, and "Create Run Sheet" repeats "Run Sheet" a second time in the same screen. `RunSheetNotCompletedEmptyState`'s planner branch now drops its own heading entirely -- the section title is the only heading, the body is one sentence ("Add set times, stages and notes for your DJs."), and the CTA is just **"Create"** (the planner already knows which section they're in). The DJ branch already had no heading of its own to remove; only its vertical padding changed, for consistency with its sibling. Padding bumped `py-8` -> `py-10` on both branches to keep the block feeling intentional now that it carries one line of content instead of two -- the only spacing change made, and made because removing content without adjusting for it read as merely smaller, not deliberately calmer. Transition into/out of this state (§4 of the brief) needed no change: it was never gated on this component's own copy, only on `allRowsIncomplete`/`isEditing`, both untouched. Updated `testRunSheetProductionPolish`'s copy assertions accordingly, and added a per-branch check (not just "no heading anywhere in the function") that neither branch contains a `>Run Sheet<`/`>Create your Run Sheet<` heading, so a future edit reintroducing one in just the planner half can't slip past a whole-function-only assertion. `npm run build` clean (`tsc --noEmit` and the production build), full `npm run test:regressions` suite passes.

**Not verified visually -- browser preview tool still unresponsive**, same as the entry below and four prior task sessions before it (300s timeout on every `navigate`). Verification rests on a manual trace and diff review only. Isaac should confirm the empty state reads as calm rather than sparse now that it is a single sentence, and that "Create" alone (no "Run Sheet" suffix) still reads clearly as a call to action next to the section title.

**Run Sheet all-incomplete empty state redesigned as an onboarding invitation, not a placeholder (2026-08-02):** follow-up polish on the "Run Sheet not completed" empty state added in the entry directly below. Isaac's read: it looked unfinished -- dashed border, deficiency-flavoured copy ("not completed"), an "Edit" CTA for a Run Sheet that does not exist yet. Redesigned `RunSheetNotCompletedEmptyState`'s planner (`canEdit`) branch with no card, no border, no icon -- typography and spacing alone: a real heading (`text-lg font-bold`, up from a barely-differentiated `text-sm font-semibold`), copy that invites rather than flags a gap ("Create your Run Sheet" / "Add set times, stages and notes for each DJ so everyone knows where they need to be."), and a CTA that names the action that actually happens ("Create Run Sheet" -- there is no Run Sheet yet to "edit"). The DJ (`!canEdit`) branch keeps its existing copy untouched -- only its dashed wrapper is removed, for visual consistency with its sibling branch in the same component. Both branches gained `mx-auto max-w-sm` so the text reads as a comfortably narrow column rather than stretching edge-to-edge on desktop.

**Duplicate-action bug fixed as part of the same pass:** the top-right header Edit button was gated only on `canEdit && rows.length > 0`, which was already true whenever DJs existed on the sheet -- including while the brand-new "Create your Run Sheet" empty state was showing, since that state requires `rows.length > 0` (DJs exist, none have anything filled in). So the empty state's own CTA and the header's Edit button were both on screen at once, unnoticed until this pass. New `showRunSheetHeaderActions = canEdit && rows.length > 0 && (isEditing || !allRowsIncomplete)` suppresses the whole header cluster for exactly the window the empty state owns; it reappears automatically the instant editing starts or the instant any single row becomes complete -- the "natural progression from creation to editing" requested, achieved by extending an existing derived boolean rather than adding new state.

**Small Edit button de-iconed:** the top-right button's `✏️ Edit` (added in the entry below, the app's first user-visible emoji) is now plain `Edit` -- no icon, matching the rest of the app's text-only buttons. `aria-label="Edit Run Sheet"` is kept as-is even though the visible label shortened, since it remains the more descriptive announcement.

Explicitly preserved, none of it touched: `saveEventRunSheet`, the accordion's single-open-row behaviour, the edit workflow (Cancel/Save, force-expand-while-editing), DJ visibility, and `canEdit` itself (the actual permission flag) -- the only new logic is the one presentation-level boolean above.

Updated `testRunSheetProductionPolish`'s empty-state assertions for the new copy and the removed `ftc-card-empty`/icon, and `testRunSheetDirtyStateAndSaveFeedback`/`testRunSheetEditMode`'s header-cluster assertions for the renamed `showRunSheetHeaderActions` gate; added new assertions for the gate's formula and the de-iconed button. `npm run build` clean (`tsc --noEmit` and the production build), full `npm run test:regressions` suite passes.

**Not verified visually -- browser preview tool still unresponsive.** Same failure as the two entries below (300s timeout on every `navigate`, a fresh tab included); this is now the fifth consecutive task session it has failed in. Verification rests on the same basis as those: a complete manual trace of the final file and diff, TypeScript/build both clean, and a regression test pinning exact source structure. **Not independently confirmed:** how the un-carded, un-bordered empty state actually reads against the surrounding card at 375px and desktop -- whether it feels appropriately "premium/calm" or under-defined without any visual boundary -- and whether removing the pencil icon leaves the plain-text "Edit" button reading clearly enough as tappable next to the section title. Isaac should look at a freshly-created event (zero Run Sheet content), then create one entry and confirm the CTA disappears and the top-right Edit button appears in its place, before this is called done.

**Run Sheet production polish — empty states, completion signalling, hierarchy, numbering (2026-08-02):** builds on the View/Edit/Save entry directly below. Three things distinguished as different scenarios that used to render the same way:

- **`rows.length === 0`** (no DJs on the sheet at all) keeps its existing dashed empty state, untouched.
- **New: rows exist but nobody has anything filled in** (`allRowsIncomplete`, i.e. every row fails the new `isRunSheetRowComplete`) replaces the row list with a dedicated **`RunSheetNotCompletedEmptyState`**, but only in the read-only presentation (`!isEditing`) -- while editing, the planner needs the real rows on screen, not a static message standing in for them. Two copies inside it: a promoter sees "Run Sheet not completed" / "Add each DJ's set time, stage and notes before the event." with a primary "Edit Run Sheet" CTA wired to the same `handleEnterEditMode` the header button uses; a DJ (or anyone without edit permission, including a promoter viewing history -- `canEditRunSheet` is false there too, a known simplification, not a bug) sees "The promoter hasn't published the Run Sheet yet. Check back later." with no CTA and no repeated "Run Sheet" heading, since the section title above it already says that.
- **Partial completion** (some rows complete, some not) shows the normal list, with each incomplete row individually muted (`opacity-75`, computed per-row as `!canEdit && !isRunSheetRowComplete(row)` -- never while that row is actually being edited) and its collapsed preview falling back to "Run Sheet details pending" only when there is genuinely nothing else to show (a row with a stage but no time still shows its real stage text).

**Completeness** (`isRunSheetRowComplete`) is deliberately "has the essentials" -- a non-empty Stage / Area AND at least one of start/finish time -- not "has everything." Notes stay optional supplementary detail and never gate it either way; a partial Set Time (only a start or only a finish) still counts, matching what the field already treats as "has a value" versus the empty placeholder.

**Progress line** beneath the title (`${completed} of ${total} DJs completed`) is derived live from `rows`, not `savedRows`, so it updates as the planner types during editing, not only after a save. It flips to **"🟢 Run Sheet Complete"** once every row is complete, and is hidden entirely while the all-incomplete empty state above is showing (so the same fact isn't stated twice). Kept deliberately small and muted (`text-xs text-ftc-text-muted`) -- no banner, no colour beyond the one emoji.

**Running order**: every row now shows `#1` / `#2` / `#3` inline with its identity row, via one new `formatRunSheetOrderLabel(index)` -- factored out to a single call site so a future named-sets feature (Opening / Main Set / Closing) only has to change that one function, not `RunSheetEntry` or its caller. Distinct from the existing "SET 1"/"SET 2" label (same DJ holding multiple entries) -- one is running order, the other is which of *that DJ's* sets this is.
