**Reading hierarchy inside expanded cards:** DJ name is now explicit `text-base font-bold` (was an unsized `font-medium`, inheriting whatever happened to be ambient); Set Time (read-only) is sized up from `text-sm` to `text-base font-semibold`, a tier below the name; Stage / Area and Notes are **untouched** -- both still route through the same shared `BookingCardExpandableNotes` at `text-sm text-ftc-text-secondary`, which already reads as "supporting information" below Set Time by weight and colour, with document order (Stage before Notes) carrying the "then... finally" distinction between those two rather than a fourth type-size tier. `BookingCardExpandableNotes` itself was not touched, so its other callers (DM booking notes, the rate proposal panel) are unaffected.

**Edit button moved onto the standard secondary button** (`EVENT_DETAIL_BTN_SECONDARY`, the same style Cancel and Message already use), replacing the `EventDetailEditButton` icon+pill it reused in the previous entry below -- that pill's `bg-ftc-bg/80` against the app's near-black base is what read as "a separate black button" rather than something integrated into the design system. **The `ariaLabel` generalisation added to `EventDetailEditButton` in the previous pass is reverted** (nothing needs it now that the Run Sheet doesn't use that component at all); the event-level "Edit event" button is back to its original single-purpose form, net zero diff on that file across both passes.

New regression `testRunSheetProductionPolish` covers all of the above directly against source (the empty-state copy and CTA wiring for both branches, the completeness/progress/completion formulas, the order-label call site, the muting condition and its helper-text fallback, the hierarchy classes, and the reverted `EventDetailEditButton`). Updated three `testRunSheetAccordionRestructure`/`testRunSheetChromeReduction`/`testRunSheetEditMode` assertions that matched the old literal `{stagePreview}`/`"ftc-card p-3"`/`EventDetailEditButton` source strings, now template literals and a plain button. `npm run build` clean (`tsc --noEmit` and the production build both), full `npm run test:regressions` suite passes.

**Not verified visually -- browser preview tool still unresponsive.** Same tool failure as the entry below (300s timeout on every `navigate`, including a freshly-opened tab); this is now the fourth consecutive task session it has failed in. Verification rests on the same basis: a complete manual trace of the final file, TypeScript/build both clean, and a regression test that pins exact source structure rather than mere existence. **Not independently confirmed:** how the muted/incomplete card styling actually reads against the dark theme, whether the new order-label badge causes any wrap/crowding in the collapsed identity row at 375px (avatar + badge + name + move buttons, when editing), and the emoji rendering (🟢, ✏️) on a real device -- this is the app's first user-visible emoji use; nothing elsewhere in the codebase does this, so it's a deliberate first, not an oversight, but worth a glance. Isaac should look at all three states (empty/not-completed, partial, fully complete) on a real event before this is called done.

**Run Sheet moved from always-editable to explicit View → Edit → Save → View (2026-08-02):** the Run Sheet previously rendered every editable affordance (textarea borders, character counters, move-up/down, Set Time picker) on open whenever the planner had permission, gated only by a dirty-check that hid an always-mounted Save button. It now opens read-only by default -- a compact, scannable document -- and editing is the deliberate result of tapping a new **Edit** button in the header. **`EventRunSheetSection` gained one boolean, `isEditing` (default `false`).** Every editable branch inside `RunSheetEntry` already switched on a `canEdit` prop (built in the accordion-restructure and chrome-reduction passes earlier the same day) -- textarea vs `BookingCardExpandableNotes`, move buttons vs none, the Set Time wheel-picker trigger vs plain text. So the entire read-only/editable split needed no new branches: the parent now passes `canEdit={canEdit && isEditing}` to each row instead of `canEdit={canEdit}`, and every one of those existing branches follows automatically. `canEdit` (the prop, permission) is untouched everywhere else, including `syncAcceptedDjs`'s accepted-DJ auto-add -- that background reconciliation runs regardless of which UI mode is on screen. **Expansion:** browsing keeps the existing single-row accordion (`expandedRowId`); editing force-expands every row (`isExpanded={isEditing || expandedRowId === row.id}`), so no extra taps are needed to reach a field once Edit is tapped. **Header:** `canEdit && rows.length > 0` still gates the whole cluster (unchanged), but it now renders either the new Edit button or a Cancel + Save pair, rather than a permanently-mounted, dirty-gated Save button -- so the old `invisible`/`aria-hidden`/`tabIndex` slot-reservation mechanic and the `hasUnsavedChanges`/`showSaveButton` variables are gone from the component entirely. **`hasUnsavedRunSheetEdits` in `lib/eventRunSheet.ts` is untouched and still exported and tested** (`testRunSheetInitialLoadIsNotDirty` and the pure-logic half of `testRunSheetDirtyStateAndSaveFeedback`) -- it's a general-purpose comparison util that simply has no caller in this component any more, not dead code removed. **Cancel** (optional per the brief, added since it was clean to add) is local-state only -- `setRows(savedRows)` -- and never calls `saveEventRunSheet`, so it cannot touch the database. **Save**, unchanged (`saveEventRunSheet`, same request shape, same response handling, same `onSaved?.("Run sheet saved")` -> shared header notification), now additionally returns to view mode and collapses every row **only on success** -- deliberately left out of the `finally` block, so a failed save keeps the planner in edit mode with their unsaved rows and the error message intact, rather than silently reverting them to read-only. **Edit button reuses the existing `EventDetailEditButton`** (icon + "Edit" pill, already used for the event-level edit form) rather than a second implementation -- generalised with an optional `ariaLabel` prop (default `"Edit event"`, unchanged for its existing caller) so the Run Sheet's button announces "Edit run sheet" instead. New regression `testRunSheetEditMode` pins the state machine directly: `isEditing` default, the `canEdit && isEditing` / `isEditing || expandedRowId` wiring, that `handleEnterEditMode` does nothing but flip the flag, that `handleCancelEdit` never calls the save path, that the view-mode reset calls sit in `handleSave`'s `try` block and specifically not in `catch`/`finally`, the header's Edit-vs-Cancel+Save structure, and the `EventDetailEditButton` generalisation including its default-preserving existing caller. Updated `testRunSheetAccordionRestructure`'s `isExpanded` assertion and rewrote the now-obsolete button-visibility assertions in `testRunSheetDirtyStateAndSaveFeedback`. `npm run build` clean (including a plain `tsc --noEmit` pass), full `npm run test:regressions` suite passes.

**Not verified visually — browser preview tool unresponsive.** The in-app browser timed out (300s) on every `navigate` call across this entire task, including after opening a fresh tab; this is the third consecutive task session where it has done this. Verification here rests on: a complete manual trace confirming every `canEdit`-branched affordance in `RunSheetEntry` is correctly wired to the new prop chain, TypeScript and production build both clean, and a regression test that pins the exact source structure of every new function rather than just its existence. **What is NOT independently confirmed:** the actual on-screen appearance and feel of the Edit/Cancel/Save button cluster at 375px (iPhone SE), 390px and desktop, whether the header wraps awkwardly with two buttons instead of one at narrow widths, and the animated expand/collapse transition when every row opens at once on a large run sheet. Isaac should click through View → Edit → (edit a field) → Save, and separately View → Edit → Cancel, on a real event with several DJs before this is called done.

**Run Sheet read-only chrome reduction — subtitle gone, Stage / Area rejoins Notes' show-more/less, Set Time loses its box (2026-08-02):** second polish pass on the previous accordion restructure, same goal (compact, scannable at 20-50 DJs) from a different angle: make the read-only view read as event information, not an editable form. **"Read-only view for accepted crew" subtitle removed outright**, along with the `readOnlyHint` prop that controlled it (one caller, no purpose once the copy is gone). **Collapsed header preview is now Stage / Area alone** — Set Time moved to expanded-only, since a DJ scanning a large sheet wants their own name and where they're playing, not times compared across dozens of rows; this (not the earlier "Stage • Time" summary) is what actually shortened the collapsed row. **Stage / Area read-only now reuses `BookingCardExpandableNotes`** exactly like Notes — same show-more/less, same no-scrollbar guarantee, same hide-when-empty — replacing the pinned-height scroll box it composed via `RunSheetReadOnlyText` (deleted). `BookingCardExpandableNotes` gained a **`previewLines` prop** (`2 | 3`, default `3`) for this: both class names are literal strings in a lookup (`previewLines === 2 ? "line-clamp-2" : "line-clamp-3"`), not interpolated, since Tailwind's static scan can't see a runtime-built class name. Its three existing callers (DM booking notes, rate proposal panel) are unaffected by the default. **Set Time read-only dropped its bordered/background box** for plain typography — it's a single short fixed string, never needs show-more, just less chrome; `readOnlyTextClassName` (which supplied that box, and before this pass was also reused for Stage / Area and DJ identity) has no remaining consumer and is removed. Expanded-panel order unchanged: Stage / Area, Set Time, Notes. Verified in-browser at 375/1280 via a throwaway route mounting the real `RunSheetEntry` (temporarily exported, reverted before commit): no subtitle, no "DJ" label, "Set 1"/"Set 2" only for a genuine multi-set DJ, collapsed rows show avatar/name/Stage-Area-preview/chevron only, a 60-character unbroken token wraps with zero horizontal overflow, a fully-empty row expands to show only "Set Time: —" with Stage/Area and Notes both absent rather than empty-rendered, animations unaffected. New regression `testRunSheetChromeReduction` (mutation-tested against 4 independent mutations); updated `testRunSheetTextareasArePinnedToTheirRowCounts` and `testRunSheetAccordionRestructure` for the removed summary line and deleted `RunSheetReadOnlyText`. `npm run build` clean, `npm run test:regressions` pass. Commit `625bb02`.

**Run Sheet rebuilt as a single compact accordion, replacing the desktop table + mobile card duplication (2026-08-02):** `EventRunSheetSection.tsx` now renders one `RunSheetEntry` list at every viewport instead of an always-expanded `<table>` (desktop) and a separate always-expanded card block (mobile) showing identical data. Collapsed, each entry is two lines — avatar/name plus an always-visible `formatRunSheetSummaryLine` ("Main Stage • 9:00 PM – 10:00 PM") — with Stage / Area, Set Time and Notes behind a chevron, animated via a new shared **`app/components/AnimatedExpandPanel.tsx`** (extracted from `BookingRequestCard`'s own card-level expand, which now imports it too — its `DM_BOOKING_CARD_EXPAND_PANEL_ATTR` selector, used by the DM scroll-to-booking code, is preserved via a new `dataAttribute` prop). Exactly one `expandedRowId` per section, so opening an entry closes whatever was open. The standalone "DJ" label is gone (avatar+name already say what it is); "Set 1"/"Set 2" only renders when the *same* DJ genuinely holds more than one entry, computed by a new pure, independently-tested **`computeRunSheetSetLabels`** in `lib/eventRunSheet.ts` (grouped by resolved profile id, falling back to matched artist name — the same identity rule `mergeAcceptedDjsIntoRunSheetRows` already uses). Read-only Notes hides completely when empty and otherwise reuses **`BookingCardExpandableNotes`** (already behind DM booking notes and the rate proposal panel) instead of a second show-more/less implementation — editable Notes is untouched, still `RunSheetCappedTextarea` with its pinned-height internal scroll, since that scroll is load-bearing for enforcing the line cap while typing.

**Real defect found only by live measurement, not by any regex test:** the expanded panel's field list was originally a single-column CSS grid (`grid gap-3`). A grid item with no explicit width sizes to its content's min-content width, and `BookingCardExpandableNotes`'s `break-word` text doesn't reduce that contribution — so a Notes value with no natural wrap point (one long unbroken run, in practice a pasted URL) rendered its `<p>` **3086px wide inside a 317px panel**, silently clipped by the panel's own `overflow-x-hidden` rather than wrapping, and since a single unwrapped line never exceeds a 3-line clamp, the "Show more" control never appeared either — the rest of the text wasn't truncated with an escape hatch, it was simply gone. Fixed by switching to plain block flow (`space-y-3`): every child there is already a full-width block, so the grid bought nothing, and a block's width comes from its containing block rather than its content — which is also why `BookingRequestCard`'s own pre-existing use of the same `BookingCardExpandableNotes` component, inside a plain block wrapper, was never exposed to this. New regression test `testRunSheetAccordionRestructure` pins the `space-y-3` class specifically so this can't quietly regress back to `grid`; also added `testRunSheetSetLabelGrouping` (direct tests of the extracted grouping function) and updated `testWithdrawalOtherReasonInputLimits` for the `AnimatedExpandPanel` extraction. All mutation-tested.

Verified in-browser via a throwaway route mounting the real `RunSheetEntry` (temporarily exported, reverted before commit) at 375/390/1280: single DJ vs. same-DJ-two-sets with another DJ's entry interleaved between them (numbering unaffected), empty notes render nothing, long unbroken Stage/Area and Notes values both wrap with zero horizontal overflow, single-open-at-a-time measured directly via `AnimatedExpandPanel`'s rendered height (not inferred from a screenshot), chevron rotation, Show more/Show less round-trip. Editing (Stage/Area textarea, set-time wheel picker, Notes textarea + counter, row reordering) all unchanged. `npm run build` clean, `npm run test:regressions` pass. Commit `a27c36d`.

**Note for whoever picks this up next:** this session's dev server working tree also had three unrelated, uncommitted files in flight from a concurrent session — `app/components/profile/EditProfileForm.tsx`, `app/onboarding/page.tsx`, `lib/bookingRequests.ts` — a "Planner" → "Promoter" copy/label rename, mid-edit. They were deliberately left untouched and unstaged (this task's commit `a27c36d` stages only the five files it actually changed, by name, never `git add -A`). If they're still uncommitted when you read this, that's someone else's in-progress work, not a leftover from this task.

**Read-only Run Sheet fields capped to match editable state, long-word wrap fixed (2026-08-02):** the DJ/accepted-crew read-only view of Stage / Area could still overflow the card horizontally or grow past 2 rows — the prior `min-h-[2.25rem]`-only cap (added for Notes, never extended to Stage / Area) covered explicit line count but not visual wrapping: a single unbroken 40+ character token (a venue name with no spaces, a pasted URL) still wraps across multiple visual lines, and with no pinned height or wrap safety net either grew the cell or blew out the card. **Confirmed directly, not assumed:** the old markup, given a 307px-wide container, was pulled to 398px by one 49-character token. `RunSheetReadOnlyText` now takes a `rows` prop (2 or 4) instead of a `notes` boolean and composes `ftc-run-sheet-textarea ftc-run-sheet-textarea-{rows}` for **both** fields — the same classes the editable textarea uses, so read-only and editable are the same field in two states. `.ftc-run-sheet-textarea` also gained `min-width: 0; max-width: 100%; overflow-wrap: anywhere; word-break: break-word;` — the same four-property combination already used by `.ftc-event-detail-notes-text` and its siblings for this exact problem elsewhere in the app. **`overflow-wrap: anywhere` is load-bearing, not `break-word` alone:** `break-word` doesn't reduce an element's min-content contribution, so a flex/grid/table-cell ancestor sized by content can still be forced wider by an unbroken token even though the text would wrap once space ran out — `anywhere` does reduce it, which is what stops the ancestor (the mobile card's grid item, the desktop `table-fixed` column) from being pulled wide in the first place. Verified in-browser at 375/390/1280, editable and read-only, card grid and a real `table-fixed` cell: 49- and 400-character unbroken runs both wrap inside 62px/110px boxes with zero horizontal overflow anywhere. New regression `testRunSheetFieldsWrapLongUnbrokenTokens`; both new/extended assertions mutation-tested. Commit `6d3fb70`. Untouched: editing, saving, the line/character caps below, permissions, ordering, database, mobile layout elsewhere.

**Run Sheet hard line limits — Stage / Area 2 lines, Notes 4 lines (2026-08-02):** behavioural cap to match the row caps below. `RunSheetCappedTextarea` now reuses **`applyCappedMultilineInputLimit` / `shouldBlockMultilineEnter` from `lib/cappedMultilineInput.ts`** — the existing FTC standard already behind the booking rate note (`ProposeBookingRateSheet`) and the profile bio (`EditProfileForm`), also used via `lib/user/profileFormUtils.ts`. **Do not write a new line-limit helper; this module is the one.** `rows` doubles as the line cap rather than a second constant, so the displayed row count and the cap cannot drift. Both entry routes are covered because both exist: `onKeyDown` preventDefaults Return at the cap (behind the standard `isComposing` IME guard), `onChange` truncates anything that replaces the value wholesale — paste, drag-and-drop, dictation, autofill. **Inherited legacy semantics worth knowing when testing:** a value already over a cap is never silently rewritten — it can be *reduced* but any edit that lengthens it is rejected outright (`null`). Run Sheet notes never had a line cap before, so 5+ line notes exist in real data and will refuse added characters until a line is removed. Verified in-browser at 375, 390 and 1280 (identical): paste 5 → first 2, paste 10 → first 4, Return blocked at cap and explicitly *not* one line below, 50/500 character caps still truncate, heights unchanged at 62px/110px with `overflow-y: auto`, and a 449-character single line still scrolls internally (scrollHeight 324 in a 108px box) so wrapping is unaffected. New regression `testRunSheetFieldsEnforceHardLineLimits`; both guards mutation-tested. Commit `f4ba26d`. **Note:** `applyEventNotesInputLimit` in `lib/events/eventNotes.ts` is still a third hand-rolled copy of this same logic (its own header says it mirrors it) and additionally uses raw `.length` rather than `countUnicodeCharacters`, so it miscounts emoji — folding it into `applyCappedMultilineInputLimit` is a clean follow-up, deliberately left out of scope here.

**Run Sheet Notes 4-row cap — root cause found in the READ-ONLY cell, fixed, diagnostics removed (2026-08-02):** supersedes the diagnostics-panel entry below; that panel is deleted and no reading was needed. Isaac reported Stage / Area correctly capped at 2 rows but Notes at ~6 on a physical iPhone SE. **Both fields are the same component, same base class, same page, differing only by a row-count modifier — so a device honouring `.ftc-run-sheet-textarea-2` cannot be failing to honour `-4`.** That asymmetry was the tell: the element growing was never the textarea. **Root cause: `RunSheetReadOnlyText` in `app/components/EventRunSheetSection.tsx`.** It renders the cell whenever `canEdit` is false — `canEditRunSheet = isOwner && isPlanner && !isHistoryEventDetail`, so **accepted crew, or the planner on a history event**. Notes there carried `min-h-[3.25rem]` and nothing else: no `max-height`, no overflow, so the div grew to fit. Measured at 375×667: **173.25px, `overflow-y: visible`, scrollHeight == clientHeight, 6.99 visible rows**. Now composes the same pinned pair as the editable field (`ftc-run-sheet-textarea ftc-run-sheet-textarea-4`): **110px, `overflow-y: auto`, scrollHeight 180 vs clientHeight 108, exactly 4.00 rows**, row 5+ scrolling internally. The two classes are applied **as a pair on purpose** — the base pins `line-height: 1.5rem !important`, overriding the `leading-relaxed` in the shared read-only class string; without it the row advance is 22.75px, 4 rows measure 105px and the 110px box shows a sliver of row 5. **Why five earlier attempts all failed:** every one tuned the *editable* textarea (rows attribute, line-height, max-height, the auto-grow effect, then pinning height/min/max). Those caps are real and correct — they just never applied to the path being looked at. **Why it looked fine elsewhere:** Stage / Area is uncapped in this path too but cannot reach 2 rows at 50 characters; and at 1280px the wider cell fits the same note in 2 lines (59.5px), so the defect only ever showed on a phone. Stage / Area deliberately left as-is (smallest fix) — **known residual:** a legacy `stage_area` value longer than 50 characters would still grow the read-only cell, since `applyTextInputLimit` permits over-limit stored content. Regression `testRunSheetTextareasArePinnedToTheirRowCounts` now asserts the read-only cell carries the 4-row pair and no longer carries its own min-height (mutation-tested). Verified at 375, 390 and 1280; build clean, regressions pass. Commit `c3663db`. **Still to confirm on the physical iPhone SE** — and note the read-only Notes box is now a fixed 4 rows even for a one-line note, which is consistent with the editable state but taller than the old content-sized box.

**TEMPORARY Run Sheet textarea diagnostics panel — REMOVED, superseded by the entry above (2026-08-02):** the Run Sheet row caps (Stage / Area 2 rows, Notes 4 rows) still fail in Production on **both** an iPhone SE and a modern iPhone, while measuring exactly 2.00 and 4.00 in Chromium. Everything reachable from source has been checked and cleared: the pinned rules are in the production CSS at the expected offsets, **unlayered** and `!important`; nothing later, no media-query rule and no shared textarea minimum overrides them (`min-height: 2.75rem` applies to `.ftc-input`/`.ftc-field-trigger`, not `.ftc-textarea`); `field-sizing` and `-webkit-fill-available` have zero occurrences; the arithmetic is right (2×24+14=62, 4×24+14=110); and the desktop-table and mobile-card render paths emit identical class strings. **Chromium cannot settle this — only real WebKit values can**, and Isaac is not using Safari Web Inspector over a cable. So `app/components/debug/RunSheetTextareaDebugPanel.tsx` (commits `f358bf2`, `0e513f7`) reads those values on the phone: one **Capture diagnostics** button measures and copies in a single gesture, reporting per field the className, rows, height/min/max, clientHeight, scrollHeight, line-height, font-size, padding, borders, box-sizing, overflow-y and a derived visible-row count against target, plus route, build id, viewport size/scale, DPR, root font-size and user agent. It also reports whether `.ftc-run-sheet-textarea`, `-2` and `-4` exist in `document.styleSheets` at all — the one line that separates "the CSS never reached the device" from "the CSS reached it and lost the cascade", which static analysis cannot resolve. **Gated entirely behind `?ftcdebug=runsheet-textareas`** — same opt-in pattern as the `?ftcdebug=realtime` panel that was added and fully removed earlier the same day. Without the flag the component returns `null` and the page has no panel and no buttons; it only ever reads the DOM and never writes to the run sheet fields. Measure-and-format is synchronous before the clipboard call because Safari only honours a write inside the gesture's own task. Verified in-browser at 375px and 1280px against a throwaway local route (deleted, never committed): the confirmation shows and auto-clears, a spy on `writeText` confirms the full payload reaches the clipboard, a forced rejection reveals the selectable fallback box, and the unflagged page is inert. `npm run build` clean, `npm run test:regressions` pass. **Next: Isaac captures a reading on the physical normal-sized iPhone with both fields overflowing. Then this panel and the flag must be deleted in full, the proven fix implemented alone, and build + regressions re-run.** The row-cap bug is **not fixed** — do not describe it as fixed until Isaac confirms 2 rows and 4 rows on the physical device.

**Planner DM/unread/notification stayed stale after a DJ accepted — root cause found and fixed (2026-08-02):** the final piece of the booking-acceptance realtime failure. The Planner Events half was already fixed (shared `notifyBookingRequestsChanged` invalidator + `subscribeToBookingRequestChanges` on both `recipient_id` and `sender_id`, since realtime filters match a single column); what remained was that after a DJ accepted, the planner's DM preview never changed, no unread highlight appeared, no Messages badge appeared and no notification arrived. **Root cause: `insertBookingAcceptedDmMessageIfNeeded` in `lib/bookingRequests.ts` deduped per *conversation*, not per *booking*.** It inserted the bare `DM_BOOKING_CONFIRMED_MESSAGE` ("Booking confirmed") — a constant identical for every booking — and skipped the insert whenever any row with that text already existed in the conversation. So exactly one acceptance message could ever exist per planner↔DJ DM: the first acceptance inserted, every later acceptance inserted **nothing**. With no `messages` row there was no realtime event, hence no preview change, no unread state and no badge — and because the notification call was gated behind `if (dmResult.inserted && …)`, the planner's notification was swallowed too. All four reported symptoms have this one cause. **Fix:** acceptance now inserts a per-event message via new `formatBookingConfirmedDmMessage(eventName)` in `lib/dm/dmBookingSystemMessages.ts` ("Booking confirmed · <event>"), and the duplicate query matches that per-event text — so dedupe identifies the booking, and a repeat acceptance of the *same* booking is still suppressed. The per-event form is recognised by `isCanonicalDmBookingSystemMessage` and still **displays** as the concise canonical "Booking confirmed" (the event name exists on the stored row purely to make each acceptance distinct — no visible copy change). The planner's notification is now a `booking_update` "Booking accepted" mirroring the existing decline path, and is no longer gated on the DM insert. No polling, no delays, no `router.refresh`, and the working Planner Events reconciliation is untouched. **All temporary investigation instrumentation removed in this same commit:** deleted `lib/diagnostics/realtimeDiagnostics.ts` and `app/components/debug/RealtimeDebugPanel.tsx`, removed both panel mounts from `OnboardingGuard`, and stripped every `rtLog`/`rtLogChannelStatus`/`rtLogBookingPayload` call site along with the `?ftcdebug=realtime` flag — a new regression test asserts none of it can return. New tests: `testBookingAcceptedDmMessageIsScopedToTheBooking`, `testRealtimeInstrumentationIsFullyRemoved`. `npm run build` clean, `npm run test:regressions` all pass. **Live multi-account verification still outstanding** — Isaac needs to confirm on two physical devices that a second acceptance in an existing DM now updates the planner's preview, unread highlight, Messages badge and notification. Note `notifications` is **not** in the `supabase_realtime` publication, so the Messages/notification badge refreshes via the existing `ftc-notifications-updated` fan-out rather than a realtime event on that table; if live testing shows the badge lagging, adding that table to the publication is the next step.

**iOS overscroll clipped the fixed bottom nav on Home-button iPhones (2026-08-02):** reported on iPhone SE — rubber-banding past the top or bottom briefly showed the fixed header/bottom nav as clipped. **Cause:** the bottom nav is `position: fixed`, so it is anchored to the *layout* viewport; an iOS bounce moves the *visual* viewport past the layout viewport and the bar stops tracking the physical screen edge. Home-button devices are worst hit because `safe-area-inset-bottom` is 0 there, so the bar already sits flush against the edge with no inset to absorb the movement. Compounding it, `.ftc-nav-bar` painted at `rgba(10,14,20,0.96)` with `backdrop-filter: blur(16px)` — a translucent bar with a backdrop filter must resample the content behind it every frame of the bounce, and on an SE-class GPU that resample visibly lags the gesture. **Fix (both in `app/globals.css`):** `overscroll-behavior-y: none` on `html` suppresses the document-level bounce (overscroll past the ends only — normal scrolling untouched, `overscroll-behavior` does not inherit, inner scrollers keep native scrolling, so no second scroll container); and a new `.ftc-mobile-nav-bar` rule paints the fixed mobile bar solid with `backdrop-filter: none`. The desktop bar is `sticky`, never rubber-bands, and keeps its blur. **Layout and spacing unchanged on every device** — bar still measures 45px and still carries `pb-[env(safe-area-inset-bottom)]`. Verified in-browser at 375×667 (SE), 390 and 1280: computed `overscroll-behavior-y: none` on html only, mobile bar `rgb(10,14,20)` / no backdrop, desktop bar still `rgba(...,0.96)` + `blur(16px)`, document and inner scrollers both still scroll, no console errors. New regression `testIosOverscrollDoesNotClipFixedChrome`. `npm run build` clean, `npm run test:regressions` pass. Commit `4948a39`.

**Not verified on real WebKit — and one defect deliberately left unfixed:** this machine has Command Line Tools only (no Xcode, so no iOS Simulator) and the in-app browser is Chromium, so the actual iOS rubber-band could not be reproduced. The computed-style and scroll checks above are solid; **the bounce behaviour itself still needs confirming on a physical iPhone SE.** Note `overscroll-behavior` requires **Safari 16 / iOS 16+** — an iPhone SE 1st gen caps at iOS 15 and will still bounce, though the solid bar should make it far less obvious there. **Separately found and NOT fixed (out of scope for this task):** the mobile nav measures **45px** (44px `min-h-11` content + 1px border) but the app reserves **56px** (`3.5rem`) for it in `.ftc-mobile-nav-offset` (`lib/design/plannerWorkspaceTokens.ts`) and in `EventDetailBottomBar`'s `bottom-[calc(3.5rem+env(safe-area-inset-bottom))]` — an 11px over-reservation leaving a dead strip above the bar. It cannot clip the bar (it is a gap, not a crop), so it is not the reported bug, but correcting it would shift bottom spacing app-wide on mobile and should be its own task.

**iOS fixed-chrome clipping on iPhone SE — root cause identified, fix declined as too costly (2026-08-02):** follow-up to the overscroll commit `4948a39`, which improved the symptom but did not remove it. Isaac confirmed on a physical iPhone SE that the header and bottom nav still "clip/slide under Safari's browser chrome" when scrolling to the very top or bottom. **That phrasing is the tell: this is not rubber-band overscroll at all, it is Safari's dynamic toolbar.**

**Root cause.** The app scrolls the *document* (`document.scrollingElement === document.documentElement`, verified in-browser). On iOS Safari, document scrolling is exactly what drives the collapsing/expanding URL bar and bottom toolbar. As those toolbars animate, Safari resizes the **layout viewport** — but `position: fixed` elements are laid out against that layout viewport (the ICB) and are only re-resolved *after* the toolbar settles, not synchronously with the animation. So mid-transition the fixed bottom nav is still painted at the old layout-viewport bottom, which is now underneath the expanding toolbar. It peaks at the very top and very bottom of a page because that is precisely when Safari restores its chrome to full size. Home-button devices are hit hardest because `safe-area-inset-bottom` is 0, so the bar sits flush to the edge with no inset to absorb the shift.

**This is already documented inside this codebase.** `lib/navigation/prepareFixedChatPageMount.ts` carries the comment *"Event Details uses document scroll; if scrollY persists on return, iOS Safari mispositions `position: fixed` chat chrome above the bottom nav"* — a previous author hit the identical behaviour. Chat pages are immune today precisely because they opt out of document scroll: `lockFixedChatDocumentScroll()` sets `html`/`body` `overflow: hidden`, and `FIXED_CHAT_PAGE_SHELL_CLASS` is a `fixed … h-[100dvh] … overflow-hidden` shell. Every other surface (Events, Gigs, Bookings, Profile, Discover) still document-scrolls and is therefore still exposed.

**Why the previous `overscroll-behavior-y: none` fix could not solve it.** It suppresses rubber-band overscroll *past the ends of the document* — a different mechanism. Safari's toolbar collapse/expand is driven by scroll position and direction, not by overscroll, so removing the bounce cannot stop the toolbar animation or the layout-viewport resize behind it. (Secondary point: `overscroll-behavior` needs Safari 16 / iOS 16+, so on an iPhone SE 1st gen it is ignored outright.) Commit `4948a39` is still a real improvement — it removes bounce-exposure and the per-frame backdrop-filter resample — and should be kept; it just addresses a different mechanism than the one causing the remaining symptom.

**The only complete fix, and why it was declined.** Stop the document scrolling app-wide and move scrolling into an app-shell container — the pattern chat already proves works. If the root scroller cannot scroll, Safari never animates its toolbars, the layout viewport never resizes, and fixed chrome never shifts. **Cost:** Safari's toolbars then stay permanently visible, costing roughly 100px of viewport on an already-small iPhone SE. It also requires real refactoring: `lib/ui/useBodyScrollLock.ts` and `app/(planner-workspace)/calendar/page.tsx` both save/restore via `window.scrollY`, which stops changing once the document is not the scroller; and `body` is `display: flex; flex-direction: column`, so shell children would need `flex-shrink` handling (verified in-browser: a 2500px child shrank to fit and produced no overflow at all, so `body` cannot simply be given `overflow-y: auto`). Isaac reviewed this trade-off on 2026-08-02 and chose to **keep the auto-hiding toolbars and accept the residual clipping** rather than pay ~100px of permanent viewport plus an app-wide scroll refactor.

**Residual known limitation (accepted, not a bug to re-report):** on document-scrolling pages in iOS Safari, fixed/sticky chrome will visibly shift during the toolbar transition at the extreme top and bottom of a page. There is no pure-CSS workaround — `dvh`/`svh`/`lvh` units, safe-area insets and `overscroll-behavior` all describe viewport *size*, none of them change *when Safari re-resolves fixed positioning*. The only lever is not scrolling the document. Revisit only if the ~100px cost becomes acceptable, or if the app moves to a standalone PWA / native shell, where Safari's toolbars do not exist and the problem disappears on its own.

**Signup decorative stacked-card graphic removed (2026-08-02):** the desktop Create Account page rendered `FtcBrandMotionLazy variant="ambient"` (the three-tile "Main room / Late set / Floor" stack) in an absolutely-positioned block pinned to the right of the form. Removed that block and its now-unused import from `app/signup/page.tsx`. Because the graphic was `position: absolute` it never reserved a column, so nothing had to be re-centred — the card is still centred by the container's existing `justify-content: center` (measured 409px on both sides at 1280px). Form markup, card padding, auth logic, container classes and the dark background are untouched. `overflow-hidden` and the card's `z-10` are left in place: both are vestigial now but removing them would alter clipping/stacking for no visual gain. Note the graphic also rendered on mobile (top-centred, behind the card) and is gone there too — that follows from "remove completely"; no mobile layout shifted since it was out of flow. `FtcBrandMotionLazy` itself is retained — `app/page.tsx` still uses the `hero` variant. Verified at 390px and 1280px, build clean, regressions pass.


## Phone / desktop parity (permanent — 2026-07-19)

**Rule:** Every FTC change must work at **~390px (phone)** and **~1280px (desktop)** with the same features, permissions, status logic, navigation outcomes, and loading/empty/error meaning. Responsive layout may differ; behaviour must not. Authoritative spec: **`FTC_WORKFLOW.md` §7**. QA templates updated: `docs/qa/README.md`, `REGRESSION-CHECKLIST.md`, `BUG-TEMPLATE.md`.

**Recent surfaces audited (2026-07-19, commit `6b98447`):** Events Active/History + batched lineup stats; event-detail guard; Gigs loading skeletons; event-detail → DM → Back; workspace tabs; profile identity hierarchy; owner vs public profile actions — all use shared logic/components; no confirmed phone/desktop behavioural mismatch found. Intentional layout differences only (main nav placement, Calendar mobile strip vs desktop grid, workspace sub-nav scroll/wrap).

**Beta readiness QA reset (2026-07-28):** reusable CLI `npm run qa:reset` + hardened `scripts/resetQaEnvironment.sql` removes **QA account data only** (non-QA users untouched). Runbook: `docs/qa/FTC-BETA-ENVIRONMENT-RESET.md`. Review commit hardens message_reads scope and verification queries.

**QA reset "chats/bookings still present" investigated — no defect found (2026-08-01):** reported symptom: after running the reset, QA DMs and booking requests were still visible. Traced the full deletion path end-to-end against live production data (not assumptions) using the app's own authenticated QA session against the Supabase REST API: queried `public.users` (confirmed all 3 active QA accounts — DJ, Both, Planner — detect correctly via `display_name`), `conversation_members`/`conversations` (both remaining threads are 100% QA-only, no mixed members), and `booking_requests` (all 3 remaining rows are QA↔QA on both sender and recipient). Ruled out every candidate root cause the task asked to check: detection gaps (none — all 3 accounts match), mixed QA/non-QA scoping (not applicable — nothing is mixed), FK-constraint-triggered transaction rollback (audited every FK in the schema — all are `CASCADE`/`SET NULL`, none `RESTRICT`), text/uuid type mismatches (all relevant id columns are `text`, confirmed against `auth_user_id()` and every table's `create table`). Decisive evidence: the **global oldest row in the entire `messages` table** was `2026-07-28T10:50:32Z` and the oldest `booking_requests` row was `2026-07-28T11:29:28Z` — nothing older exists anywhere — meaning the previous reset run genuinely cleared everything before that instant, and every "surviving" row was QA test activity created *after* that run (through today, still growing live during the investigation: message count rose 227→271 between two queries seconds apart). The deletion logic was already correct; nothing needed fixing there. What was missing was a way to tell "stale leftover" apart from "fresh activity since last run" without re-investigating from scratch each time — added `public.qa_reset_log` (small permanent table, timestamps every run) plus two new verification blocks in `scripts/resetQaEnvironment.sql`: "QA data cleared this run" (row count + age range of what's about to be deleted, captured pre-delete) and "reset run history" (last 10 runs with the gap between each). Runbook updated with a new "I ran the reset but chats/bookings are still there" section explaining this. `npm run qa:reset:check` passes, `npm run build` clean, `npm run test:regressions` all pass. Commit `3d91a7f`. **Not yet re-run against production** — Isaac needs to paste the updated `scripts/resetQaEnvironment.sql` into the Supabase SQL Editor himself (no working service-role key or DB connection available in this environment to run it directly) and share the verification output to confirm the current QA-only conversations/bookings clear as expected.

**QA reset script simplified back to a single paste-and-run file (2026-08-01):** per explicit request, removed everything from the investigation pass above that wasn't needed for the reset itself — `public.qa_reset_log` (permanent logging table), the pre-delete snapshot temp table, the "reset run history" verification block, plus the pre-existing "mixed bookings remaining" and "expected accounts not yet signed up" informational queries — none were required for the delete/preserve behaviour, all now covered by runbook prose instead of live SQL output. **No destructive logic changed**: every `delete`/`insert`/`update` statement, the detection regex, the FK-safe delete order, and the storage cleanup are byte-for-byte identical to the hardened version — confirmed via diff review before commit. Verification section condensed from ~20 scattered rows across multiple ad-hoc sections to 5 short, purpose-labelled blocks: QA chats/messages remaining (0), QA bookings remaining (0), QA events/plans remaining (0), QA accounts still present (profiles preserved), non-QA data preserved. Updated `scripts/reset-qa-environment.mts`'s required-marker validator and `testQaEnvironmentResetScript` in `scripts/test-regressions.mts` to match the new structure (and to assert the log table/snapshot table stay gone, guarding against reintroduction). `npm run qa:reset:check` passes, `npm run build` clean, `npm run test:regressions` all pass. Commit `edf1b37`. Still not yet run against production — same blocker as above (no privileged DB access from this environment).

**Pause rule:** new Critical/High production defect pauses tester onboarding.

## Core product

- **Booking acceptance did not propagate to any other view/account — root-caused, fix shipped, NOT YET VERIFIED (2026-08-02, commit `be938e6`):** **Two independent defects, one of which is a database-side prerequisite Isaac must apply.** **(1) `booking_requests` is not in the `supabase_realtime` publication.** Grepped every SQL script: only `message_reactions` and `message_reads` are ever `alter publication supabase_realtime add table`-ed. Postgres emits no replication events for unpublished tables, so the app's *only* booking subscription (`dm-bookings:<conversationId>`, `app/dm/[conversationId]/page.tsx`) has never received a single event since it was written — it subscribes successfully and stays silent, which is exactly why it looked functional. Everything that appeared to update live was local optimistic state (`setBookings`/`setMessages`) in the tab that performed the write — hence planner and Both accounts never updating. Requires `scripts/setupBookingRequestsRealtime.sql`. **(2) The shared Gigs snapshot cache was never invalidated by a mutation.** `updateBookingRequestStatus` only fired the `ftc-notifications-updated` window event, which refreshes *mounted* views; the Gigs list is unmounted while the DJ is in the DM, so module-scoped `memorySnapshot` (gigsListSnapshotPrefetch) survived and the next mount re-read it via `loadGigsListSnapshot({force:false})` — the gig stayed under Incoming with stale counts until a full page load cleared the module. That is the precise mechanism behind "only a hard refresh fixes it", and it is pure client, fixed here. **Acceptance paths do NOT differ** — `BookingRequestCard` renders only in the DM, `updateBookingRequestStatus` has exactly one caller, and the Gigs list has no Accept action at all, so there is a single acceptance path (the DM). **Architecture:** new `lib/bookings/bookingRequestsSync.ts` — one reconciliation entry point `notifyBookingRequestsChanged()` that fans out to registered cache invalidators then re-fires the existing `ftc-notifications-updated` bus (reused, not replaced). Caches *register* invalidators rather than the notifier importing them, because `gigsListSnapshotPrefetch` imports `bookingRequests` and a direct import would close an initialisation cycle. Every status mutation now calls it — accept, decline, rate-proposal accept, and **cancel, which previously did not notify at all** (a related pre-existing gap). `subscribeToBookingRequestChanges` registers on **both** `recipient_id` and `sender_id` (Realtime filters match a single column; the planner is the sender, so a recipient-only filter is why the planner would still miss it even once published), mounted once in `NavBadgeProvider` (renders for every signed-in user on every page) rather than per screen. Handlers are payload-agnostic — invalidate and refetch — so the DB stays source of truth and correctness does not depend on which columns an UPDATE payload carries. No polling, forced reloads, `router.refresh` or timeouts. **SQL:** `replica identity full` is required alongside publication membership — `recipient_id`/`sender_id` are unchanged on a status UPDATE, so without full row images Realtime cannot match either filter and drops the event before any client sees it. **Regression test** `testBookingStatusChangesReconcileEverywhere`, mutation-tested two ways (removing the cache-invalidator registration; dropping the sender-side filter) — each correctly fails. `npm run build` clean, full suite passes. **⚠️ NOT VERIFIED END-TO-END AND NOT CLAIMED FIXED:** the SQL has not been run, and this environment has no Supabase credentials, so none of the 13 required multi-account/multi-device scenarios were executed. Until Isaac runs the SQL and confirms live, treat this as root-caused with the fix staged, not resolved.

- **Gigs pill indicator dot softened (2026-08-02, commit `5720264`):** visual polish only. **Identification first — there is no separate active-indicator element.** Inspected `PlannerWorkspaceSubNavLink`, `.ftc-filter-pill` / `.ftc-workspace-subnav-pill` in `globals.css` (no `::before`/`::after` anywhere on the pills) and confirmed the only dark round thing inside a selected Gigs pill is the **incoming-count badge** (`WorkspaceGigsPendingBadge`), which on the active blue pill renders `bg-ftc-bg/20 text-ftc-bg` — a dark circle carrying the number, reading as a dot at a glance. Verified visually in a harness before editing (at count 0 nothing renders, consistent with the report). **Change:** `WORKSPACE_GIGS_PENDING_BADGE_SLOT_CLASS` (`lib/design/workspaceSubNavBadge.ts`) `h-4 min-w-4` → `h-3 min-w-3` — 16px → 12px, exactly **25%**, mid-range of the requested 20–30%. Font (`text-[10px]` → `text-[9px]`) and horizontal padding (`px-1` → `px-[3px]`) scale **with** the box rather than staying put: left at 10px/4px a single digit would have measured ~13.6×12 and turned the circle into an oval, and the brief said keep the shape. **Measured before/after** in-browser at both widths — single digit 16×16 → 12.39×12.00 (circular within 0.4px); "12"/"99" 22.05×16 → 18.77×12; "99+" 28.41×16 → 24.56×12, **no clipping at any count**; vertical centring exact (equal gap above/below) at every count; **pill height 30.5px before and after**, since 12px sits far under the pill's 1.625rem min-height — so pill height/width/padding/typography are untouched as required. Centring remains purely flex (`items-center justify-center`), no absolute offsets, so it holds for any label length — checked live with "AUFTRITTE" and a single-character "G" label for future localisation. Identical at 390px and 1280px, no page overflow. **Scope:** that class is used *only* by this badge (grepped) — the Incoming/Confirmed/History pills and the bottom main-nav DJ count badge use different classes, so counts, navigation, the planner workspace and all other styling are untouched. Colour, rounding and the pill's `gap-1.5` unchanged; there was no animation on the badge to preserve. Regression assertions added to `testWorkspaceGigsSubNavBadge`-adjacent coverage: locks 12px sizing, the centring classes, `rounded-full`/`tabular-nums`, and asserts it can neither regrow to `h-4`/`text-[10px]` nor switch to hand-positioning (`absolute`/`translate-`/inset utilities). **Mutation-tested both ways** — regrowing the dot and replacing flex centring with an absolute offset each correctly fail. Debug harness removed before commit, confirmed absent from the build. `npm run build` clean, full suite passes.

- **DJ workspace now leads with Gigs: `Gigs | Calendar` (2026-08-02, commit `7659300`):** follow-on to `6299d9d`, which removed the DJ Events tab but left the DJ row reading `Calendar | Gigs` — so the first tab was Calendar while `getDefaultRouteForRole` was already landing DJs on `/bookings`. The tab order contradicted the landing page; this aligns them. **Implementation:** moved `gigs` ahead of `calendar` in `WORKSPACE_SUB_NAV_TABS` (`lib/plannerEventsNav.ts`) — the one canonical array `PlannerEventsSubNav` filters by role via `isWorkspaceSubNavTabVisible`. Order is kept **role-independent on purpose**: a role-aware order would let tabs reorder as the role resolves (null → dj), which is exactly the flicker `mergeWorkspaceNavRole` was built to prevent, so the fix is a filter over one fixed order rather than per-role arrays. **Roles:** DJ `Gigs | Calendar`; promoter unchanged `Events | Event Plans | Calendar` (no Gigs tab, so literally unaffected); **"both" changed as a side effect** — `Events | Event Plans | Gigs | Calendar` (was `…Calendar | Gigs`). Both-role accounts are planners, so Events stays first per the brief; only Gigs and Calendar swap. Flagged rather than special-cased, since special-casing would have meant role-dependent ordering. **De-duplication:** `getEventsAreaSubNavItems` previously rebuilt the same order by hand — a second source that would silently drift (the exact failure mode that shipped stale copy twice this session). It now filters `WORKSPACE_SUB_NAV_TABS`, so tab order has one definition; its intentionally stricter null-role behaviour (`/events,/calendar`, vs `isWorkspaceSubNavTabVisible` showing all tabs to avoid first-paint flicker) is preserved and asserted, and a new loop asserts the two can never disagree on ordering for any role. **Audit (requirement 5):** no logic anywhere assumes Calendar is first — remaining `/calendar` references are the tab href, path matching, and calendar-origin return links, all order-independent; the only index reads are in tests. Two stale comments calling the DJ workspace "Calendar + Gigs" (`plannerEventsNav.ts`, `currentUser.ts`) were corrected. **Verified live** at 390px and 1280px by mounting the real `PlannerEventsSubNav` under a mocked `GuardProfileProvider` at a real workspace path (`/calendar/nav-debug`, removed before commit and confirmed absent from the build) — DOM order read back per role, identical at both widths, no page horizontal overflow; "both" scrolls within its own container at 390px, the documented intentional responsive behaviour. Console errors during the harness were "Not authenticated"/anon-permission only (no Supabase session), unrelated. **Behaviour preserved** — Incoming/Confirmed/History, availability calendar, deep links, tab persistence, back navigation, counts, badges and realtime all run through unchanged code paths; only array order moved. Rebased onto `68d1774` (Gigs count decrease fix) and re-verified build + suite on the combined state. **Not verified against real authenticated accounts** — no QA credentials here; Isaac should confirm on the real DJ and Both accounts.

- **Gigs nav count now follows Incoming downwards (2026-08-02, commit `68d1774`):** closes the limitation flagged in the `6299d9d` entry below — the badge rose immediately and cleared to 0 correctly, but a decrease to a *non-zero* count was ignored (3 incoming, accept one, badge kept reading 3 until it hit 0). **Root cause was not the display latch.** `applyPersistedGigsPendingCount` (`lib/navigationBadgeCache.ts`) already calls `syncWorkspaceGigsSubNavDisplayLatch` with each new count, so the latch tracks reality; the initial diagnosis in the `6299d9d` entry named the latch, and a mutation test disproved it — reverting the resolver's latch handling did not fail the new test, which is how the real cause was found. It was the hook's high-water ratchet, `Math.max(stableCountRef.current, latchedCount, rawCount)` on every render, which could only go up until a hard zero reset it. **Fix:** a known local count is now used as-is. It is the freshest value there is — only `applyPersistedGigsPendingCount` writes it, and every badge refresh routes through that, including the one `notifyNavigationBadgesRefresh` fires from `lib/bookingRequests.ts` on accept/decline/cancel — so the hold now applies only when the count is genuinely unknown (first visit, cleared storage, mid-transition before seeding), which is the case it was written for (`070e397`/`7def1a6`) and where a transient provider zero would otherwise blink the badge off. The ratchet moved out of the hook into a pure exported `resolveStableGigsPendingCount` so the decision is directly testable; the hook keeps only the ref holding the previous render's value. **Requirements 1, 2 and 4 of the follow-up request were already shipped in `6299d9d`** (numeric badge, shared `gigsTabCountDisplay` formatter, `countDjGigsByTab(...).pending` source, absolute positioning + `tabular-nums`) and were re-verified rather than reimplemented. Regression test `testWorkspaceGigsCountFollowsIncomingDownwards` covers the decrease, the clear, the rise past 99, **and** the unknown-count hold, so the flicker protection cannot be lost while fixing the decrease; the pre-existing `testWorkspaceGigsSubNavCountSurvivesStaleRuntimeZero` still passes alongside it. **Mutation-tested** by restoring the high-water behaviour, which fails the named assertion. Verified live at 390px and 1280px for all three roles via a temporary debug route on the real `AppNavigation` (removed before commit): DJ badge tracked 0/3/2/99+/1/0 exactly with correct singular/plural aria labels, `150 → 2` now renders "2" instead of staying at "99+", updates arrived in the same DOM node without a remount, and tab widths stayed constant (mobile 129/129/129, desktop 50.08/88.37/63.97) with no horizontal overflow. Planner (`Events | Event Plans | Calendar`, no badge) and Both (`Events | Event Plans | Calendar | Gigs`, count on the sub-nav pill where its Gigs tab lives) unchanged. `npm run build` clean, full suite passes. **Still not verified on real authenticated accounts or on iPhone Safari** — no Supabase QA credentials and no iOS device in this environment.

- **DJ Events tab removed; DJ workspace is Calendar + Gigs, with a numeric incoming count on the main nav (2026-08-02, commit `6299d9d`):** DJ-facing navigation only — planner and "both" accounts are untouched and keep Events + Event Plans. **Unique DJ Events functionality WAS found, and was migrated before the tab was removed.** `cancel_event` (`scripts/updateEventCancellationCancelsBookings.sql`) only cancels booking_requests `where status = 'pending'`, so after a planner cancels an event the DJ's *accepted* booking keeps `status = 'accepted'`; the client cancel flow (`events/[eventId]/page.tsx`) only syncs planner caches, no DB write. Gigs reads `booking_requests` alone (`listReceivedBookingRequests` — no join to `events`), so a cancelled event's confirmed gig kept showing under **Confirmed** as though still going ahead, and Gigs History only picked it up once the date passed. DJ Events → History (`isEventCancelled`) was the only list in the DJ UI where that cancellation was visible; the DM activity message covers all bookings but a notification only goes to the pending ones. **Migration:** `gigsListSnapshotPrefetch.ts` now presents accepted bookings on cancelled events as cancelled, which routes them to History with the cancelled badge and drops them from the Confirmed count through the existing filters — **no extra network request**, because `loadGigsEventArtwork` (renamed `loadGigsEventLookup`) was already fetching those event rows for cover artwork via `getEventArtworkByIds` and throwing the `status` away. Pending bookings are deliberately left alone: the database already cancels those, and re-deriving them would change what Incoming means. **Everything else on the audit list was already covered by Gigs** — event-detail access (`buildGigsEventDetailHref`, with return-to-tab state), all four statuses (`filterDjGigsByTab`: pending→Incoming, accepted+future→Confirmed, declined/cancelled/accepted+past→History), rates, booking status, Open DM. Note the DJ Events tabs were `!isEventCancelled` / `isEventCancelled` with no date filter, so "Upcoming" had been listing past events too. **Navigation changes:** `canViewEventsSubNav(role) = role !== "dj"` gates the workspace sub-nav Events tab (null role still shows it, matching the existing no-flicker convention); `getDefaultRouteForRole` is role-aware again (`dj → /bookings`, else `/events`) — this deliberately re-splits what `9b4a13b` collapsed, because the premise changed: DJs no longer have an Events workspace to land on; `AppNavigation.getNavItems` takes the role and returns one workspace-selector item, labelled **Gigs → /bookings** for DJs and **Events → /events** otherwise, both keeping `isPlannerEventsAreaPath` as `isActive` so the tab highlights across the workspace and no-ops when tapped from inside it; `/events` redirects DJ-only accounts to their own workspace (mirrors the existing `booking-plans` guard) so a stale link or history entry can't strand them; `resolveEventDetailBackHref` gained `isDjWorkspace` so a notification/DM deep link with no origin params returns to Gigs, not Events; the "Back to Events" fallback labels in `profileNavigation.ts` and `profile/setup/page.tsx` say "Back to Gigs" for DJs. **Gigs count badge:** the *workspace sub-nav* pill already rendered a numeric count — what had no badge at all was the bottom-left main nav. The DJ workspace tab now carries one, using the shared `gigsTabCountDisplay` formatter (0 → no badge, 1–99 → exact number, >99 → `99+`, `formatGigsTabCountAriaCount` for the label, e.g. "more than 99 incoming gigs"). Source is the same stabilised count as the sub-nav pill and the Incoming tab — `countDjGigsByTab(...).pending` — via a new shared `useWorkspaceGigsPendingCount` hook extracted verbatim from `PlannerEventsSubNav`'s private `useStableWorkspaceGigsSubNavCount`, so the two badges cannot drift. Absolutely positioned with `tabular-nums`, so the count appearing/changing width/clearing never reflows the tab row. **Known limitation, inherited not introduced:** that shared count latches — it ratchets *up* immediately and clears to 0 correctly, but a decrease to a non-zero value (3 → 2 after accepting one of three) keeps showing the higher number until the count reaches 0. This is the existing anti-flicker latch (`readWorkspaceGigsSubNavDisplayLatch`, from `070e397`/`7def1a6`) that the sub-nav pill has always had; loosening it would risk reintroducing the badge flicker those commits fixed, so it was flagged rather than changed — **Isaac's call**. **Verified live at 390px and 1280px** for all three roles via a temporary debug route rendering the real `AppNavigation` under a mocked `GuardProfileProvider`/`NavBadgeProvider` (removed before commit): DJ → "Gigs"/`/bookings` + sub-nav `Calendar | Gigs`; promoter → "Events"/`/events` + `Events | Event Plans | Calendar`; both → "Events"/`/events` + `Events | Event Plans | Calendar | Gigs`. Badge across 0/1/2/99/150 gave none/1/2/99/99+ with correct singular/plural aria labels, updating **in the same DOM node without a remount** (proving the `useSyncExternalStore` subscription is live). Mobile tab widths 129/129/129 and desktop 50.08/88.37/63.97 identical at every count — zero layout jump; no horizontal overflow at either width. Console errors during the harness were "Not authenticated" only (no Supabase session), unrelated. Regression tests: `testRoleAwareWorkspaceNavigation` (replaces `testMainNavAlwaysTargetsEventsWorkspace`, whose entire premise — one Events destination for every role — this task inverts), plus new `testCancelledEventGigsAreSurfacedInGigs` (a real behavioural test of the transform through `countDjGigsByTab`/`filterDjGigsByTab`, not just source regex) and `testDjMainNavGigsCountBadge`. Both new behaviours **mutation-tested** — reverting the cancelled-event transform and un-gating the nav item each correctly fail the suite. `npm run build` clean, full suite passes. **Not verified against real authenticated accounts** — no Supabase QA credentials in this environment, so the three roles were exercised through mocked profiles; Isaac should confirm on the real DJ / planner / Both accounts, and on older and newer iPhone Safari, which this environment cannot emulate.

- **DJ invite rate-mode help: mode heading restored, this time above the body (2026-08-02, commit `127874a`):** the "?" explainer beside the **Fixed offer / Ask for rate** pills in the DJ-invite rows. **Root cause of the request:** `61c13f5` (entry below, shipped earlier the same day) deliberately *removed* the panel's mode name — it made `InlineOptionHelpPanel`'s `label` optional and stopped `EventDjSendOfferControls` passing it, reasoning that the pill directly above already shows the name. In practice the panel opens below both pills, so with the name gone there was nothing in it identifying which mode it described. Hence "restore the heading". **Not a straight revert:** the run-in form `61c13f5` removed (`**Fixed offer.** You set the amount…`) read as one undifferentiated sentence, which is the reason it was worth removing in the first place. The name now renders as a small heading on its own line above the body — the form that makes the mode identifiable without the copy running together. That is the third and, on the evidence of the two failure modes, correct arrangement. **Changes:** `InlineOptionHelpPanel` renders `label` as its own `<p className="font-semibold text-ftc-text">` above a body `<p>` (`mt-0.5` only when a label is present, so the label-less case keeps its exact previous spacing); `EventDjSendOfferControls` passes `label={activeHelp.label}` again. `label` stays **optional** — `61c13f5`'s API change is preserved, and the crew-chat caller (`CREW_CHAT_HELP` in `events/[eventId]/page.tsx`) is untouched. **Body copy is `61c13f5`'s, unchanged and byte-identical across both pickers** — this task independently arrived at the same two strings before the rebase revealed `61c13f5`, so nothing was re-litigated. Container border/background/padding/text size/line height unchanged; rate-mode logic, validation, `aria-pressed`/`aria-expanded`/`aria-label`, disabled states and toggle behaviour untouched. `formatDjSendOfferSummary` keeps its concise summary-card wording and the pills keep the mode names as their own labels — the heading is not duplicated. **Ripple, deliberate:** crew chat's help panel gets the same heading treatment (its `label` becomes a heading rather than a run-in lead); its copy is unchanged. Forking the primitive to spare it would mean two versions of one panel, which `FTC_WORKFLOW.md` §5 forbids. **Test:** folded into `61c13f5`'s `testBookingRateModeDescriptionsAreUnified` rather than added alongside it — a second test asserting the same copy strings would have been the exact duplication that test exists to prevent. Its two now-inverted assertions (panel omits the label; panel renders the run-in span) were replaced with heading-above-body assertions, and the canonical-string, no-trailing-stop, superseded-wording and crew-chat assertions are kept as-is. Verified live at 390px and 1280px via a temporary debug route rendering the real component enabled and disabled (removed before commit): both headings render above their body, panel chrome unchanged, `rateMode` unaffected by opening help, disabled instance still disabled, zero horizontal overflow. `npm run build` clean, full regression suite passes.

- **Send-booking-requests confirm UI consolidated; Event Plans no longer forks it (2026-08-02, commit `5c82979`):** the follow-up flagged in the `464cd37` entry below. Read both implementations in full and diffed them precisely before touching anything — they had drifted in more than copy. **What was actually duplicated:** `bookings/page.tsx` already shared `DjInviteSelectionRow` and `DJ_INVITE_LIST_MAX_HEIGHT_CLASS` (two earlier partial extractions) but still hand-rolled the Summary card, the send button, and the whole `useSendBookingRequestsDraft` surface (`searchQuery`/`selectedDjIds`/`djOffers` state, `filteredDjs`/`sendableSelectedDjIds`/`allSelectedAreDuplicates`/`sendOfferSummary`/`hasInvalidFixedOffers` memos, `toggleDjSelection`/`updateDjOffer`). **Drift found, and its cause:** `git log -L` on both Summary-card ranges shows the panel's was restyled by `46fa81b` ("Polish Invite DJs sheet selection, hierarchy, and confirm button") and the page's never was — hence page had `border border-ftc-border-subtle`, full-opacity `bg-ftc-bg-elevated`, `p-4`, a `text-xs` heading, `mt-3` spacings and no `leading-snug`, against the panel's borderless `bg-ftc-bg-elevated/70`, `p-3.5`, `text-[10px]` heading, `mt-2.5` and `leading-snug`. Unintentional drift, not intent. **Genuine behavioural divergence:** the panel's label state machine has four states and branches on `selectedCount === 0 → "No DJs selected"` *before* the duplicates branch; the page had three and showed a disabled `"Send Requests"` with nothing selected. The page's second branch keyed off `allSelectedAreDuplicates` rather than `sendableCount === 0`, but given the first-branch difference those are otherwise equivalent, so the zero-selected label was the only real gap. Disabled logic was already equivalent (the page has no `disabled` prop to honour). **Fix (Option A of three presented — Isaac chose it):** extracted `SendBookingRequestsSummary` (component) and `resolveSendButtonLabel` (helper, re-signatured to take plain counts instead of a `SendBookingRequestsDraft` so surfaces holding their own selection state can call it) from `SendBookingRequestsPanel.tsx`; both files now call them. Copy and label logic live in exactly one place. **Two further gaps found while comparing, same "fixed one, missed the other" class, both fixed:** the page's DJ list `<ul>` never got the `overscroll-contain [-webkit-overflow-scrolling:touch] touch-pan-y` containment the panel's list carries (the exact touch-scroll-trapping bug `DJ_INVITE_LIST_MAX_HEIGHT_CLASS`'s doc comment describes), and its `filteredDjs` memo was a byte-for-byte reimplementation of `filterBookableDjsBySearchQuery`. **Deliberately left alone as intentional differences:** the page's plain labelled search input (vs the panel's rounded pill `InviteDjSearchField`), its `"No DJs match your search"` empty state (vs `PlannerEmptyPanel`), its `"N DJs selected"` count line, and its `flex-1` button sitting in a row with Back — step-in-a-wizard vs panel-in-a-sheet framing; changing them is a redesign, not consolidation. **Test replaced, not extended:** `testEventPlansSendPanelCopyMatchesSendBookingRequestsPanel` grepped both files for matching literals, which only ever guarded against the second copy going stale and never against the duplication itself — replaced with `testEventPlansSendPanelReusesSharedConfirmUi`, which asserts the shared component/helper owns every string and that `bookings/page.tsx` imports and renders them while containing none of the literals itself (re-forking fails the test), plus the touch-scroll classes. Verified at 390px and 1280px via a temporary debug route rendering the real shared component and helper across all states (valid/invalid/empty summary; zero-selected/all-duplicates/ready/sending button): Summary card identical to the panel's, all four labels correct, Back/Send stacks on phone and sits inline on desktop, no horizontal overflow — route removed before commit. `npm run build` clean, full regression suite passes. **Not actioned (Option B, the remaining duplication):** `bookings/page.tsx` should eventually adopt `useSendBookingRequestsDraft` + render `SendBookingRequestsPanel` wholesale, deleting ~150 more lines — but that entangles with the deep-link intents, the page's own `eventBookings` fetch, the duplicate-clearing effect, `requestSendBookingRequests`/the unavailable-DJ confirm dialog, and would swap the search field and empty state. Real regression surface; presented and deferred per `FTC_WORKFLOW.md` §6.
- **Booking rate-mode descriptions simplified and unified (2026-08-02, commit `61c13f5`):** searched for every rate-mode description string first (per instruction) and found the copy lives in **two** separate production pickers that had already drifted: `BookingRateModeField.tsx` (the "Rate type" cards in the Booking Request modal) and `EventDjSendOfferControls.tsx` (the compact pills + "?" help panel in the Create Event and Event Plans DJ-invite rows). Both now carry byte-identical copy — Fixed offer: `"You set the amount. The DJ can accept or decline"`, Ask for rate: `"The DJ tells you what they'd charge. You can accept it or make your own offer"`. **Why the Ask-for-rate rewrite matters:** the old wording ("…keep your original offer") was factually wrong whenever the optional suggested-amount field was left empty — there is no original offer in that case; the new wording holds whether or not an amount is entered. Trailing full stops dropped per house style. **Label-repetition fix (and the collateral it avoided):** the help panel under the pills used to render `"Fixed offer. You set the exact amount…"` — repeating the option name the pill directly above already shows. `InlineOptionHelpPanel` is **shared**, though: `app/events/[eventId]/page.tsx` also renders it for the crew-chat explainer, where the bold `"Group chat."` lead is genuinely useful context. So rather than deleting the label span (which would have silently stripped crew chat too), `label` was made **optional** and the rate-mode call site simply omits it — crew chat is byte-unchanged. The `?` buttons keep their `aria-label`s ("Fixed offer help" / "Ask for rate help"), verified in the live accessibility tree. Out of scope and deliberately untouched: `lib/booking/proposeRateHelperPreference.ts` (DJ-side "propose your fee" helper — a different flow, not the planner-side rate-mode picker) and the DM booking system messages that legitimately reference an original offer after one exists. Regression test: `testBookingRateModeDescriptionsAreUnified` (replaces the narrower `testBookingRateModeFieldDescriptions`) — asserts one canonical string against **both** components, that superseded phrasings are gone from both, no trailing full stops, that the pill panel passes no `label`, and that crew chat still does. **Mutation-tested three ways** — drifting the pill copy from the card copy, reintroducing the label repetition, and stripping crew chat's label — each correctly fails, so the "fix one file, miss the other" failure mode that shipped stale copy to production earlier today is now guarded. Verified live at 390px and 1280px (both help panels opened and read from the rendered DOM); debug route removed before commit, confirmed absent from the production build. `npm run build` clean, full regression suite passes.

- **Rate type picker copy simplified (2026-08-02, commit `90bce5b`):** the two "Rate type" cards in `BookingRateModeField.tsx` (Booking Request modal) — descriptions changed to `"Set the amount. The DJ can accept or decline"` and `"The DJ sends their price. You decide whether to accept"`, no trailing full stops per the simplified house copy style. **Scope decision worth recording:** there are two separate rate-mode explainers in the app and the requested "Heading / Body" structure mapped ambiguously onto them, so this was confirmed with Isaac before editing rather than guessed — (1) `BookingRateModeField.tsx`, the only one with a genuine two-slot heading+body (semibold `<p>` title + muted `<p>` description), and (2) `EventDjSendOfferControls.tsx`'s "?" help popover in the DJ invite rows, which has a single longer `help` string and its own bold label lead. Isaac chose: update the Rate type cards only, **keeping** `"Fixed offer"`/`"Ask for rate"` as the card headings and folding the new heading+body into the single description line beneath each. Keeping those names matters — that exact terminology is reused in booking summaries (`lib/bookingRequests.ts`), event lineup cards (`EventLineupBookingCard.tsx`), and notification bodies, so replacing them on the picker would have desynced the picker from the rest of the app. `EventDjSendOfferControls.tsx` deliberately untouched. Regression test: `testBookingRateModeFieldDescriptions` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) — asserts both title/description pairs exactly, that the superseded wording is gone, and that no description ends in a full stop; **mutation-tested both ways** (reverting the wording, and re-adding a trailing full stop) and confirmed each correctly fails. Verified live at 390px (cards stack) and 1280px (side-by-side) via a temporary debug route, including that selection state still toggles — route removed before commit, confirmed absent from the production build. `npm run build` clean, full regression suite passes.

- **Booking confirm copy fix (bf0dd9e) never reached the Event Plans flow — actual gap found and fixed (2026-08-02, commit `464cd37`):** reported symptom: physical iPhone still showed "SEND SUMMARY"/"CONFIRM 4 DJS" after `bf0dd9e`/`252e94e` shipped. **Investigated deployment state before touching any code, per explicit instruction:** confirmed both commits exist on `origin/main` (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`), confirmed `follow-the-crowd.vercel.app`'s current deployment (`dpl_4ADkdbUWTc4C36UKBubFRQaEwb1P` at investigation time) was built well after those commits, then fetched the deployed JS chunks directly (no Vercel CLI/login available or needed — `NEXT_PUBLIC_`-style string literals compile straight into the shipped bundle) and grepped them: `"Send summary"` was absent and `"Send Requests"` was present in `SendBookingRequestsPanel.tsx`'s compiled output — that component's fix genuinely was live. **Root cause:** `bf0dd9e` only edited the shared `SendBookingRequestsPanel.tsx` (used by Create Event → Invite DJs, `EventsPageClient.tsx`). The Event Plans "Use Plan" flow (`app/(planner-workspace)/bookings/page.tsx`) has its own separate, hardcoded copy of the identical confirmation UI — never reused the shared component — with its own `"Send summary"` heading and a literal `` `Confirm ${sendableSelectedDjIds.length} DJ...}` `` button template that `bf0dd9e` never touched. That's exactly the screen in the reported screenshot and exactly why the fix appeared to not deploy: it deployed fine, just to the wrong (only) component. Fixed the same two lines in `bookings/page.tsx`, byte-for-byte the same copy change as `bf0dd9e` — `"Send summary"` → `"Summary"`, the dynamic count template → fixed `"Send Requests"` — leaving the `"Confirming"`/`"No new DJs to confirm"` states, all logic, counts, validation, and styling untouched. Regression test `testEventPlansSendPanelCopyMatchesSendBookingRequestsPanel` added (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) to lock this in, since the original `bf0dd9e` regression test only ever read `SendBookingRequestsPanel.tsx` and had no way to catch this second, divergent implementation. **Verified against production, not just source** — after pushing `464cd37`, polled `follow-the-crowd.vercel.app` until Vercel's new deployment (`dpl_CCkarxxDJFrkHn21trCCKVgGXu83`) went live (~40s), then re-fetched and grepped its JS chunks directly: zero remaining occurrences of `"Send summary"` or the `Confirm ${...}` template anywhere in the bundle, and `"Send Requests"`/`"Summary"` confirmed present in *both* the shared-component chunk and the `bookings/page.tsx`-specific chunk (matched by their distinct compiled state-machine shapes). `npm run build` clean, full regression suite passes. **Flagged, not actioned:** `SendBookingRequestsPanel.tsx` and this page's inline implementation are now two independently-maintained copies of the same confirm UI — worth consolidating in a future task so this class of gap (fix one, miss the other) can't recur; out of scope for this copy-only fix.

- **Event Plan Notes text past 8 rows was unreachable; editor heading shortened (2026-08-02, commit `8b03e4c`):** two scoped changes. **(1) Heading:** `"Edit event plan"` → `"Edit"` on the editor card in `booking-plans/page.tsx` — the `"Create event plan"` title, the `"Event Plans"` page title (which comes from the workspace chrome, not this file), and every other control's copy are untouched. **(2) Notes textarea scrolling — root cause found:** two independent bits of code both forced overflow hidden on a *height-capped* field. `.ftc-event-notes-textarea` set `overflow-y: hidden !important` **while also** setting `max-height: calc(8lh + 1.25rem + 2px)` — a capped-height textarea with hidden overflow silently discards everything past the cap, with no way to scroll to it, select it, or edit it. Independently, `applyBoundedTextareaHeight` re-applied `textarea.style.overflowY = "hidden"` **inline** on every value change and every `ResizeObserver` fire, so fixing only the CSS half would not have worked. The field is worst-affected by long unbroken text because its auto-grow measures *explicit newlines* (`countEventNotesLines`), so a paragraph with no `\n` counts as one line, stays at the 4-row minimum, and previously hid ~336px of content unreachably. **Fix:** CSS now `overflow-y: auto`, and the hook sets height only — overflow is solely owned by the stylesheet so the two cannot disagree. Added `overscroll-behavior: contain` (matching `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea`) so a touch scroll hitting either end doesn't chain to the page. **No height override added or changed** — min 4 / max 8 rows and the auto-grow behaviour are byte-identical, so visible height is unchanged; the 500-char cap, counter, 8-line cap, trimming rules and data model are untouched. **Deliberately did NOT adopt the Profile Bio `.ftc-input-shell` wrapper** — that pattern solves focus-*border painting* (a visual defect), not scrollability (a functional one), and adopting it would have restructured the counter/border relationship across all four Notes surfaces, violating "preserve all styling"; verified in-browser that rounded corners render cleanly with the scrollbar active, so it isn't needed. Verified against the real component in a throwaway harness (removed before commit) across empty / short / exactly-500 / 500-unbroken / multi-line-with-blank-lines: field scrolls top→bottom→back, first and last characters reachable and editable, caret follows to the end, page does not scroll, counter correct, 500-char cap still truncates (600 → 500). The shared `PlannerMultilineField` backs all four Notes fields (Event Plan create/edit, Create Event, Edit Event), so all four are fixed together. New regression test `testEventNotesTextareaScrollsWhenContentExceedsCap` locks in that neither half can reintroduce the clipping. `npm run build` clean, full `npm run test:regressions` pass. **Not verified on a physical iPhone** — Isaac should confirm there.
