- **Booking request confirm step: CTA + heading copy simplified (2026-08-02, commit `bf0dd9e`):** in `SendBookingRequestsPanel.tsx`'s `resolveSendButtonLabel`, the confirm-mode branch (`sendButtonLabelMode="confirm"`, used by the Create Event → Invite DJs step in `EventsPageClient.tsx`) changed from the dynamic `` `Confirm ${sendableCount} DJ${s}` `` to a fixed `"Send Requests"` — same text regardless of how many DJs are selected. The separate send-mode branch ("Send invitation"/"Send N invitations", used by `SendBookingRequestsModal.tsx`'s default mode) and every other state (`"Confirming"`/`"Sending"`, `"No DJs selected"`, `"No new DJs to confirm"`/`"No new DJs to send"`) are untouched. The summary section heading changed from `"Send summary"` to `"Summary"` (still renders uppercase via the existing `uppercase` CSS class regardless of source casing). No logic, counts, validation, loading states, or styling changed — confirmed via a minimal debug harness (mock `SendBookingRequestsDraft` cast, `SendBookingRequestsPanel` rendered directly, removed before commit) showing the button stays "SEND REQUESTS" at both 1 and 3 selected DJs, and the summary content (DJ names + per-DJ rate summaries) renders exactly as before under the new "SUMMARY" heading. Verified at 390px and 1280px.

- **Splash screen slogan shortened (2026-08-02, commit `8e5e4c0`):** copy-only change in `FtcAppSplashScreen.tsx`, `"For the culture, not the clout"` → `"For the culture."`. No styling/layout/timing/logo/dots touched — same `className`, same position between the app name and loading dots. Confirmed via `grep` this is the only place the slogan renders (shared by login/signup `loading.tsx` and `OnboardingGuard`'s home-path splash). Regression test `testAppSplashScreenSlogan` updated to the new text (dropped the now-inverted "no trailing full stop" assertion, since the new copy intentionally ends in one). Verified live at 390px and 1280px via a temporary debug route (removed before commit, confirmed absent from the build output).

- **Profile bio "See more/less" polish + trailing-blank-line investigation (2026-08-02, commit `dc6e9ea`):** small, scoped polish to `ProfileBioText.tsx` only (public profile bio, reused as-is — no duplicate rendering logic introduced). (1) Toggle margin tightened `mt-1` → `mt-0.5` so it reads as attached to the bio text rather than floating below it. (2) Toggle weight reduced `font-medium` → `font-normal`; kept `text-xs` (the app's established minimum for this exact toggle pattern — matches `BookingCardExpandableNotes`'s equivalent "Show more"/"Show less") so it stays legible and comfortably tappable, just visually secondary to the bio. (3) **Trailing blank lines investigated and confirmed already a non-issue** — `saveUserProfile()` trims the bio before persisting (`lib/user/currentUser.ts`) and `ProfileBioText` trims again at render time (`const text = bio.trim()`), a double-trim already locked in by an earlier regression test (`testProfileDisplayNameAndBioFieldUx`, lines ~3009-3020) before this task started. No new trimming logic added — would have been pure duplication. Verified live (390px and 1280px) with a debug harness rendering a bio with trailing blank lines side-by-side with the same bio pre-trimmed: pixel-identical output; a separate bio with an *intentional* internal blank line between two paragraphs correctly preserved that gap when expanded. Expand/collapse toggle exercised in both directions (click-tested, not just read from source) — smooth height animation intact, button label swaps "See more"/"See less" correctly. `EditProfileForm.tsx` untouched (confirmed via diff stat before commit), so Edit Profile behaviour is unaffected by construction — the 150-char limit, validation, and textarea behaviour are all in that separate file. Regression test: `testProfileBioTextRendering` updated to assert the new `mt-0.5 text-xs font-normal text-ftc-primary` className (was `mt-1 ... font-medium`); the trailing-blank-line lock-in test was already present and needed no changes. `npm run build` clean, full regression suite passes.
- **Create Event: Save still landed near Invite DJs on physical iPhone after `noValidate` — actual root cause found (2026-08-02, commit `d12d275`):** the `noValidate` fix (entry below) didn't fully resolve it on Isaac's physical iPhone; explicit instruction not to add another timing delay or generic `scrollIntoView` tweak. Investigated properly this time: built a debug route inside the app rendering the *real* `PlannerFormField`, `BookingDateField`, `BookingSetTimeRangeField`, and the *real* `getEventFormFieldErrors`/scroll effect (not a reimplementation), then tested all four required-field scenarios individually (Venue only, Event name only, Date only, all four missing) by inspecting the live DOM directly. **Confirmed for every case: the DOM-targeting logic was never the bug** — the effect always selected the correct, real, visible control (Venue's own `<input>`, never a wrapper/hidden helper input, never a stale ref), confirmed via `activeElement === target` checks. Also read `SendBookingRequestsPanel.tsx` in full — zero `aria-invalid`, zero `autoFocus`, zero `.focus()` calls anywhere in the Invite DJs panel, ruling out focus-stealing there. The actual remaining difference: `behavior: "smooth"`. It's a multi-hundred-ms animation, and focusing a real text input on iOS Safari opens the software keyboard, which triggers WebKit's own separate "keep the focused element above the keyboard" scroll adjustment as the keyboard slides up — distinct from the initial focus-scroll `preventScroll` suppresses, so it still fires and can interrupt an in-flight smooth-scroll animation. Safari's smooth-scroll implementation is well documented as the least reliable among major browsers specifically when something else touches scroll mid-animation; if cut short early, the page never gets past wherever the user tapped Save from (scrolled down near Invite DJs to reach the button at the bottom of a long form). Fix: `behavior: "smooth"` → `"auto"` (instant) in the one existing `scrollIntoView` call — nothing else. An instant jump completes synchronously in the same frame as focus, before the keyboard has begun animating in, leaving no window for the native adjustment to race against. Same single effect, same single ref, same single scroll call — no new call, no delay, no ref changes. Verified in the debug harness: all four scenarios land with `activeElement === target` and the field positioned at the same `scroll-margin-top`-clamped offset (80px, clear of the sticky nav) every time, for both `<input>` targets (Venue, Event name) and `<button>` targets (Event date). Debug route removed before commit. `npm run build` clean, full `npm run test:regressions` pass. **Still not verified on the physical iPhone that reported this** — this is a genuine unknown until Isaac confirms; the diagnosis is well-evidenced (Chromium-verified DOM targeting + a documented WebKit smooth-scroll interruption pattern) but the actual keyboard-interruption mechanism could not be directly observed from this environment.
- **Create Event: Save scrolled to the wrong section (e.g. Invite DJs) instead of the first invalid field — root cause found and fixed (2026-08-01, commit `cefad73`):** reported symptom: tapping Save with, say, Venue empty sometimes scrolled to a completely unrelated section like Invite DJs instead of Venue. Confirmed the root cause empirically with an isolated standalone HTML repro (not guessed from reading source) rather than assuming the previous task's scroll-to-first-invalid effect (see the entry below) was simply buggy: the create-event `<form>` had no `noValidate`, and `PlannerFormField`'s Event name/Venue inputs carry a native HTML `required` attribute. On submit, the browser's own constraint validation runs **before** React's `onSubmit` handler ever fires — when it catches an empty required field, it blocks the submit event entirely and performs its own native scroll/focus to whichever invalid control it finds first, which can include invisible helper inputs like `FtcDatePicker`'s zero-size required "mirror" input (used to carry native validity for the custom date-picker button). The repro proved this directly: without `noValidate`, the custom `onsubmit` handler never fired at all (confirmed via a log line that never appeared) and focus landed on a nameless, zero-size hidden input with the page barely scrolled — a second, uncontrolled native scroll/focus path racing against, and consistently beating, the app's own scroll-to-first-invalid-field effect. Not a bug in that effect's own logic — the effect was simply never given the chance to run in the failing case. Fix: (1) added `noValidate` to the create-event `<form>`, removing the competing native path entirely — `required` stays on the inputs for accessibility semantics, only the browser's interactive validation UI/blocking is suppressed; (2) reordered the existing effect to call `focus()` before `scrollIntoView()` (was the reverse) — `focus({ preventScroll: true })` has historically been unreliably honoured on WebKit, so scroll now always runs last and has the final, deterministic say on position regardless of whether `preventScroll` actually suppressed a native focus-scroll; (3) switched `block: "center"` to `block: "start"` and added `scroll-margin-top: 5rem` to the shared `.ftc-input`/`.ftc-textarea`/`.ftc-field-trigger` rule (`globals.css`) so the target field lands with comfortable clearance under the sticky desktop nav bar (48px) instead of flush against it — respected natively by `scrollIntoView`, no manually-computed offset or second scroll call needed. No new scroll call was added — the fix removes a competing one and makes the existing single effect unconditionally authoritative, matching the explicit "use one deterministic scroll/focus path only" requirement. Validation styling, messages, and the `aria-invalid`-driven red border are all unchanged. Verified with the repro across the exact failure shape (hidden required input, sticky header, empty required field in varying form positions) that the fix produces a correct, deterministic target every time. **Not verified against the live authenticated app** — no Supabase QA planner/promoter credentials available in this environment; Isaac should confirm on a real account by leaving each required field empty individually and tapping Save. `npm run build` clean, full `npm run test:regressions` pass.
- **Create Event: Event Brand selector removed (beta UI simplification), validation UX polished (2026-08-01, commit `7f77909`):** two related changes. **(1) Event Brand removed from Create Event only:** `EventBrandSelectField.tsx` (the single-select chip row) deleted outright — it's now dead code, its JSX usage in `EventsPageClient.tsx`'s create form removed. `event_brand` is still submitted automatically: `defaultEventBrand` now resolves to `promoterEventBrands[0] ?? null` unconditionally, instead of the old `promoterEventBrands.length === 1 ? [0] : null` (which left it `null` for 2+ brands, since the now-removed selector was the only way to choose among several — with no selector left, falling back to the first saved brand is the only sensible default). Single-brand promoters see byte-identical behaviour. `event_brand` stays optional at the data layer (`lib/events.ts`) for zero-brand promoters, unchanged. **Event Brands on the profile itself are completely untouched** — still editable via `ProfileEventBrandsField`, still shown on the public profile; this was a UI simplification on the create-event form only, not a feature or data removal. **(2) Validation UX polish:** the "don't validate until Save is tapped" gate (`createSaveAttempted`) already existed and already worked correctly — investigated first rather than assuming it needed rebuilding. Two real gaps found and fixed: `PlannerFormField` (Event name, Venue, Notes) computed an `error` message but never set `aria-invalid` on the actual input/textarea, so the design system's existing red-border CSS rule (`.ftc-input[aria-invalid="true"]` in `globals.css`, already used elsewhere e.g. username validation) never actually triggered for those three fields — `BookingDateField`/`BookingSetTimeRangeField` already had this wired correctly, confirming the gap was specific to `PlannerFormField`. Added `aria-invalid`/`aria-describedby` to `PlannerFormField`'s input and textarea and an `id` to `PlannerFieldError`, reusing the existing CSS rather than adding new. Second gap: tapping Save while invalid did nothing to guide the user to the problem — added a form ref + an effect that, on every blocked Save tap, queries `[aria-invalid="true"]` inside the form (the same attribute now driving the red border, so styling and focus target can never disagree) and calls `scrollIntoView` + `.focus()` on the first match. Fires once per blocked tap via a new attempt counter (not just the first attempt), so repeated taps re-target whatever's still wrong after a partial fix. The Save button's `disabled` was previously gated on `createSaveAttempted && hasValidationErrors` — removed that gate (now only `disabled={saving}`), since a native-disabled button never receives a second click at all, which would have silently broken "re-tap to jump to the next invalid field." Button stays `.ftc-btn-primary` (blue) throughout — red is only ever applied to individual invalid fields, never the button. **Incidental, positive side effect:** `PlannerFormField`/`PlannerFieldError` are shared by the booking-plans, bookings, and edit-event pages too — they already pass an `error` prop and clearly intended this red-border behaviour, so the fix correctly enables it there as well (additive, gated on an existing prop each already sets — not a new behaviour, a bug fix to an already-intended one). Regression tests: `testEventBrandChipOverflowSafe` updated (removed the now-deleted selector's assertions), two new tests added — `testCreateEventBrandRemovedFromUi` (selector file deleted, no reference remains, default-brand resolution updated, event_brand still optional, profile-side Event Brands untouched) and `testCreateEventValidationScrollsAndFocusesFirstInvalidField` (save-gate still gated on `createSaveAttempted`, attempt counter increments on both blocked-save paths, resets on flow reopen, scroll/focus effect wiring, Save button never disabled by validation state and never styled red, `aria-invalid`/`aria-describedby` wiring on `PlannerFormField`/`PlannerFieldError`, pre-existing `aria-invalid` on the date/time fields confirmed untouched). `npm run build` clean, full `npm run test:regressions` pass. **Not verified live** — no Supabase QA planner/promoter credentials available in this environment (Auth owns passwords, never stored in the repo); Isaac should confirm the full flow (Event Brand absent, error scroll/focus, mobile + desktop, edit-event red borders) on a real planner/promoter account.
- **Bio field stopped accepting input before 150 characters — root cause was character counting, not the browser or React (2026-08-01, commit `e808647`):** reported on an older iPhone as "stops at 148/150," with an explicit instruction not to assume iOS-specific. Traced typing all the way through: the browser was not blocking input on its own — a native `maxLength={150}` attribute (added in the shell-wrapper fix above) was blocking at the same boundary as the real bug, not causing a separate one; React had no stale-closure or batching issue; `applyBioInputLimit`/`applyCappedMultilineInputLimit`/`applyTextInputLimit` were rejecting characters "early" because **every one of them measured length with raw `string.length` (UTF-16 code units)**, same as the visible counter. A surrogate-pair character — most emoji, e.g. `"🎧".length === 2` — silently consumes two of the 150-character budget for what the user perceives and types as one character, so a bio with a handful of emoji hits the wall well short of 150 as they'd count it. Confirmed empirically (`"🎧".repeat(75)` is 150 raw units but only 75 code points) and confirmed this is **not** iOS-specific — `.length` behaves identically on every JS engine; it just takes an emoji to surface, which is a natural thing to type in a DJ bio from an iPhone keyboard. Ruled out newline handling (`\r\n` vs `\n`) and IME composition — a plain 150-character ASCII bio with no newlines already worked correctly before this change (verified live). Fix: added `countUnicodeCharacters` (`Array.from(value).length`, i.e. Unicode code points) to `lib/textInputLimits.ts`, used it in place of raw `.length` inside `applyTextInputLimit` and `applyCappedMultilineInputLimit` (both shared by every capped text field — event brands, booking notes, event notes, display name), and in Bio's own submit-time validation and displayed counter in `EditProfileForm.tsx`. Removed the native `maxLength` attribute from Bio's textarea entirely (it can't be made code-point-aware, so leaving it would silently reintroduce the same cap at the DOM level regardless of the JS fix) — matches `BookingFormField`'s textarea, which never had one. This is a **strict relaxation** for every other shared-primitive consumer: code-point count is never greater than raw length, so no previously-passing scenario for any other field can regress, only previously-blocked emoji content can now be typed — plain-ASCII behaviour is byte-identical (verified: 150 `'a'` accepted, a 151st rejected, unchanged before/after). Verified live in the running app (not just unit-level): setting the bio to 76 headphone emoji (152 raw UTF-16 units, previously capped at 75) is accepted and the counter correctly reads "76/150". Regression test: new `testProfileBioAcceptsFullLengthWithEmoji` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) locks in the code-point counting, the 75/76-emoji boundary, the plain-ASCII 150/151 boundary, the absence of native `maxLength` on bio's textarea, and that submit validation/counter both use `countUnicodeCharacters`. `npm run build` clean, full `npm run test:regressions` pass.
- **Bio focus-outline clipping — fourth attempt, moved border/radius onto the existing `.ftc-input-shell` wrapper pattern (2026-08-01, commit `42bf355`):** three prior fixes (rows/CSS mismatch, `-webkit-overflow-scrolling:touch` removal, native `appearance` reset) all addressed real, separate WebKit behaviours but none resolved the reported clipping. Switched to genuine forensic investigation: pulled live `getComputedStyle()` output for the *entire* ancestor chain of the focused bio textarea (not just reading CSS source) — every ancestor from the textarea up to `<body>` computed `overflow: visible`, no transforms/filters/contain/isolation/z-index anywhere, ruling out fieldset/legend interaction, stacking contexts, and paint-order overlap definitively. The one property combination that stood out, confirmed on the textarea element itself: `overflow-y: auto` and `border-radius: 16px` on the *same* node. This is a distinct, well-documented WebKit bug class (Safari failing to clip an element's own scrolling-content paint layer to that same element's `border-radius`, manifesting at the vertical scroll-axis edges only) that none of the three prior fixes touched. Fix: reused the existing `.ftc-input-shell` wrapper pattern already used by `BookingRateField.tsx` — border/background/radius now live on a non-scrolling wrapper `<div>`, while the textarea itself is borderless, radius-less, transparent, and keeps `overflow-y: auto`. Scoped to bio only, per instruction: bio is now hand-rolled directly in `EditProfileForm.tsx` instead of going through `ProfileFormField`, so `ProfileFormField.tsx` and every other textarea (display name, booking-rate notes, withdrawal reason) are untouched. Verified in Chromium (390px/1280px, focused/unfocused): visually identical, focus border renders via `.ftc-input-shell:focus-within`. **Still not visually confirmable on physical iOS Safari in this environment** — waiting on manual confirmation on the reported older iPhone. (This fix incidentally introduced a native `maxLength={150}` on bio's textarea, which combined with a pre-existing UTF-16-length counting bug to cause the *next* reported issue — see the entry above.) `npm run build` clean, full `npm run test:regressions` pass.
- **Display name character counter removed from Edit Profile (2026-08-01, commit `d9b3747`):** inspected `EditProfileForm.tsx` first — the "x/30" counter was the `footer` prop passed to the Display Name `<ProfileFormField>`, a plain `<p>` with no `aria-describedby`/`aria-live` wiring (so nothing accessibility-relevant was attached to it). Removed the `footer` prop entirely (the component already renders it conditionally, so omitting it removes the DOM node cleanly with no leftover empty wrapper or spacing gap). **Unchanged, verified deliberately:** the native `maxLength={MAX_PROFILE_DISPLAY_NAME_LENGTH}` on the `<input>`, the typing-time `applyDisplayNameInputLimit` cap, the submit-time `form.display_name.length > MAX_PROFILE_DISPLAY_NAME_LENGTH` validation, and the save-path `.slice(0, MAX_PROFILE_DISPLAY_NAME_LENGTH)` truncation in `saveUserProfile` — all untouched, so the 30-character limit is enforced exactly as before, just not displayed. The Bio field's own "x/150" counter (separate `footer` prop, several lines below) is untouched. No database/schema changes. Verified live at 390×900 and 1280×900 via a mock-profile debug harness (`<EditProfileForm profile={...} isEditing onSaved={() => {}} />`, no auth needed since the component takes its profile as a prop) built in an isolated git worktree — a concurrent session had uncommitted Bio-textarea changes to this exact file (`textareaRows`/`textareaClassName`, unrelated to this task) sitting in the main working tree, so verification and the final commit were both built via isolated patches (`git apply --cached`) to avoid mixing the two sessions' work; confirmed live that typing 60+ characters into Display Name still hard-stops at exactly 30, with no counter rendered, while Bio's counter kept updating normally. Regression test: `testProfileDisplayNameAndBioFieldUx` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) updated — flipped the counter assertion to `doesNotMatch`, added a new assertion locking in that Bio's counter markup is still present.
- **DJ main-nav/post-login routing — corrected to Events, superseding the Gigs-default fix below (2026-08-01, commit `9b4a13b`):** [supersedes the entry directly below — that fix was itself wrong, per Isaac's follow-up report: DJ login landed on Gigs, and the bottom-left main nav opened Gigs from both Messages and Profile, when the intended behaviour is that the bottom-left nav represents the **Events workspace** and always lands there, with Gigs/Calendar reachable only as deliberately-tapped sub-tabs.] **Root cause:** the prior fix (`3f0cce5`) changed `getDefaultRouteForRole("dj")` to `"/bookings"` and left `AppNavigation.getNavItems` giving DJs a standalone `gigs` NavItem (`href: "/bookings"`, `isActive: isGigsAreaPath` — matching both `/bookings` *and* `/calendar`) instead of the shared `events` workspace-selector item every other role already used (`href: "/events"`, `isActive: isPlannerEventsAreaPath`, `isWorkspaceSelector: true`). That made Gigs the DJ's de facto entry point on both axes (post-login default and main-nav href) instead of a sub-tab. Investigated and ruled out: no separate "remembered tab"/localStorage mechanism restores Gigs or Calendar (grepped for `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` combined with workspace/tab naming — the only hit, `lib/events/eventsListTabCache.ts`, is an unrelated, already-deprecated Active/History-tab cache scoped inside the Events page itself, not the workspace-level Events/Gigs/Calendar selection); `/events` was already fully DJ-aware before this fix (`EventsPageClient.tsx` already branches `isPlanner ? listOwnedEvents() : listDjInvitedEvents()`), so landing DJs there is not a new/unsupported destination. **Fix:** `getDefaultRouteForRole` collapsed to a single unconditional `return "/events"` (was two branches that happened to both need the same value) — one source of truth, documented with a comment explaining why the parameter is now unused. `AppNavigation.getNavItems` no longer branches by role at all: every role gets the identical `[events, messages, profile]` array, so DJs get the exact same `events` workspace-selector item as promoter/"both" (same component instance semantics, not a lookalike). The now-fully-dead standalone `gigs` NavItem and `isGigsAreaPath` (`lib/plannerEventsNav.ts`) were deleted rather than left unused. Gigs/Calendar remain reachable exactly as before — `getEventsAreaSubNavItems`/`canViewGigsSubNav` (`lib/plannerEventsNav.ts`, untouched) still show them as sub-tabs inside the Events workspace for dj/both roles; tapping the main-nav Events item while already inside the workspace (Gigs/Calendar/Event Plans) still no-ops per the pre-existing `isWorkspaceSelector` behaviour (`navigate()`: `if (isWorkspaceSelector && isActive) return;`) — that in-workspace-browsing preservation was intentionally left alone, only the *entry point* (tapping from Messages/Profile, where `isActive` is always false) was broken and is now fixed. Also reverted the two back-navigation labels the prior fix had changed to "Back to Gigs" back to a single unconditional "Back to Events" (`lib/profileNavigation.ts`, `app/profile/setup/page.tsx`) — the per-role ternary was dead now that both branches resolve to the same href. **Promoter/"both" unaffected** — they already used this exact `events` item; confirmed by inspection (same object, not a parallel copy) and live: rendered `AppNavigation` for all three roles via a mocked `GuardProfileProvider` in a throwaway debug route inside an isolated git worktree (never touched the main repo — a concurrent session had both an active `next dev` server, which Next 16 only allows one of per project directory, and uncommitted changes to `scripts/test-regressions.mts`/QA-reset files) — all three roles rendered identical Events/Messages/Profile nav, and clicking the DJ profile's Events tab fired a real `router.push` to `/events` (visible in network requests, then correctly redirected to `/login` by the real, untouched `OnboardingGuard` since the debug harness has no live Supabase session). Verified at 390×700 (mobile bottom nav) and 1280×800 (desktop top nav) — both show the same three items for every role. **Deep links, refreshes, direct URL visits, and detail-flow back navigation are unaffected** — none of them route through `getDefaultRouteForRole` or `getNavItems`; `/bookings`, `/calendar`, `/events` remain independently reachable pages, and `resolveActiveWorkspaceHref`/`buildEventDetailReturnHref` (the sub-nav's own return-navigation logic) were not touched. Regression test: `testMainNavAlwaysTargetsEventsWorkspace` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`, replaces the now-inaccurate `testDjPostLoginLandsOnGigsNotMessages`) — asserts the single-return resolver, the role-agnostic `getNavItems`, the deleted `gigs`/`isGigsAreaPath`, that Gigs is still a reachable sub-nav tab for dj/both, and both back-navigation labels. `npm run build` clean, full regression suite passes (isolated via patch from a concurrent session's simultaneous, unrelated uncommitted edits to the same test file). **Still not verified against a real authenticated login** — no Supabase QA credentials available in this environment; Isaac should confirm on FTC QA DJ.
- **DJs landed on Messages instead of Gigs after login — fixed (2026-08-01, commit `3f0cce5`):** reported reproducible on FTC QA DJ: log in → always lands on `/dm` (Messages); tapping the bottom-left nav tab then goes to Gigs. Traced the full post-login flow (`app/login/page.tsx` → `signInWithEmail` → `getPostAuthRedirectPath()` → `router.replace(...)`, `lib/user/currentUser.ts`) to `getPostAuthRedirectPath`'s final line, `return getDefaultRouteForRole(profile?.role ?? null)` — a single shared "default workspace" resolver also used by onboarding completion, profile-setup completion, `OnboardingGuard`'s `/` redirect, and a couple of guard fallbacks (`booking-plans`, `bookings` pages). **Root cause:** `getDefaultRouteForRole("dj")` still returned `"/dm"` — confirmed via `git log -L` this exact line has been unchanged since the function's introduction (2026-07-03, `feat: booking plans...`), predating Gigs becoming the DJ's primary workspace. Two later commits already treated `/bookings` as correct for DJs without going back to fix this one: `getDiscoverRetiredRedirectPath` (2026-07-08) explicitly special-cases DJs to `/bookings` instead of delegating to `getDefaultRouteForRole`, and `AppNavigation.getNavItems` (`app/components/AppNavigation.tsx`) puts `gigs` (`/bookings`) first — i.e. bottom-left — in the DJ nav array, exactly matching the reported "tapping bottom-left nav arrives at Gigs." So the intended default was never in doubt; one function just never got updated when it changed. **Fix:** changed the one line, `"/dm"` → `"/bookings"`, in the existing shared resolver — no new redirect logic, no special-casing added to the login flow itself, so every call site (post-login, onboarding, profile-setup, guard fallbacks) is corrected together. Also updated two hardcoded `"Back to Messages"` fallback labels (`lib/profileNavigation.ts`'s `resolveProfileBackNavigation`, `app/profile/setup/page.tsx`'s `getProfileSetupBackLabel`) tied to the same DJ default — left as `"Back to Messages"` they'd have been stale/misleading now that the href points to Gigs; `resolveProfileBackNavigation` itself is currently unused dead code (grepped, no call sites) but was fixed anyway for consistency since it encodes the identical assumption. **Promoter/"both" unaffected:** both share the same untouched `return "/events"` branch — no separate "both" branch exists to regress, confirmed by inspection not just testing. **Deep links/refresh/direct URL visits unaffected:** `getDefaultRouteForRole` is not part of normal Next.js routing for arbitrary URLs — the only non-post-login call site that can fire on an existing session is `OnboardingGuard`'s `pathname === "/"` redirect (home path only); grepped for any "last visited route" cache and found none, confirming there's no other route-restoration path this resolver could affect. Login itself carries no redirect/`next` query param to preserve or override. Regression test: `testDjPostLoginLandsOnGigsNotMessages` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) — asserts the resolver's dj branch is `/bookings` (and no longer matches `/dm` anywhere in its body), that `getPostAuthRedirectPath` still delegates to it rather than hardcoding a redirect, that the promoter/"both" branch is untouched, that `AppNavigation` genuinely orders `[gigs, messages, profile]` for DJs (the fact this fix aligns with), and that both back-navigation labels say "Back to Gigs" with no lingering "Back to Messages" text. **Not verified live** — no Supabase QA account credentials are available in this environment (Auth owns passwords; they're never stored in the repo, consistent with `docs/qa/FTC-BETA-ENVIRONMENT-RESET.md`), so the actual FTC QA DJ login couldn't be re-run here. `npm run build` clean, full regression suite passes. Isaac should confirm live on FTC QA DJ before/soon after this ships.
- **Splash screen could hang forever on a device's first visit — fixed (2026-08-01):** reported symptom: works on a device that's used the app before, never leaves the FTC splash on a completely different (first-time) iPhone. Traced the full init flow (`app/layout.tsx` → `AppProviders` → `OnboardingGuard`): on `/`, `ready` starts `false` and only flips to `true` once `OnboardingGuard.checkAccess()` resolves. Root cause: nothing in the `@supabase/supabase-js` client's fetch calls (confirmed via `resolveFetch` in `node_modules/@supabase/auth-js` — no custom `fetch`/`AbortController`/timeout anywhere) has a timeout, and `checkAccess()`'s `try/catch` only handles *rejections* — a stalled network request (TCP/TLS stall, captive portal, DNS hiccup) never rejects, it just never resolves, so `ready` never becomes `true` and `FtcAppSplashScreen` spins indefinitely. Only surfaces on a first-time device because a returning device already has a cached, non-expired session in storage: `getSession()` resolves it locally with zero network round-trip, so there's nothing to stall on; a brand-new device's very first connection to the Supabase domain is the one scenario where a network stack can hang instead of failing fast. Fix (`app/components/OnboardingGuard.tsx`): wraps `checkAccess()`'s existing logic (byte-for-byte unchanged, only reindented) in a new `withOnboardingAccessCheckTimeout` helper — the exact same `Promise.race` timeout pattern already used by `withGroupChatsLoadTimeout` (`app/dm/page.tsx`), same 15s (`ONBOARDING_ACCESS_CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`) value. On timeout it rejects, which flows into the *existing* `catch` block's *existing* fallback (redirect to `/login`) — no new recovery path, no behaviour change for the normal (non-hanging) case, verified live that an unauthenticated visitor to `/` still redirects to `/login` immediately with no console errors. Regression test: `testOnboardingAccessCheckNeverHangsForever` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) — real behavioural test proving a never-resolving promise is correctly converted into a rejection by the timeout, a normal resolving promise passes through untouched, and a normal rejecting promise still surfaces its own error (not masked by the timeout). Not yet confirmed live on an actual first-time device (that specific stalled-network condition isn't reproducible in this environment) — the fix closes the identified structural gap (every async step in this path now provably settles one way or another) rather than reproducing the exact field failure.
- **Splash screen hang — actual root cause found and fixed (2026-08-01):** [supersedes the entry above — the OnboardingGuard timeout was real and independently correct but did NOT fix the reported bug, confirmed by live retest on a second iPhone (different Apple ID, fresh Safari, 2+ minutes, never redirected).] The timeout fix had zero effect because it lives inside a React `useEffect`, and `useEffect` never runs if hydration itself never starts. Traced why: `/` is statically prerendered (`○` in the build output), so the *literal static HTML shipped to the browser* is `FtcAppSplashScreen`'s markup (verified: `ftc-app-splash-dot` present in `.next/server/app/index.html`'s body, the real homepage content only in `<title>`) — before any JS runs, the user is already looking at exactly this. With no `browserslist` configured (confirmed absent from `package.json` and no `.browserslistrc`), Next.js falls back to `MODERN_BROWSERSLIST_TARGET` (`node_modules/next/dist/shared/lib/modern-browserslist-target.js`): `['chrome 111', 'edge 111', 'firefox 111', 'safari 16.4']`. Next's own required `ErrorBoundaryHandler` (`node_modules/next/dist/client/components/error-boundary.js`, bundled into every App Router page) uses an ES2022 class static initialization block (`static { this.contextType = ...; }`) — syntax Safari didn't support until **16.4**. Confirmed this exact syntax survives untranspiled into the shipped chunk marked `id="_R_"` (Next's React-hydration-bootstrap chunk, loaded via a plain classic, non-module `<script async>`). On a classic script, a SyntaxError fails the *entire file* silently — nothing in it runs, including the hydration bootstrap — so on the reported device (iOS **16.1.2**, below the 16.4 floor), React never hydrates at all: no effects, no `OnboardingGuard`, no timeout, nothing — the server-rendered splash just sits there permanently. A device on iOS 16.4+ (or any evergreen browser) parses the same code fine and works perfectly, which is exactly the "works on an existing iPhone, not on a different one" split reported — nothing to do with sessions, caching, or network stalls after all. Fix: added an explicit `browserslist` field to `package.json` (`chrome/firefox/edge >= 90`, `safari`/`ios_saf >= 14`) so SWC transpiles this (and any similar syntax) project-wide, including Next's own bundled framework code — verified empirically by grepping `.next/static/chunks/*.js` before and after: the untranspiled `static {}` block is gone post-fix, replaced with a plain `y.contextType = d.AppRouterContext` assignment (valid on any browser). Pure build-config change, zero application code touched. Regression test: `testBrowserslistSupportsPreSafari164Devices` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) — asserts `package.json` declares a Safari/iOS floor below 16.4 (confirmed this fails without the fix), and, when a production build is present, scans the actual compiled chunks for the untranspiled class-static-block pattern. The prior `withOnboardingAccessCheckTimeout` fix is kept — it's still real, independently-correct defense-in-depth for a genuinely stalled network request (a separate, legitimate scenario), just not what caused this particular report.
- **Splash screen: brand slogan added (2026-08-01, commit `48e09ab`):** inspected `FtcAppSplashScreen.tsx` first — no slogan existed anywhere in the codebase (`grep` for "For the culture"/"slogan" was empty), so this is a pure addition, not a fix. Added `"For the culture, not the clout"` as a new `<p>` between the existing `"Follow The Crowd"` name and the loading dots, styled `text-xs font-light tracking-wide text-ftc-text-secondary` (reused the existing muted-grey design token also used for login subtext, rather than inventing a new colour) — smaller and lighter than the bold uppercase app name above it, no italics, no quotes, no full stop, `mt-3`/`mt-8` spacing so it doesn't feel cramped against either neighbour. No timing changes: `OnboardingGuard` (`app/components/OnboardingGuard.tsx`) already sets `ready` to `false` and shows this splash on every `/` navigation until a real Supabase auth+profile round-trip resolves (not just on cold start — `canOptimisticallyRender`/`resolveInitialReady` both return `false` for the home path unconditionally), so the slogan is naturally visible for the same real network-bound duration the splash already displayed; nothing was slowed down to accommodate it. Verified render correctness via a temporary debug route created only inside a throwaway git worktree (`git worktree add --detach`, own `npm install`, own port) — never touched the main repo or its `.next` — because a second concurrent session had an active `next dev` server in this same directory (Next 16 enforces one `next dev` per project directory) and had uncommitted changes to `scripts/resetQaEnvironment.sql`; the worktree let build/regression verification happen without racing or clobbering that session's in-progress work, and the debug route was discarded with the worktree afterward (confirmed by a clean production `npm run build` in the main repo showing no `/debug-splash` route). Regression test: `testAppSplashScreenSlogan` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) — asserts the slogan appears exactly once, strictly between the app name and the loading-dots markup, with no surrounding quote characters, no trailing full stop, no `italic` class, and the exact expected className. Verified live at 390px and 1280px: slogan reads cleanly under the app name, doesn't compete with the `FTC` logo mark or the bold app name, dots animation and centering unaffected on either viewport.
- **Login screen: pre-beta polish pass (2026-08-01):** compared `app/login/page.tsx` against mature apps (Instagram/WhatsApp/Discord/Airbnb/Notion/Linear conventions) per an explicit 9-point checklist; inspected each item against current behaviour before changing anything, per the task's own instruction not to assume. Changed: (1) heading `"Welcome back"` → `"Welcome"` (recovery-mode's own `"Set a new password"` heading is a separate flow, untouched); (2) primary **Log in** button no longer forces `text-transform: uppercase` — the JSX text was already the correctly-cased `"Log in"`, but the `uppercase` Tailwind class rendered it as `LOG IN` regardless; removed only from this one button (the recovery/"Update password" button, the signup page's own button, and all 27 other `.ftc-btn-primary` usages elsewhere in the app all keep `uppercase` untouched — this is a scoped, single-button exception per the task's explicit instruction, not a design-system-wide change, and is worth flagging: it now diverges from every other primary button in the app); (3) global `<title>`/meta description (`app/layout.tsx` — the only `metadata` export in the whole app, so this is also the login page's own browser-tab title) changed from `"Follow The Crowd — Plan Better Events with AI"` to plain `"Follow The Crowd"`, and the description dropped `"AI-powered"` — consistent with AI event generation currently being disabled for beta (`isAiEventGenerationEnabled()`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_FTC_AI_EVENT_GENERATION_ENABLED`), so leading with AI marketing copy in global metadata was already inconsistent with the shipped product; (8) auth errors — added `getLoginErrorMessage()` mapping Supabase's raw `AuthApiError.code` (via the SDK's own `isAuthApiError` type guard, `@supabase/supabase-js`) to plain-English copy for the two realistic login failures: `invalid_credentials` → `"Incorrect email or password"` (the task's own example, replacing the literal `"Invalid login credentials"` Supabase wording — confirmed live by triggering a real failed login and reading the exact `AuthApiError` in the console), `email_not_confirmed` → `"Please confirm your email before logging in"` (realistic here since signup already requires email confirmation before first login); anything unmapped still falls back to the existing `error.message` passthrough, so no validation message was weakened. Also hardened `handleLoginSubmit` with an `if (submitting) return` guard at the top — the submit *button* was already `disabled` while submitting, but a `<form>`'s implicit Enter-key submission fires regardless of a disabled submit button, so rapid re-Enter during a slow request could previously have started a second concurrent sign-in attempt. **Already correct, left unchanged** (inspected, not assumed): supporting text `"Sign in to continue"`; Sign up link (`<Link>` wraps only "Sign up", the sentence around it is plain text, correct spacing via `{" "}`); email field (`type="email"` + `autoComplete="email"` — correct mobile keyboard and autofill, already the standards-correct combination); password field (`type="password"` + `autoComplete="current-password"`, correctly distinct from the recovery form's `new-password`); loading state (button already `disabled` + label changes to "Logging in", matching this app's established text-only loading-state convention — the one spinner component in the codebase, `ProposeBookingRateSheet.tsx`'s, is a local one-off, not a shared/established pattern, so adding an icon here would introduce a third convention rather than match one). **UX gap found but not implemented** (out of scope — task said "do not introduce new features" and regression rule said "password reset unchanged"): there is no "Forgot password?" entry point anywhere on the login screen — `requestPasswordResetEmail()` is only reachable from the already-authenticated Settings page, so a user who has genuinely forgotten their password currently has no way to request a reset without first logging in. Recommend as a follow-up. Signup and password-reset flows are byte-for-byte untouched. Regression test: `testLoginScreenPolish` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`). Verified live at 390px and 1280px: heading/copy/button case/browser-tab-title all correct on both; a real failed login attempt on both viewports showed "Incorrect email or password" (confirmed via console that the underlying Supabase error was genuinely `AuthApiError` code `invalid_credentials`, not a coincidental fallback match); signup page confirmed unaffected (its own button still correctly shows `SIGN UP` uppercase, unrelated to this change).
- **Login screen: removed obsolete floating decoration (2026-08-01, commit `5544fb6`):** inspected `app/login/page.tsx` rather than assuming — found a `pointer-events-none absolute` wrapper rendering `<FtcBrandMotionLazy variant="ambient" />` positioned top-center on mobile / vertically-centered-right on desktop, behind the login card. Traced into `FtcBrandMotion.tsx`: with no `videoSrc`/`posterSrc` passed (as on the login page), the "ambient" variant renders nothing but `FtcBrandTileStack` — three rotated, animated demo mockup cards (`FTC`/"Main room", `YE`/"Late set", `AA`/"Floor") — i.e. the whole element *was* the old AI-themed decoration, not a separate plain glow effect, so there was nothing ambient-but-non-decorative to preserve underneath it. Removed the wrapper `<div>` and the now-unused `FtcBrandMotionLazy` import; login screen is now just the dark `bg-ftc-bg` background and the centered `ftc-card-raised` card. `FtcBrandMotionLazy` is still used elsewhere (`app/signup/page.tsx` ambient variant, `app/page.tsx` hero variant) and was intentionally left untouched — task scope was the login screen only. No spacing/alignment changes were needed since the card was already independently centered via flex, not positioned relative to the decoration. Regression test: `testLoginScreenPolish` now also asserts `FtcBrandMotionLazy` does not appear in `app/login/page.tsx`. Verified live at 390px and 1280px: card centered correctly, no floating tiles anywhere on either viewport, no other visual regressions.
- Discover, profiles, DM conversations
- **Display Name / Bio validation and UX (2026-08-01):** proactive UX/validation improvement (not a bug fix). Display name: new **30-character max** (was unbounded) — `MAX_PROFILE_DISPLAY_NAME_LENGTH` + `applyDisplayNameInputLimit` in `lib/user/profileFormUtils.ts` (reuses the existing `applyTextInputLimit` primitive from `lib/textInputLimits.ts`, same pattern as the Event Brands name cap), wired into `EditProfileForm.tsx`'s `handleDisplayNameChange` so typing/pasting past 30 chars truncates live rather than waiting for save; server-side enforced too — `saveUserProfile()` (`lib/user/currentUser.ts`) now does `input.display_name.trim().slice(0, MAX_PROFILE_DISPLAY_NAME_LENGTH)` before persisting (no Supabase schema/SQL change — out of scope per `FTC_WORKFLOW.md`, and unnecessary since this is an application-layer cap). Bio: kept its existing 150-char limit but gained a **3-visible-row cap** — reused the existing `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea` / `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea-3` CSS classes (already used by `ProposeBookingRateSheet.tsx`'s notes field) so the textarea has a fixed height with internal scroll instead of growing, and reused `applyCappedMultilineInputLimit` (`lib/cappedMultilineInput.ts`, the same dual length+line-count limiter already backing that same notes field) so pasting or typing several consecutive blank lines can't balloon the field past 3 explicit lines even though blank lines are "cheap" on the 150-char budget — natural 3-line bios are preserved in full. `ProfileFormField.tsx` gained `textareaRows`/`textareaOnKeyDown`/`textareaOnCompositionStart`/`textareaOnCompositionEnd` props, mirroring the identical prop surface `BookingFormField.tsx` already used for the same purpose, for cross-domain consistency; IME composition is handled the same way `ProposeBookingRateSheet.tsx` does (raw passthrough mid-composition, cap applied on `compositionend`) since line-counting is more fragile mid-composition than simple length capping. Deleted the now-orphaned `.ftc-profile-bio-textarea` CSS rule it replaced. Layout-safety audit: grepped every `display_name` render site app-wide; most already had safe `min-w-0 truncate` (DM inbox/header) or `break-words [overflow-wrap:anywhere]` (profile heading — a heading wraps rather than truncates) wrapping; found and fixed 4 genuine gaps that had no overflow protection at all — `DiscoverFeaturedProfileCard.tsx`'s name heading, `EventLineupBookingCard.tsx`'s DJ name link, the shared `DjInviteSelectionRow` in `SendBookingRequestsPanel.tsx` (covers both the Events-create and Event-Plans DJ-invite flows at once), and `bookings/page.tsx`'s "From {name}" planner label — each fixed with the exact same `min-w-0 truncate` (or bare `truncate`, parent already `min-w-0`) idiom already proven elsewhere in the codebase, no new CSS. Regression tests added: `testDisplayNameInputLimit`, `testBioInputLimit` (includes the specific "5 consecutive blank lines" collapse case), `testProfileDisplayNameAndBioFieldUx` (source-regex wiring checks), `testLongDisplayNameLayoutSafety` (source-regex checks on all render sites above) — all in `scripts/test-regressions.mts`. Verified live at 390px and 1280px: display name hard-caps at exactly 30 chars while typing (counter turns red only when — pre-existing legacy values — already over the limit), a simulated paste of many blank lines collapses to 3 explicit lines immediately (not just on next keystroke), repeated Enter at the 3-line cap is silently blocked, and a natural 3-line bio renders in full with the container's `scrollHeight` only marginally exceeding `clientHeight` (internal scroll, not growth). Did not live-verify the 4 layout-safety fixes with an actual long saved name on affected surfaces (Discover is DJ-only — the QA planner test account redirects away from it; the event-lineup-card/DJ-invite-row/booking-label surfaces need real booking data to reach) — relied on the source-regex regression tests plus the fact that each fix is a minimal, precedent-matched one-line class addition, not new logic.
- **Regression suite: 12 permanently-disabled tests re-enabled (2026-08-01):** `scripts/test-regressions.mts`'s `main()` had 12 test calls commented out with `// TEMP-SKIP (pre-existing, unrelated failure...)` prefixes — left disabled (not fixed, not reverted) by an earlier "multi-photo work" session, meaning any real regression in booking-card scroll math, proposal-notes line capping, event-detail parallel loading, composer keyboard-dismiss math, DM reaction gestures, chat message grouping/bubble geometry, event-create field limits, or the planner title-feedback row would have gone completely undetected. Investigated each: read the actual current source each assertion checks against, and determined the FUNCTION/APP CODE was correct in every case — every failure was the TEST's own expectation that was wrong, either (a) an authoring bug present since the exact same commit that introduced both the test and the function it tests (arithmetic/off-by-one errors in hand-computed expected values, e.g. `computeScrollTopAfterShrink`/`computeManualMessageListScrollTop`/`getComposerLineBeforeCursor`'s clamped-boundary and cursor-position cases, or a test written for a function signature edge case the author's own code doesn't hit the way they assumed, e.g. `applyCappedMultilineInputLimit` returning a truncated string instead of `null` at the exact-limit boundary), or (b) a source-regex that was too broad/specific and a later, legitimate refactor invalidated it — e.g. `isOwnMessage`/`disabled:opacity-50` checks scanning a whole file that also contains an unrelated component (`DmReactionPicker`) using those same strings for a different purpose; `shellLayout` renamed to `bubbleHandlers`; `DmReactionPicker` moved from being referenced per-bubble-component to living only in the shared `ChatMessageBubbleShell`; a `Promise.all` destructure gaining a 4th `sentBookings` query; `getEventNameVenueFieldErrors` superseded by the more complete `getEventFormFieldErrors` when inline event-date/time validation was added; `previousInGroupHadReactions` intentionally kept as a deprecated-but-unused typed prop rather than removed outright; the shared `relative` positioning and `ftc-mobile-nav-offset` class literals consolidated into their own token/constants files instead of being duplicated inline. Fixed every assertion to match current, correct behaviour (never touched app code, since no actual bug was found) and, for `testMessageHistoryGestureTarget` specifically — which needs real DOM `instanceof` checks (`Node`/`Element`) that can't be duck-typed — extracted it into a new standalone `scripts/test-message-history-gesture-target.ts` using `happy-dom` (already an installed devDependency, same established pattern as `scripts/test-dm-chat-reopen-scroll.ts` and siblings) instead of leaving it permanently skipped; also fixed a second, previously-undiscovered `shouldStabilizeComposerTouchMove` DOM-wiring bug in that same test (the button under test was never actually appended to the composer root it was tested against). All 12 calls uncommented in `main()`; full `npm run test:regressions` now passes clean (only the separate, already-known `testQaEnvironmentResetScript` SQL-formatting issue remains, unrelated and untouched).
- **Event Brands (2026-08-01):** promoter/both-role profile field replaces the old single "Event brand name" text field with a Music-Genres-style removable chip list (`ProfileEventBrandsField.tsx`) — tap **+ Add brand** for an inline single-line text input, Enter/blur commits, max **10 brands**, max **40 characters** each, trims + collapses internal whitespace, case-insensitive duplicate rejection, blank entries ignored, live `N / 10 brands` counter; read-only public profile shows the same chips (`PromoterProfileSections.tsx`) via a new shared `ProfileTagChipList.tsx` (also now used by `ProfileGenreTags.tsx` — no behaviour change there). **No schema change**: reuses the existing `public.users.promoter_brand_name` text column as a `|`-joined list (mirrors how `genre` already stores a delimited tag list in one column) — `parseStoredEventBrands`/`serializeEventBrands`/`addEventBrandTag` in `lib/user/profileFormUtils.ts`. An existing user's single brand name has no separator, so it parses as a one-item list automatically — zero migration, zero data loss, zero deploy-ordering risk (a column *rename* was considered and rejected specifically because `PROFILE_FIELDS` in `lib/user/currentUser.ts` has no missing-column fallback and is on the hot path of every authenticated page load via `OnboardingGuard`; reusing the existing column sidesteps that entirely). `resolveProfileIdentityPresentation`'s brand-name identity fallback now derives the *first* brand instead of the raw delimited string. DJ profile editing (`DjProfileSections.tsx`) untouched.
- **Event Brands — event creation (2026-08-01):** the promoter/both-role create-event form (`EventsPageClient.tsx`) gained an optional **Event brand** chip picker (`EventBrandSelectField.tsx`, reuses the same chip styling, single-select): 0 saved brands → field doesn't render, event creation unaffected; 1 saved brand → auto-preselected on opening the create flow (still changeable — deselect by tapping it again); 2+ → tap to choose one, none selected by default. Never required. Adds a genuinely new, optional `event_brand text` column (`scripts/setupEventBrands.sql`, **not yet run in production — Isaac needs to run this**) — unlike the profile side, there was no existing column to reuse, so this one *does* need a migration. Protected against the deploy-before-migration window the same way `crew_chat_started_at`/`history_hidden_at` already are: `lib/events/eventQueryFields.ts` gained the same missing-column-detect-and-retry-without machinery for `event_brand` on reads. Reads alone aren't enough for an *insert* — `mapEventInputToRow` (now exported from `lib/events.ts` for testability) only includes `event_brand` in the payload when a brand is actually selected *and* the column isn't already known to be missing; this specific check exists because `withEventFieldsFallback`'s retry only varies the `.select()` fields, not the insert payload, so an unconditional `event_brand` key would retry the identical failing insert forever — verified with a dedicated test (`testMapEventInputToRowEventBrandFallback`) exercising the missing-column path directly. Net effect: event creation is unaffected today (no brand can be selected until a promoter saves one, and even then the brand silently won't persist until the migration runs, without breaking creation). Event *editing* and the event detail page do not show/edit the brand — out of scope for this MVP pass, creation-only per the spec.
- **Event Brands — auto-preselect fix (2026-08-01):** live production verification (real authenticated session, real QA account with one saved brand) caught the 1-brand auto-preselect not actually applying — the chip rendered but never showed as selected. Root cause: it was implemented as a separate `useEffect` setting `form.eventBrand`, which raced against `openCreateFlow()` / the "From scratch" handler / `handleSelectPlan()` — all of which reset the *entire* form (including `eventBrand` back to `null`) and are declared later in the component, so in the same effect flush they ran after and clobbered the auto-select effect's own update. Fixed by removing the effect and computing the default (`defaultEventBrand`) inline at every place the form resets for a new event instead, so there's no separate update left for a later reset to undo; also covers the one reset path with no such call site at all (a calendar-origin bootstrap can open the form already-populated on the very first render, before `guardProfile` has loaded — falls back to unselected in that instant rather than getting it wrong). Verified live after the fix: chip now shows selected (`aria-pressed="true"`, primary-coloured border) on opening the create form, and remains deselectable by tapping it again.
- **Event Brand chips match Genre chips (2026-08-01):** UX polish pass — `ProfileTagChipList.tsx` now exports a shared `PROFILE_TAG_CHIP_BASE_CLASS` (border, background, rounding, `text-xs font-medium`) consumed by the read-only chip list itself *and* by both editable pickers (`ProfileGenrePicker.tsx`'s selected-tag chip, `ProfileEventBrandsField.tsx`'s removable brand chip) — Event Brand and Genre chips are now guaranteed byte-for-byte identical wherever they appear, not just visually similar independently-typed literals. Only the brand chip's horizontal padding differs (`pl-3 pr-2` instead of `px-3`), and only because it carries an inline remove ✕ button that Genre's edit-mode chip doesn't have — same height, border, and typography in every other respect. Confirmed (via the existing `PromoterProfileSections.tsx` early-return) that a profile with zero event brands already renders nothing, no empty card.
- **Event Brand chips overflowing on long/unbroken names — fixed (2026-08-01):** confirmed root cause: the existing 40-char limit (`MAX_EVENT_BRAND_NAME_LENGTH`) alone can't prevent overflow — a 40-character brand with no spaces has no natural line-break point, and none of the chip components had a `max-width`/truncation, so an otherwise-valid unbroken name could still force the chip (and the page) wider than the viewport instead of wrapping. Also found a *separate* latent gap while implementing: `parseStoredEventBrands`/`serializeEventBrands` (`lib/user/profileFormUtils.ts`) previously shared the SAME truncating helper as `addEventBrandTag`, meaning any pre-existing stored brand already over 40 chars (legacy data, or anything that reached storage another way) got silently and irreversibly shortened the next time the profile was merely *loaded or saved for an unrelated reason* — not just when the user actively edited that brand. Split into two paths: `normalizeNewEventBrandName` (still enforces the 40-char cap, used only by `addEventBrandTag` — the actual point of entry) vs. `dedupeEventBrandsPreservingLength` (trims/dedupes but never truncates, used by parse/serialize — so existing stored values, even oversized ones, pass through untouched). Rendering fix: `ProfileTagChipList.tsx` now exports `PROFILE_TAG_CHIP_MAX_WIDTH_CLASS = "max-w-[12rem]"`, paired with `min-w-0 truncate` on every chip that renders a saved/selected brand — the read-only list itself (also covers Genre tags, harmless since those are all short/curated), `ProfileGenrePicker`'s selected-tag chips (same plain-text shape), `ProfileEventBrandsField`'s editable brand chips (text wrapped in its own inner `min-w-0 truncate` span so only the text ellipsizes — the remove ✕ stays a separate `shrink-0` sibling, always fully visible/tappable), and `EventBrandSelectField.tsx` (event-creation selector, previously had its own untouched styling with the same overflow gap). 10-brand max, case-insensitive dedup, whitespace collapsing, and add/remove behaviour are all unchanged. Verified live (390px and desktop): short brand, exactly-40-char, a 60-char unbroken word, and 10 brands at once all render cleanly with zero horizontal overflow (`document.documentElement.scrollWidth <= window.innerWidth` confirmed at both widths); live-typing a >40-char name into the add-brand input still caps at exactly 40 via the existing `applyEventBrandNameInputLimit`, commits without erroring, and displays truncated with the ✕ intact.
- **DM image bubbles: shared sizing + balanced multi-image grid (2026-08-01):** two related polish fixes. (1) Single images: extracted the max-width/max-height literals from the 2026-07-31 fix (below) into `lib/dm/dmImageLayout.ts` (`DM_IMAGE_BUBBLE_MAX_WIDTH_CLASS`/`DM_IMAGE_BUBBLE_MAX_HEIGHT_CLASS`) so `DmMessageAttachment.tsx` and the multi-image grid share one literal instead of two independently-typed copies that could drift; behaviour unchanged (still `max-h-72 max-w-[min(100%,18rem)]`, aspect ratio always preserved, no crop). (2) Multi-image grid (`DmMessageAttachmentGroup.tsx`): replaced the old uniform `grid-cols-2`/`grid-cols-3` collage (which gave 3 images a cramped 3-up row and had no shape at all for 5+) with a proper balanced grid — 2 images: equal 2-column; 3: large-top/two-bottom (first cell spans both columns as a 2:1 rectangle, two equal squares below); 4: 2x2; 5+: same 2x2 shape, capped to 4 visible tiles with a `bg-black/60` **"+N"** overlay on the last tile (the standard pattern used by WhatsApp/Telegram/iMessage) — reused the codebase's existing dark-overlay convention rather than the sibling-label style `CrewChatAvatarStack.tsx` uses for a different context (avatar row, not a photo grid). Pure layout logic (`resolveImageGridCellClass`, `resolveVisibleGridImages`) now lives in `lib/dm/dmImageLayout.ts`, unit-tested directly (`scripts/test-regressions.mts` → `testDmImageGridLayout`) instead of only via source-regex assertions. Tapping any tile (including the overlaid one) still opens that image in a new tab — unchanged; upload and realtime paths untouched, only the render/grid-shape layer changed.
- **Event Brands — saved brands dropped on reopening Edit Profile — fixed (2026-08-01):** confirmed root cause and it was NOT in the chip UI or in `parseStoredEventBrands`/`serializeEventBrands` (both already correct and shared with the working public-profile display). It was a stale-cache bug in `lib/user/currentUser.ts`: `saveUserProfile()` writes the update to Supabase successfully but never called `invalidateCurrentUserProfileCache()` afterward, so the module-level `cachedProfile` (and its `localStorage` mirror, seeded back into memory on every page load via `seedProfileFromPersistentStorage()`) kept serving the pre-save snapshot indefinitely — `getCurrentUserProfile()` short-circuits and returns that cached object whenever one exists for the current user, and nothing ever invalidated it after a profile save. Since the promoter/both edit route (`app/profile/setup/page.tsx`) hydrates `EditProfileForm`'s `brandTags` from `getCurrentUserProfile()`, reopening Edit Profile could show a stale subset of brands (in the reported case, only the very first brand ever cached) — while the public profile page was unaffected because it fetches via the separate, uncached `getUserProfileById()`. Fix: `saveUserProfile()` now calls `invalidateCurrentUserProfileCache()` on success, mirroring the exact pattern `saveUserRole()` already used — forces a fresh Supabase fetch (and a fresh `localStorage` write) the next time any code path calls `getCurrentUserProfile()`. This is a general profile-cache fix, not Event-Brands-specific — every profile field was equally at risk of serving stale data after a save. Also added a `title={tag}` native tooltip to `ProfileTagChipList.tsx`'s read-only chip (shows the full brand/genre name on hover/long-press when truncated) for cleaner truncation, per the "does not present the truncation cleanly enough" report — the existing `max-w-[12rem]` + ellipsis truncation itself (commit `976c7c8`) was already correct and is unchanged. No legacy-field migration needed: there is no separate old single-brand column — `parseStoredEventBrands`'s existing "no separator = one-item list" parsing already covers a pre-existing single un-delimited value, confirmed via a dedicated hydration regression test. Regression tests added (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`): `testProfileCacheInvalidatedAfterSave` (source-regex guard against this exact regression recurring), `testEventBrandEditFormHydration` (full 10-brand array, legacy single-value, empty, and oversized-legacy hydration via `createProfileEditBaseline`), `testEventBrandSafeSave` (no-op save round-trips losslessly; removing one brand affects only that one). Verified live end-to-end against a real authenticated QA promoter account (production Supabase, local dev server): added 10 brands including a 40-char unbroken name → saved → left the page → reopened Edit Profile → all 10 present in original order (previously only 1 would have shown); saved again unchanged → still all 10; removed one → only that one gone, other 9 intact after reload; confirmed via `localStorage`'s `ftc-user-profile-local` that the cache itself now holds the fresh 9-brand value post-save; long-name chip confirmed via computed style to have `overflow:hidden`/`text-overflow:ellipsis`/`scrollWidth (280) > clientWidth (190)` (visible ellipsis) plus the new `title` tooltip; no horizontal overflow at 390px or desktop (`scrollWidth === clientWidth`). QA account's brand list restored to its original single entry after testing.
- **Event Brands — tap-to-reveal full name on public profile (2026-08-01):** the previous truncation fix (`976c7c8`) plus the `title` tooltip added in the cache-fix pass above only reveal a truncated chip's full name on mouse *hover* — there was no tap equivalent, so a mobile/touch user could never read a truncated Event Brand (or Genre tag) at all; the Edit Profile chip and public-profile chip were never actually two different implementations (both already used the shared `ProfileTagChipList`/`PROFILE_TAG_CHIP_*` constants), the real gap was purely "no touch-friendly reveal." Fixed by making `ProfileTagChipList.tsx`'s read-only chip a real `<button>` (was a plain `<span>`) that, on tap, checks whether its text is actually overflowing (`scrollWidth > clientWidth`) and — only if so — opens a small popover anchored directly beneath that specific chip showing its full, untruncated text; short/untruncated chips are inert on tap (no visual change), so this is a pure addition, not a redesign. Each chip is wrapped in its own `relative` positioning anchor (`<span className="relative inline-block">`) so the popover always anchors to the exact chip that was tapped, never a shared/central position. Dismissal reuses the exact outside-pointerdown + Escape-key pattern `CalendarMonthNav`'s month/year popover already established (`window` `mousedown`/`keydown` listeners, only attached while a popover is open); tapping a different chip closes the current popover and opens the new one in the same tap (mousedown closes the old one before the click handler evaluates the new one). Edge-aware alignment: computed once at open time via `getBoundingClientRect()` — centers under the chip by default, but flips to left- or right-anchored (`EDGE_ALIGN_THRESHOLD_PX = 96`) when the chip is within 96px of a viewport edge, so the popover can never push the page into horizontal scroll regardless of which chip in a wrapped row was tapped. Because the reveal lives in the shared `ProfileTagChipList` component (not duplicated per tag type), both the public profile's Event Brands (`PromoterProfileSections.tsx`) and Genre tags (`ProfileGenreTags.tsx`) get it automatically with one implementation. Edit Profile is completely untouched — `ProfileEventBrandsField.tsx`/`ProfileGenrePicker.tsx` only ever imported the shared className *constants* from `ProfileTagChipList.tsx`, never the component itself, confirmed via a zero-diff check on both files. Regression test added: `testProfileTagChipTapToReveal` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) — verifies the button conversion, the truncation gate, single-popover toggle-close, per-chip anchoring, edge-aware alignment, outside-tap/Escape dismissal, and that the editable Edit Profile fields remain untouched. Verified live end-to-end (real QA promoter account, 390px and desktop): 2 long unbroken brand names + 1 short brand + the pre-existing short brand all render on one line, truncated; tapping a long chip near the card's right edge opens the popover right-aligned (not centred, avoiding off-screen clip) with the full name; tapping a second long chip switches directly to it in one tap; tapping a short chip or anywhere outside closes any open popover; Escape closes it; `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` confirmed at both viewports (no horizontal overflow); re-opened Edit Profile afterward and confirmed add/remove/save still work exactly as before, then restored the QA account to its original single brand.
- **Event Brands — popover replaced with a native bottom sheet (2026-08-01):** superseded the tap-to-reveal popover above (`6a28961`) entirely per explicit direction, rather than patching it further. Goal: viewing long Event Brand names on another user's public profile should feel native on mobile and scale cleanly to the 10-brand max. New architecture: `ProfileTagChipList.tsx` is back to a fully plain, non-interactive chip list (no hooks, no popover state, no `<button>` — reverted to the pre-popover `<span title={tag}>` shape) and is now Genre-tags-only again (`ProfileGenreTags.tsx`, untouched). A new dedicated component, `EventBrandsChips.tsx`, is the only thing `PromoterProfileSections.tsx` renders for Event Brands on the public profile: the trigger row reuses the exact same shared `PROFILE_TAG_CHIP_BASE_CLASS`/`PROFILE_TAG_CHIP_MAX_WIDTH_CLASS` + truncate treatment (so the card's size/layout never changes — chips still truncate with an ellipsis by default), but tapping **any** chip (not just a truncated one, since the sheet is also a legitimate way to browse the full list) opens a bottom sheet: title "Event Brands", subtitle "`N` brand(s)" (correct singular/plural), every brand listed in full with `whitespace-normal break-words` (no truncation/ellipsis anywhere in the sheet), the tapped brand auto-scrolled into view (`scrollIntoView({ block: "center", behavior: "smooth" })`) and given a subtle `border-ftc-primary/40 bg-ftc-primary/10` highlight. Tapping a different chip while the sheet is already open only re-targets the active/highlighted brand — there is no close-then-reopen path (`openSheetFor` always just sets state, never toggles), so the sheet visibly stays put and only the highlight moves. Chrome/behaviour reuses established FTC sheet conventions throughout rather than inventing new ones: `useBodyScrollLock` (same hook as `ProfileGenrePicker`'s "Add genres" sheet), Escape-to-close via a `document` keydown listener, backdrop-tap-to-close (`fixed inset-0 bg-black/60`, inner panel `stopPropagation`s), `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal="true"`/`aria-labelledby`/`aria-describedby`, and the same border/radius/surface tokens (`rounded-t-2xl border-ftc-border-strong bg-ftc-bg-elevated`) every other FTC sheet uses. New pieces built for this (no existing equivalent in the codebase to reuse): a genuine slide-up-from-bottom CSS entrance animation (`@keyframes ftc-sheet-slide-up`, `translateY(100%)` → `0`, 220ms ease-out, with the same `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` → `animation: none` override every other FTC keyframe animation has — new, generically-named so a future bottom sheet could reuse it) added to `app/globals.css`; a minimal focus trap (`handlePanelKeyDown` cycles Tab/Shift+Tab within the panel's own focusable elements); and a swipe-down-to-dismiss gesture scoped *only* to the header/drag-handle area (`onTouchStart`/`onTouchMove`/`onTouchEnd`, live `translateY` drag-follow while the finger moves, closes past an 80px threshold or snaps back with a 200ms transition otherwise) — deliberately not attached to the scrollable list itself, so it can never fight normal list-scrolling. Scoping decisions, explicitly not built: no dedicated close (✕) button, matching the established precedent that removed one from `ProfilePhotoViewer.tsx` on the grounds that tap-outside + Escape already cover it; no browser-history/`popstate` interception for the hardware back button (no existing FTC dialog does this either — Escape is the fully-supported keyboard/OS dismiss path, and native back navigation closes the sheet anyway as a side effect of leaving the page, just not as a "close-without-navigating" special case). Edit Profile is untouched: `ProfileEventBrandsField.tsx`/`ProfileGenrePicker.tsx` never imported the previous popover's component (only the shared className constants), and don't import `EventBrandsChips` either — confirmed via a zero-diff check. Regression test rewritten: `testEventBrandsBottomSheet` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) replaces the now-obsolete `testProfileTagChipTapToReveal`, asserting the popover is fully gone, the sheet's chrome/dismiss/a11y/highlight/animation pieces are all present, and Edit Profile stays unaffected. Verified live end-to-end (real QA promoter account, production Supabase): 10 brands (mixed short + several 40-char unbroken long names) all truncate cleanly on the card; tapping a long chip opens the sheet with the correct "10 brands" subtitle, full untruncated text for every row, and the tapped one highlighted/in view; tapping a different chip (including a short, non-truncated one) while open kept the sheet open and only moved the highlight; backdrop tap and Escape both closed it; a simulated swipe-down past the threshold on the header closed it, a small swipe below the threshold snapped back without closing; body scroll was locked and focus landed on the panel while open; 2-brand and 1-brand cases showed the correct "2 brands"/"1 brand" subtitle text; verified at both 390px and 1280px with `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` (no horizontal overflow) at both; re-verified Edit Profile's add/remove/save flows are unaffected; QA account restored to its original single brand afterward. One dev-environment note (not a code issue): running `next build` and `next dev` back-to-back against the same `.next` directory corrupted Turbopack's persistent dev cache mid-session (`Internal Server Error`, RocksDB-style "Compaction failed"/"Unable to write SST file" in the server log) — resolved by `rm -rf .next` before restarting `next dev` fresh; worth remembering if a local dev server ever throws a bare "Internal Server Error" right after a production build.
- **Public profile bio: long words overflowed, didn't match Edit Profile — fixed (2026-08-01):** root cause: the Edit Profile bio textarea already wrapped unbroken long words safely (native `<textarea>` wrapping plus `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea`'s `overflow-x:hidden` backstop), but the public profile's `ProfileBioText.tsx` `<p>` only had `whitespace-pre-wrap` (preserves line breaks, wraps at normal word boundaries) with no `overflow-wrap` rule — the exact same gap the 2026-08-01 display-name layout-safety audit (above) already found and fixed on the heading/username lines in this same `ProfileHero.tsx` file, just never applied to the bio itself. Fix: added `break-words [overflow-wrap:anywhere]` to the bio `<p>`, matching that same precedent exactly. Also removed the pre-existing "More/Less" expand toggle (`useState`/`useLayoutEffect` overflow measurement + button) — out of scope for beta per spec ("no Show more feature") — the bio is now unconditionally `line-clamp-4` with a CSS ellipsis, no interactive state. The 150-character limit (`MAX_PROFILE_BIO_LENGTH`) and the 3-explicit-line typing cap (`MAX_PROFILE_BIO_LINES`, from the entry above) are both untouched — this was a render-only fix, nothing in the edit/save path changed. Regression test `testProfileBioTextRendering` added (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) asserting the wrap/clamp classes are present and the toggle state/button are gone. Verified live at 390px and 1280px with a bio combining an emoji, a blank line, a 68-character unbroken word, and a long URL: `scrollWidth === clientWidth` on both the bio element and the page (zero horizontal overflow), blank line preserved, long word wraps cleanly mid-word, clamps to exactly 4 lines with a trailing ellipsis, no layout jump elsewhere on the card. `npm run build` clean, full `npm run test:regressions` pass.
- **Bio editor grown to 5 lines + public-profile "See more/See less" restored (2026-08-01):** follow-up spec change on top of the entry above (beta scope changed — a Show-more feature is wanted after all, and the editor felt cramped at 3 lines). (1) Edit Profile bio textarea grew from 3 to ~5 visible lines via a new dedicated `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea-5` modifier class (`app/globals.css`) — deliberately a new class, not a change to the existing `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea-3`, since that shared class is also used unchanged by the booking rate-notes field (`ProposeBookingRateSheet.tsx`) and the withdrawal reason field (`WithdrawalReasonDetailsField.tsx`); widening `-3` directly would have silently grown both of those too. Still fixed-height with internal scroll (no indefinite auto-grow); 150-char limit and the 3-explicit-newline typing cap are both unchanged, since they live in the save/typing path, not the textarea's visible size. (2) `ProfileBioText.tsx`'s "More/Less" toggle — removed in the entry above — is restored as "See more"/"See less", collapsed to 4 lines by default, but now with a smooth animated height transition instead of an instant clamp toggle: adapted the same measured-height mechanism already shipped and proven in `BookingCardExpandableNotes.tsx` (collapsed vs. full height measured via `ResizeObserver` + a temporary `line-clamp-4` class toggle in a `useLayoutEffect`, animated via a wrapper `transition-[height] duration-200 ease-out`, `prefers-reduced-motion` respected) — kept self-contained in `ProfileBioText.tsx` rather than extracting a shared component, since `BookingCardExpandableNotes.tsx` is an unrelated DM booking-card feature out of scope for this change. The `[overflow-wrap:anywhere]` long-word-wrap fix from the entry above is preserved on top of the restored toggle. Updated `testProfileBioTextRendering` and `testProfileDisplayNameAndBioFieldUx` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) for the new expected behaviour. Verified live at 375px and 1280px: textarea measured `height: 138px` via computed style (5 lines); a bio combining an emoji, a blank line, a long unbroken word, and a URL, saved at exactly 150/150 via the existing counter, collapses to 4 lines with "See more" on mobile (fits without truncation at 1280px, so no toggle shown there — confirmed the `ResizeObserver` re-measures correctly per viewport); tapping "See more" smoothly expands to the full untruncated text with zero horizontal overflow (long word and URL both wrap cleanly) and the button switches to "See less"; tapping again collapses back cleanly. `npm run build` clean, full `npm run test:regressions` pass.
