QA account's bio restored to its original short value after testing.
- **Bio textarea clipped on older iPhones (root cause: `lh` unit unsupported pre-Safari 16.4) — fixed (2026-08-01):** reported as a per-device/per-account inconsistency (newer phone fine, older iPhone on the FTC QA DJ account clipped the bio textarea) but confirmed to be neither account- nor role-specific — `EditProfileForm.tsx`'s bio field and `ProfileHero.tsx`/`ProfileBioText.tsx` have zero `role ===` branching anywhere, so DJ, Planner, and Both all share the exact same textarea and renderer and were equally affected. Confirmed root cause: `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea-5` (added in the entry above) sized itself entirely with `calc(5lh + 1rem + 2px)`. The `lh` CSS unit shipped in Safari **16.4** — this project's own `browserslist` (`package.json`) commits to `ios_saf >= 14`, so every iOS Safari version from 14.0 through 16.3 (over two years of the project's own supported range) doesn't understand `lh` at all; on those browsers the whole `height`/`min-height`/`max-height` declaration is invalid CSS and gets dropped entirely, leaving the textarea at its unstyled browser-default height — clipping the bio. This is the same class of gap as the 2026-08-01 splash-screen fix (`static {}` class blocks, also a pre-16.4 Safari gap) — a recurring pattern worth remembering: any newer CSS/JS feature needs an explicit pre-16.4 fallback check given this project's stated `ios_saf >= 14` support floor. Fix: added a plain-pixel fallback (`height: 138px` etc., matching the calc() result at this field's actual font-size/line-height, measured live) declared *before* the `lh`-based declaration in the same rule — standard CSS graceful degradation: `lh`-capable browsers apply the later, more precise `calc()` as a normal cascade override, while browsers without `lh` support keep the fallback instead of falling through to an unstyled default. Scoped to the bio's own `-5` class only — the shared `-3` class (booking rate notes, withdrawal reason field) has the identical theoretical gap but was left untouched as out of scope for this task. Also finished the in-flight "See more"/"See less" polish from the same investigation: tightened the gap above the toggle (`mt-2` → `mt-1`, matching the identical value already used in `BookingCardExpandableNotes.tsx`) and reduced its visual weight (`font-semibold` → `font-medium`) so it reads as secondary to the bio text while staying clearly tappable. Investigated trailing blank lines per spec: already fully handled by two existing trims — `saveUserProfile()` trims the bio before persisting (`lib/user/currentUser.ts`), and `ProfileBioText` trims again at render time — no new trimming logic needed, added regression coverage locking in both instead. `testProfileBioTextRendering` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) extended to cover the pixel-fallback/cascade-order, the tightened spacing/weight, the trailing-blank-line trims, and the role-agnostic wiring. Verified live (Chromium, which supports `lh`) at 375px and 1280px: textarea computed to exactly 138px; a 5-visual-line bio (emoji included) showed all 5 lines fully readable with an internal scrollbar for overflow, none clipped or hidden behind the border; public profile toggle gap/weight confirmed via computed style (`marginTop: 4px`, `fontWeight: 500`, actual visual gap 5.5px); expand/collapse round-tripped cleanly with zero horizontal overflow at both viewports. **Not verified on an actual physical pre-16.4 iOS Safari device or simulator** (none available in this environment) — the fix is a standard, well-established CSS graceful-degradation pattern (unsupported declarations are dropped, cascade falls through to the last valid one), not something that requires live old-hardware confirmation to trust, but flagging this the same way the splash-screen fix's completion summary did. `npm run build` clean, full `npm run test:regressions` pass. Commit `2c9e948`.
- **Bio textarea still broken on older iPhone after the `lh`-fallback fix — actual root cause found (2026-08-01):** the `lh`-fallback fix above did not resolve it; reported symptom changed to something more specific — text rendering on a single line instead of wrapping, not just a short/clipped box. Investigated fresh rather than re-tuning the height again (explicitly instructed not to). Confirmed root cause: the bio's `textareaRows` HTML attribute was hardcoded to `1` (a one-line hint) while `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea-5`'s CSS forcibly overrode the rendered height to 5 lines with `!important` — a genuine structural mismatch between what the browser is told the textarea *is* (one line) and what CSS then *forces it to look like* (five lines). Relying on `!important` CSS to fight a contradictory `rows` value is inherently fragile and depends on the browser fully overriding native `rows`-driven sizing/layout heuristics, not just the visible box height — this is why the `lh`-fallback fix (which only addressed the height *value*) didn't touch the actual bug, which was the `rows`/CSS *disagreement* itself. Confirmed this is not account- or role-specific (same as the entry above — zero role branching in either component) and not really a "newer vs older iPhone" difference in kind, just in whether that particular WebKit build happened to fully paper over the mismatch. Fix: set `textareaRows={5}` so the native HTML attribute matches the intended size directly — `rows` is a foundational, universally-supported textarea attribute with no version threshold at all (unlike `lh`), and combined with `resize:none` + `overflow-y:auto` (already on the shared `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea` base class), a `<textarea rows="5">` natively stays exactly 5 rows tall with internal scroll for overflow — no CSS height override needed. Removed `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea-5` entirely (dead code) rather than patching it again — it was the source of the fragility, not a fix for it. Live-measured native size is `~127–140px` depending on exact font metrics (browser-computed, not a hardcoded guess like the old `138px` fallback was). The shared `-3` class (booking rate notes `ProposeBookingRateSheet.tsx`, withdrawal reason field) has the **identical** `rows={1}` vs CSS-height mismatch pattern and is a strong candidate for the same latent bug, but was left untouched as out of scope for this task — worth a dedicated follow-up. `scripts/test-regressions.mts` (`testProfileBioTextRendering`, `testProfileDisplayNameAndBioFieldUx`) updated to assert `textareaRows={5}` + the plain `ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea` class, confirm `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea-5` is fully gone, and confirm the base class has no `white-space`/`word-break`/`appearance` overrides that could affect wrapping. Verified live (Chromium) at 320px/375px/1280px: `rows=5` computes to a natively-sized box with no CSS height override at all, wraps a long unbroken word and multiple explicit lines cleanly, zero horizontal overflow, last line never clipped, 150-char cap and counter unaffected. **iOS Simulator was unavailable in this environment** ("stopped retrying after repeated crashes") — could not confirm on physical old/new Safari directly, but the fix trades a version-gated CSS feature for a basic, always-supported HTML attribute, which is a strictly stronger guarantee than the previous attempt had. `npm run build` clean, full `npm run test:regressions` pass. Commit `6de3208`.
- **Bio textarea outline clipped on older iPhone after the `rows` fix, plus booking-rate notes had the identical latent bug — both fixed (2026-08-01, commit `14cc4f1`):** two tightly related textarea fixes. **Part 1 — bio outline clipping:** with the box now natively sized (previous entry), the field's own border/focus outline was clipped at the top and bottom. Checked every candidate the task called out — pseudo-elements (none exist anywhere in `globals.css`, confirmed via grep), parent overflow (`.ftc-card` and `ProfileFormField.tsx`'s label wrapper have no `overflow`), box-shadow (`:focus`/`:focus-visible` explicitly set `box-shadow: none` — there is no ring to clip), absolute offsets (the character counter is a normal in-flow `<div>`, not absolutely positioned), fixed-height assumptions (ruled out — no CSS height override exists anymore, that was the previous bug), border-radius (standard, no unusual interaction). Root cause: the shared `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea` base class carried `-webkit-overflow-scrolling: touch`, which promotes the element onto its own WebKit compositing layer for momentum scrolling — on older iOS Safari, that layer's paint boundary clips the element's own border at the top/bottom edges. This explains why it surfaced *after* the `rows` fix rather than being a new regression: the clipping vulnerability was likely always structurally present, but only became visible once the box was correctly sized and the bright primary-colour focus border (vs. the muted default) made it obvious on an actual focus interaction. Modern WebKit (iOS 13+) applies native momentum scrolling to any `overflow: auto` region without this property, so removing it is a no-op on current devices and only drops a known clipping trigger on older ones — no Safari-version hack, no replacement property added. **Part 2 — booking-rate notes mismatch:** `ProposeBookingRateSheet.tsx`'s notes field had the exact same `rows`/CSS disagreement flagged as a follow-up candidate in the entry above — `textareaRows={1}` paired with `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea-3` forcing a 3-line height via `!important`. Fixed identically: `textareaRows={3}` (matching the field's own `MAX_NOTE_LINES = 3`) with the `-3` modifier dropped from this field's `textareaClassName` only. The shared `.ftc-fixed-scroll-textarea-3` CSS rule itself is untouched — `WithdrawalReasonDetailsField.tsx` still correctly depends on it with a matching `rows={3}`, so removing the rule would have regressed that field for nothing. `.ftc-modal-textarea` (a separate, confirmed-unused duplicate class with the same `-webkit-overflow-scrolling: touch`) was deliberately left alone as dead code, out of scope. Files changed: `app/globals.css`, `app/components/booking/ProposeBookingRateSheet.tsx`, `scripts/test-regressions.mts` (rewrote `testProposeBookingRateNotesTextareaGrowth`'s assertions for the new wiring, added an assertion that the base class has no `-webkit-overflow-scrolling`, and corrected a now-stale assertion in `testProfileBioTextRendering` that had claimed the booking notes field was intentionally left on the `-3` class). Verified live (Chromium) at 375×812: real click-focus (not `.focus()`, which didn't trigger the CSS focus state in this browser automation) shows the cyan focus border rendering completely on all four sides with intact corners, no clipping. **Booking-rate notes field could not be live-QA'd** — the active FTC QA DJ account had zero incoming gigs, so there was no reachable booking request to open `ProposeBookingRateSheet` with; accepted code-level verification as sufficient given the fix is structurally identical to the bio fix (same shared CSS class, same `BookingFormField`/`ProfileFormField` component pattern) and is covered by the rewritten regression assertions. **iOS Simulator remains unavailable in this environment** — no physical/simulated old-vs-new iOS Safari confirmation possible; relying on the well-documented nature of the WebKit compositing-layer clipping behaviour and the universally-supported `rows` attribute, same caveat as the entry above. `npm run build` clean, full `npm run test:regressions` pass.
- **Profile copy polish (2026-08-01):** three copy-only string edits in the profile edit flow, extending the app-wide no-trailing-period / no-ellipsis conventions already established by the 2026-07-31 passes. (1) `EditProfileDiscardDialog.tsx` — unsaved-changes body "Your changes will not be saved." → "Your changes will not be saved" (matches the no-trailing-period rule already applied to the cancel/delete confirmation dialogs). (2) `ProfileGenrePicker.tsx` — at-limit helper "You can select up to 8 genres." → "Select up to 8 genres" (drops both the hedging "You can" and the full stop; now reads as a direct instruction like the rest of the app's helper text). (3) `EditProfileForm.tsx` — username availability status "Checking…" → "Checking", which closes the one gap the 2026-07-31 loading-ellipsis standardisation explicitly left open: that pass was scoped to *button labels* only and deliberately skipped this string because it is a live-region status message beside the username field, so it kept its ellipsis while every loading button lost theirs. It now matches. Verified via repo-wide grep that each old string had exactly one occurrence and none remain (searched both the three-dot `...` and the real `…` character). Nothing else touched — the diff is three string literals; every className, the `atLimit` conditional, the debounce/sequence-guard logic, `usernameLiveTone`, and all surrounding markup are byte-identical. Adjacent-but-distinct copy deliberately left alone: `EditProfileForm.tsx`'s genre *field error* "Choose up to 8 genres" is a different string in a different component/context (validation error vs modal helper) and was already period-free, so it was out of scope for this pass. Clean `npm run build`; full `npm run test:regressions` pass (no test asserted on any of the three strings).
- **Event Brands — bottom sheet replaced with adaptive two-line chips (2026-08-01):** third and final iteration, and the one that removes the problem instead of decorating it. Both previous attempts (popover `6a28961`, bottom sheet `1cfa4fc`) existed only to make a *single-line-truncated* chip readable — they added an interaction to recover information the profile should simply have shown. This pass drops the interaction entirely and makes the chips themselves adaptive: short names stay on one line exactly as before, longer names wrap onto a second line, capped at two. Implementation is a single new shared constant in `ProfileTagChipList.tsx` — `PROFILE_TAG_CHIP_TEXT_CLASS = "line-clamp-2 [overflow-wrap:anywhere]"` — applied to an inner text span (the clamp needs `display:-webkit-box`, which would fight the chip's own `inline-flex items-center`, so it can't go on the chip itself). Two details are load-bearing: (1) `-webkit-line-clamp` renders an ellipsis **only** when the text genuinely overflows its two lines, which is what satisfies "only show an ellipsis if the name still exceeds 2 lines" without any JS measurement; (2) `overflow-wrap: anywhere` — **not** `break-word` — because only `anywhere` also shrinks the element's *min-content* width, which is precisely what lets a single unbroken 40-character brand wrap inside the `max-w-[12rem]` cap. `break-word` would leave min-content at the full unbroken word width and reintroduce the original horizontal-overflow bug. Both utilities already had precedent in the codebase (`line-clamp-3` in `BookingCardExpandableNotes`, `[overflow-wrap:anywhere]` in `DmBookingCardLayout`), so nothing new was invented. Measured empirically: at 12rem two lines hold ~60 characters versus the 40-character `MAX_EVENT_BRAND_NAME_LENGTH`, so **no valid brand ever clamps** — every brand is fully readable on the profile itself, which is the precondition the spec set for deleting the tap interaction. Consequently `EventBrandsChips.tsx` is deleted outright (−231 lines), `PromoterProfileSections.tsx` renders the shared `ProfileTagChipList` again (one implementation for Event Brands and Genre tags, not two), and the `ftc-sheet-slide-up` / `.ftc-event-brands-sheet-panel` CSS added for the sheet is removed from `globals.css` as dead code. Net −266 lines. Chip styling is byte-identical (same `PROFILE_TAG_CHIP_BASE_CLASS` colours/border/`rounded-full`/typography, same `gap-2` wrap spacing, same `max-w-[12rem]`); the only visual change is that a long chip is now two lines tall. Edit Profile is deliberately NOT changed — `ProfileEventBrandsField`/`ProfileGenrePicker` keep their strict single-line `min-w-0 truncate` so a row of chips with remove buttons keeps a uniform editing height; the new regression test asserts that divergence in **both** directions so a future "make them consistent" change has to be deliberate. Genre tags are provably unaffected: the longest of the 50 `PROFILE_GENRE_OPTIONS` is 17 characters and a measured 23-character string still renders on one unclamped line. Regression test `testEventBrandsBottomSheet` replaced by `testPublicProfileEventBrandsAdaptiveChips`, which asserts the sheet component and its CSS are gone, the chip has no button/state/tooltip/`title` left, the clamp+wrap constant is present, `truncate` is absent, and no height is pinned; `testEventBrandChipOverflowSafe` updated for the read-only chip's new shape (its edit-surface assertions are unchanged). Verified live (real QA promoter account, production Supabase) at **390px and 1280px** with **1, 5 and 10** brands: short names 1 line, every 39–40-character unbroken name exactly 2 lines with `clamped: false` (no ellipsis anywhere), `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` at both widths (no horizontal scrolling), no fixed card height so the card grows naturally, and clicking all 10 chips opened nothing (0 dialogs, 0 tooltips, 0 buttons, 0 `title` attributes, body scroll never locked). Edit Profile re-checked at 10/10: chips still `white-space: nowrap` + `text-overflow: ellipsis` with `line-clamp: none`, remove works, counter updates, "+ Add brand" reappears under the limit. QA account restored to its original single brand.
- **DM composer selected-photo strip polish (2026-08-01):** completes the DM media polish pass above. Removed the "Clear all" button (`DmComposer.tsx`) — individual photos are already removable via each thumbnail's × button, so a bulk-clear affordance was redundant; the underlying `clearPendingAttachments` handler is untouched and still fires on successful send and on conversation switch, only its wiring into this one UI button (and the now-unused `onClearPendingPhoto` prop, removed from `DmComposer` and its call site in `app/dm/[conversationId]/page.tsx`) is gone. Thumbnails resized from `h-16 w-16` (64px) to `h-[4.5rem] w-[4.5rem]` (72px, +12.5%) for an easier preview — every thumbnail already shared one literal size/radius/spacing/selected-ring class (`.dm-composer-pending-photo-selected`, a `box-shadow` ring, applied uniformly since every staged photo is inherently "selected") so this was a single-point size change, not a per-item fix. The × button's corner offset was nudged from `right-0.5 top-0.5` to `right-1 top-1` to keep it proportional on the larger thumbnail; its size and every other property are unchanged and were already identical on every item. Horizontal scroll (`overflow-x-auto`), the `DM_MAX_PHOTOS_PER_MESSAGE` cap, and the file-input/upload/validation path are all untouched. `test-regressions.mts` updated: asserts `onClearPendingPhoto`/`Clear all` are both absent and the new thumbnail size string is present.
- **DM composer attachment tray — final polish before beta (2026-08-01):** two visual-only tweaks, no behaviour change. (1) Selected-thumbnail ring: `.dm-composer-pending-photo-selected`'s `box-shadow` went from a solid 2px `--ftc-color-primary` ring to a 1px `--ftc-color-primary-border` ring (the same 28%-opacity subtle-emphasis token already used elsewhere, e.g. `PlannerFieldError`'s focus ring and `BookingTimeWheelPicker`) — much closer to iMessage/Instagram's low-emphasis selection style, image stays the visual focus. (2) Remove (×) button (`DmComposer.tsx`): background went from `bg-ftc-bg/90` (90% opaque, could visually merge with a dark photo showing through the 10% gap) to fully opaque `bg-ftc-bg`, and the glyph from the dim `text-ftc-text-secondary` to the brighter `text-ftc-text` token — reads clearly over light or dark photos alike. Also dropped the now-redundant `hover:text-ftc-text` (the default already equals it, and hover never fired on touch anyway, so the dim state was in effect permanent on mobile). Same size (`h-5 w-5`), same position (`right-1 top-1`), same click/disabled behaviour — only fill/text-colour tokens changed. Verified against light, dark, mid-tone, screenshot-style, and synthetic "camera" test images via a temporary debug harness (not committed) — contrast holds uniformly across all of them since the fix isn't image-dependent (fixed colour tokens, not derived from the photo).
- **DM composer attachment tray — density pass (2026-08-01):** completes the tray polish above with a sizing reduction, no behaviour change. Thumbnails: `4.5rem` → `3.75rem` (72px → 60px, ~17% shorter), still perfectly square and consistent regardless of source (`object-cover` on a fixed box already made this size-invariant to a photo's own resolution/aspect ratio — portrait, landscape, screenshot, camera, camera roll all render identically). Remove (×) button: `h-5 w-5` → `h-[1.125rem] w-[1.125rem]` (20px → 18px, ~10% smaller, reusing the same `1.125rem` arbitrary value the send icon already uses at mobile width) and nudged from `right-1 top-1` to `right-0.5 top-0.5` to sit tighter in the corner of the now-smaller tile — mirrors the inverse of the previous pass's `right-0.5`→`right-1` nudge, which was sized for the larger 72px thumbnail. Upload, ordering, multi-select, horizontal scroll, send, and realtime paths untouched. Verified via a temporary debug harness (not committed): 1/2/4/10 (max) images, portrait/landscape/screenshot/camera/camera-roll all render as identical 60px tiles, remove-one and remove-all-individually both collapse the tray cleanly with no leftover gap, and send resets the composer to its normal (no-tray) height with no layout shift.
- **DM multi-image messages: full-group lightbox + fixed sender-side ownership alignment (2026-08-01):** two bug fixes on top of the grid-shape/composer polish passes above, found and reproduced live (uploaded 5 real test photos into a QA conversation) before fixing. (1) **Ownership alignment**: the multi-image grid (`DmMessageAttachmentGroup.tsx`) previously sized itself with `DM_IMAGE_BUBBLE_MAX_WIDTH_CLASS` (a *max*-width only). CSS Grid with `aspect-square`/`aspect-[2/1]` cells and `w-full` images has no intrinsic content width for the browser to shrink-wrap around, so instead of collapsing to a real footprint the way a text bubble or single image does, the grid rendered near the row's full available width regardless of sender — confirmed live: an outgoing 5-photo grid rendered flush to the left edge of the screen while the plain text bubble directly below it (same sender, same message list) correctly hugged the right edge. Fixed by giving the grid an explicit *width* instead — new `DM_IMAGE_BUBBLE_GRID_WIDTH_CLASS = "w-[min(100%,18rem)]"` (`lib/dm/dmImageLayout.ts`) — so it has a real, fixed footprint the existing `resolveMessageGroupLiClass`/flex alignment chain (already correct and unchanged, same one text bubbles use) can right/left-align exactly like every other message shape. No sender label added — alignment alone now correctly signals ownership, matching the requirement. Single-image messages are untouched (still `DM_IMAGE_BUBBLE_MAX_WIDTH_CLASS`, still intrinsically-sized, already aligned correctly). (2) **Full-group image viewer**: tapping any tile in a multi-image message previously called `window.open` on just that one image, in a new tab, with no relation to the rest of the message. New `DmImageLightbox.tsx` — an in-app modal gallery opened from `DmMessageAttachmentGroup`'s own state (`lightboxIndex`) — now opens on the tapped image with every image in the message (in upload order, `created_at` ascending from the existing query) swipeable left/right, including images hidden behind the "+N" tile (tapping "+N" opens at `visibleImages.length`, the first hidden image, not the last visible one it's drawn over). Supports keyboard arrows/Escape, swipe-down-to-dismiss, double-tap-to-zoom, and real two-finger pinch-zoom (tracked via raw `touchstart`/`touchmove` distance ratio, since pinch has no built-in React/CSS primitive) with pan-while-zoomed. Single-image messages are explicitly untouched — `DmMessageAttachment.tsx` still uses the original `window.open` new-tab viewer, per the requirement to preserve single-photo behaviour exactly. Upload flow, realtime, reactions, avatars, and timestamps are all unaffected — the fix is scoped entirely to the grid's own width and its tap handler. Regression test: `testDmImageGroupFullViewerAndOwnershipAlignment` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`); existing `testDmMultiPhotoSend` updated for the renamed width constant. Verified live in production after deploy at 390px (mobile): uploaded 5 real test photos, confirmed the grid now hugs the right edge like the text bubble above it (was previously edge-to-edge), tapped a grid tile → lightbox opens on that exact image with the correct "i / 5" count, `ArrowRight`/`ArrowLeft` navigate and `Escape` dismisses, tapping the "+1" overlay tile opens image 5/5 (the first hidden one, not the last visible one) — all as intended. **Desktop (~1280px) not verified**: this environment's browser window is capped by host screen size to ~606px wide (`resize_window` to 1280px silently clamps down; a request past that hard-fails with "Bounds must be at least 50% within visible screen space"), which sits just under Tailwind's `sm:` (640px) breakpoint — so the desktop-only lightbox prev/next chevron buttons (`hidden sm:flex`) were never actually rendered in this environment and could not be visually confirmed. Everything else in both fixes (the grid's width, the alignment chain, keyboard/swipe/pinch navigation) is breakpoint-independent and already visually confirmed working at the widest reachable width (606px, no horizontal overflow) — only the desktop chevron buttons specifically are unverified. Isaac: please confirm those show and work on an actual ≥1280px screen before/soon after beta.
- **DM 5+ image messages: Instagram-style Gallery Overview before the lightbox (2026-08-01):** builds on the full-group lightbox above. Previously tapping ANY multi-image message (2 photos or 20) jumped straight into the single-image lightbox on the tapped photo — for large groups this loses context, you can't see what else is in the message before committing to one photo. New behaviour, gated purely by count: 1 image → unchanged (`DmMessageAttachmentView`'s own new-tab viewer); 2-4 images → unchanged (tap opens `DmImageLightbox` directly on the tapped photo); **5+ images → tapping any tile (including the "+N" overlay tile) now opens a new `DmImageGalleryOverview.tsx` first** — fullscreen dark overlay (same `bg-black/90` dim as the lightbox), close (×) top-left, "N Photos" count top-center, every image in the message (upload order, never re-sorted) in a 3-column `object-cover` square grid, vertically scrollable only (no horizontal scroll) for 9+ photos. Tapping a thumbnail opens the existing lightbox on that exact photo; closing the lightbox returns to the overview (not the chat); closing the overview returns to the chat. State lives in `DmMessageAttachmentGroup` as a small union (`{mode:"closed"|"overview"|"lightbox", index?}`) rather than the old standalone `lightboxIndex` — `openFromGrid` picks overview vs. lightbox based on a new `DM_GALLERY_OVERVIEW_MIN_IMAGES` constant (`lib/dm/dmImageLayout.ts`, currently 5, one more than the existing "+N" overlay's own 4-image cap so the two thresholds can't drift apart silently). The lightbox itself, its pinch/double-tap zoom, swipe navigation, and swipe-down dismiss are all byte-for-byte unchanged — only its escape-key/body-scroll-lock dismiss effect was extracted into a new shared hook (`lib/dm/useDmMediaViewerDismiss.ts`) so the overview could reuse it instead of a third copy-pasted implementation (the same pattern already existed twice — this lightbox and the unrelated `ProfilePhotoViewer.tsx`, which was left untouched as out-of-scope). Upload flow, reactions, timestamps, avatars, and the "+N" overlay's own rendering are all unaffected. Regression test: `testDmImageGalleryOverviewForLargeGroups` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`); existing `testDmImageGroupFullViewerAndOwnershipAlignment` updated for the renamed `lightboxIndex` → `view.index` state shape. Verified live in production after deploy: uploaded a fresh 2-image test message alongside the existing 5-image one from the prior fix's testing — the 5-image message now opens "5 Photos" overview on tap (previously would have opened the lightbox directly), tapping the 3rd and 5th (last, previously-hidden-behind-"+1") thumbnails opened the lightbox at the correct "3 / 5" and "5 / 5" positions, tapping the "+1" overlay tile also opened the overview (not a specific image), Escape from the lightbox returned to the overview, a second Escape from the overview returned to the chat; the new 2-image message opened the lightbox directly ("1 / 2", no overview step) and Escape closed straight to the chat — confirming the 2-4 path is genuinely unchanged. Checked browser console before/after deploy: the only error present is a pre-existing, unrelated React hydration warning (minified error #418) that reproduces on a bare page load with no interaction and was present on both the pre- and post-deploy bundle — not caused by this change. **Desktop (~1280px) not verified, same as the prior lightbox entry**: this environment's browser window is hard-capped by host screen size to ~606px regardless of the requested width (confirmed again this session — a 390px request also silently settled at 606px), which sits just under Tailwind's `sm:` (640px) breakpoint, so no `sm:`-gated styling could be visually exercised. Nothing in this fix adds a new `sm:`-gated class (the overview grid is unconditionally `grid-cols-3` at every width), so the only carried-over unverified surface is the lightbox's own desktop chevron buttons noted in the prior entry — no new desktop-only surface was added by this change. Isaac: please confirm the overview grid and lightbox chevrons together on an actual ≥1280px screen.
- **DM media viewers: fixed non-responsive lightbox close button + unified close UI (2026-08-01):** bug fix on the full-group lightbox and Gallery Overview above. Reported symptom: the lightbox's top-right "×" didn't always close it. **Root cause (confirmed by live reproduction before fixing — clicked the "×" on a real 2-image message, nothing happened):** the header bar (count + close button) and the full-screen touch-handling image layer (`DmImageLightbox.tsx`'s `onTouchStart`/`onTouchMove`/`onTouchEnd` div) were both `position: absolute` siblings with no `z-index`. Per CSS's painting order for `z-index:auto` positioned siblings, later DOM order wins — the touch layer (`absolute inset-0`, default `pointer-events: auto`, rendered *after* the header) painted on top of the header everywhere the two overlapped, including the header's own screen region. The "×" was still visibly drawn (the touch layer is transparent except where the actual `<img>` pixels are) but was never the actual hit-tested element outside the exact rendered bounds of that message's image — so whether a click "worked" depended entirely on that image's aspect ratio/size relative to the button's position, matching the reported "not always" behaviour exactly. The lightbox's prev/next chevrons didn't have this bug (they're declared *after* the touch layer in the JSX, so they already painted on top correctly) — only the header, declared *before* it, was affected. **Fix:** gave the header explicit `z-10` (`inset-x-0 top-0 z-10`) so it deterministically stacks above the touch layer regardless of DOM order — not a bigger hit target or a duplicate handler elsewhere, an actual fix to the broken stacking. **Consistency:** extracted `DmMediaViewerCloseButton.tsx`, one shared component (44×44px hit area — `h-11 w-11`, meets the 44×44pt minimum; same icon, spacing, `bg-black/40` pill styling) now rendered by both `DmImageLightbox` and `DmImageGalleryOverview`, so the two can't independently drift again. `DmImageGalleryOverview`'s close button moved from top-left to top-right to match the lightbox exactly (removed the now-unneeded invisible spacer that previously kept its count visually centered — with only 2 header children now, `justify-between` alone puts the label left / close right on both screens identically). Both continue to use the shared `useDmMediaViewerDismiss` hook (Escape + body-scroll-lock) — dismissal behaviour was already identical and is unchanged. Preserved untouched: swipe between images, pinch/double-tap zoom, gallery overview, lightbox→overview→chat return routing, existing animations, existing image loading. Regression test: `testDmMediaViewerCloseButtonConsistentAndClickable` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) — asserts the shared component is used by both screens (not two inline copies), the 44×44 class, both headers lay out as `label` then `DmMediaViewerCloseButton` under `justify-between` (top-right on both), and the lightbox header's `z-10`. Verified live in production after deploy: reproduced the exact failure on the exact message that failed before the fix (2-image message, landscape photos) — "×" click closed it immediately, repeated twice more successfully; 5-image message → Gallery Overview now shows "5 Photos" with close top-right → tapped a thumbnail → lightbox opened → "×" returned to the overview (not chat) → overview's "×" (now top-right) returned to chat; "+1" overlay tile still opens the overview. Console checked before/after: only the same pre-existing, unrelated hydration warning (#418) present across every recent deploy, not caused by this change. **Desktop (~1280px) not verified, same known environment gap as the two prior entries**: this environment's browser window is hard-capped to ~606px regardless of requested size (confirmed again), below the `sm:` 640px breakpoint — the lightbox's own desktop chevrons remain the one unverified surface, unchanged by this fix. Isaac: please confirm the close button (and the carried-over chevrons) on an actual ≥1280px screen.
- **Music genres sheet jumped vertically on the 2nd search character — fixed (2026-08-01):** reported on iPhone Safari: open Add genres → focus search → type one character (fine) → type a second and the whole sheet/search input lurches down. **Root cause (measured live at 390×844 before writing any fix, not inferred):** the sheet (`ProfileGenrePicker.tsx`) is bottom-anchored (`fixed inset-0 flex items-end`) and was sized with `max-h-[88dvh]` and **no definite height**, so its height tracked its own content. While the filtered list overflowed the cap the sheet stayed pinned at the cap and the top edge was stable; the first keystroke that narrowed the list enough to fit *under* the cap made the sheet stop being clamped and shrink to fit content — and because it is anchored to the bottom, every pixel of that shrinkage came off the **top**, dragging the header and search input downward. Instrumented `getBoundingClientRect()` across keystrokes: empty (50 rows) and `"d"` (22 rows) both held `sheetTop 84 / height 614 / inputTop 161` with `height === max-height` (clamped); `"de"` (2 rows) dropped to `sheetTop 376 / height 322 / inputTop 453` — **the search input moved 292px down on the second character**. **Why the second character specifically:** nothing special about the count itself — it is simply the first keystroke that narrows 50 genres enough for total content height to cross below the 88dvh cap. With this list that crossover lands on character 2 for essentially every query (`d`→22 rows, `de`→2; `t`→21, `te`→13; `h`→26, `ho`→11), which is why it reproduced so consistently. Ruled out by inspection/measurement: no component remount (list is `key={genre}`, input is never keyed or conditionally rendered, `filteredGenres` is memoised), no `scrollIntoView`, no `visualViewport` handling, no focus management, and no vertical centring — the sheet is `items-end`, not `items-center`. **Fix:** new `.ftc-filter-sheet-panel` class (`app/globals.css`) giving the sheet a **definite** height so content can no longer drive it; the existing `min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto` list then absorbs every result-count change internally. Written as a `height: 88vh` base upgraded to `88dvh` inside `@supports (height: 1dvh)` — **not** two plain back-to-back `height` declarations, because the CSS minifier collapses the duplicate and silently deletes the `vh` fallback (caught by grepping the built chunk: the first attempt shipped `height:88dvh` only, which would have left iOS Safari < 15.4 — inside this project's `ios_saf >= 14` browserslist range — with no valid height and therefore still broken). Also wired the existing shared `useBodyScrollLock` hook: the background page genuinely did scroll behind the open sheet (measured `scrollY` 705→994 with the modal open), which the required behaviour explicitly forbids. No fixed pixel heights, no timeouts, no `scrollIntoView`, no global overflow hacks, no modal redesign. Regression test: `testGenreSheetHeightDoesNotTrackFilteredContent` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) — asserts the bug's preconditions still exist (long list + a 2-char query that collapses it), that the content-height shape (`max-h-[*dvh]`) is gone, that `min-h-0`/`flex-1`/`overscroll-contain` and the pinned Done button survive, that the scroll lock is wired, and that `88dvh` appears **only** inside `@supports` so the `vh` fallback cannot be minified away. Both failure modes mutation-tested (reverting the class, and collapsing the `@supports` back into a duplicate declaration) — each correctly fails the test. **Known, deliberately out of scope:** `.ftc-input` is `font-size: 0.875rem` (14px), and iOS Safari auto-zooms any focused input below 16px. That is a separate pre-existing issue affecting every input app-wide (not the reported 2nd-character jump, which happens after focus), so it was left alone rather than changed globally under this task — Isaac may want it as its own task.
- **DM image lightbox: replaced single-image transform with a real paged track — fixed floating-card swipe bug (2026-08-01):** reported symptom: swiping between images in the fullscreen viewer moved the current photo away like a floating card, exposing black backdrop, then the next photo popped in via a crossfade instead of sliding in — not a native paged gallery feel. **Root cause:** `DmImageLightbox.tsx` mounted exactly **one** `<img>`, keyed by the current image's own url, and applied the live swipe delta directly to that single element's `transform`. There was no second image anywhere in the DOM to represent the adjacent page, so dragging the current image away exposed the plain black backdrop behind it; on release, changing `index` unmounted that `<img>` (different `key`) and mounted a brand-new one, which cross-faded in via `opacity` from the centre rather than sliding in from the edge. **Fix:** every image in the message now mounts once as a persistent slide inside a single flex "track" (`images.map`, keyed by each image's own url — never a single swapped element); the whole track translates by `calc(${-index * (100/N)}% + ${dragOffsetPx}px)` during paging, so the next/previous slide is already sitting in the adjacent position before a swipe even starts. Verified live via synthetic `TouchEvent` dispatch (deterministic pixel-level swipe simulation, not just eyeballing): all 5 images stay mounted for the lifetime of the viewer (`imgCount` never changes while paging — literally zero reloads), the track transform moves 1:1 with the finger during drag (`transition:none`) and snaps with a real 300ms easing on release, and the counter/track update in lockstep at every step (`1/5`→`2/5`→...→`5/5`, confirmed pixel-exact `-20%`/`-40%`/etc. per index). **Zoom/pan vs. paging separation (requirement 4):** pan/zoom transform lives only on the *current* slide's own `<img>`, entirely separate from the track's transform — verified a single-finger drag while zoomed (`scale=4`) pans the image (`translate(60px,30px) scale(4)`) while the track transform stays completely untouched at its resting position; pinching back down to exactly `scale=1` now also clears any leftover pan offset (a real, necessary fix — without it, un-zooming left a stale image position that "restored" swiping into a visually-misplaced state) so paging is genuinely restored, not just re-enabled underneath a wrong offset. **Rubber-band (requirement 5):** first/last image use the same bounded damping formula as iOS `UIScrollView` (`RUBBER_BAND_RESISTANCE = 0.55`) — measured a raw 300px drag past the last image visually travel only ~130px and snap cleanly back to the same index on release (no navigation past bounds, `goToIndex`'s existing clamp is the second line of defence). Sub-threshold swipes (<50px) correctly snap back without changing index. Desktop: keyboard arrows and the `hidden sm:flex` prev/next buttons are unchanged call sites (`goToIndex`) and now animate via the same track transition automatically — confirmed both still work (arrow key advanced 1/5→2/5; "Next photo" button click advanced 2/5→3/5). Preserved untouched: `useDmMediaViewerDismiss` (Escape/scroll-lock), the header/counter/close-button chrome from the prior fix, `DmMessageAttachmentGroup`'s view-state routing (lightbox↔overview↔chat), image `object-contain` sizing (`max-h-[90vh] max-w-[92vw]`, so mixed portrait/landscape/aspect-ratio images render exactly as before — confirmed with a genuinely wide landscape photo mixed into a 5-image test set, no layout distortion). Regression test: `testDmImageLightboxUsesPagedTrackNotFloatingCard` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) — asserts the old single-`<img>`-keyed-by-`currentImage.url` shape is gone, every image renders as its own persistent slide, the track spans/translates by index, pan and page-drag are separate transform paths, the pinch-back-to-1 pan reset exists, drag disables transition while dragging, and `goToIndex` is unchanged; mutation-tested (reverting to a fixed non-translating track with a reintroduced `currentImage` variable) and confirmed it fails correctly. **Testing note — double-tap zoom:** this exact toggle logic (`now - lastTapRef.current < 280` + functional `setScale` toggle) is byte-identical to the pre-existing implementation (only a variable rename, `translate`→`panOffset`) — not modified by this fix. Live verification of this *specific* sub-280ms gesture via synthetic touch dispatch proved unreliable in this environment for two confirmed, unrelated reasons: a busy-wait "settle" delay was found to block React's own commit from completing before the DOM was read back (so some "failures" were stale reads of an already-correct value), and `setTimeout`-based delays were separately confirmed throttled by the backgrounded automation tab (a requested 30ms gap measured up to ~2000ms via an attached listener). With a genuinely non-blocking, non-throttled gap (`await` + real elapsed time, confirmed via direct timestamp logging), double-tap correctly toggled in both directions on a fresh, never-zoomed lightbox instance. Not a code regression — recommend a quick manual double-tap check on a real device as a sanity confirmation, not because there's evidence it's broken. Isaac: please also confirm the prev/next chevrons and rubber-band feel on an actual ≥1280px / real-touch-device screen — this environment's browser window remains hard-capped to ~606px (same known limitation as the two prior lightbox entries).
- **DM image viewer: locked paging to horizontal-only, removed vertical drift (2026-08-01):** reported symptom: while paging through sent photos, the active photo could also be dragged vertically — no vertical lock during a horizontal swipe. **Root cause:** `handleTouchMove`'s paging branch set `dragOffset.y` to 40% of the raw vertical finger delta (`setDragOffset({ x: pageDeltaX, y: rawDeltaY * 0.4 })`), and that value was added directly into the *current slide's own* `transform` (`translate(panOffset.x, panOffset.y + dragOffset.y)`) — so any vertical component of a swipe, however small or accidental, visibly moved the photo up/down at the same time it paged left/right. There was no direction decision at all: `handleTouchMove` entered "swipe" mode the instant total movement (`Math.hypot(dx, dy)`) crossed 10px, regardless of which axis actually dominated; direction (page vs. the old swipe-down-to-dismiss) was only resolved later, at `touchend`, by which point the drift had already been visible on screen for the entire gesture. **Fix:** `dragOffset` no longer has a `y` field at all — nothing left to apply even by mistake — and gesture direction is decided **once**, the moment raw movement crosses `GESTURE_LOCK_THRESHOLD_PX` (10px, same threshold as before, now named), by comparing `|dx|` vs `|dy|` at that exact instant: `drag.mode = Math.abs(rawDeltaX) >= Math.abs(rawDeltaY) ? "swipe" : "vertical"`. A gesture locked `"swipe"` never touches the vertical axis again for its duration (small accidental diagonal wobble mid-drag can't retroactively move the image); a gesture locked `"vertical"` never moves the track or the image and never re-evaluates mid-drag (so it can't suddenly start paging either). Zoomed panning (`scale > 1`, its own `"pan"` mode chosen up front at touchstart from the current scale) is completely untouched — still free horizontal + vertical movement, unaffected by the new lock, exactly per requirement 2. **Dismissal:** the old swipe-down-to-dismiss branch (`deltaY > SWIPE_DISMISS_THRESHOLD_PX && ...` → `requestClose()`) is removed along with its now-dead `SWIPE_DISMISS_THRESHOLD_PX` constant — direction locking makes it structurally unreachable in practice (a gesture that reaches the "swipe" branch was, by construction, dominantly horizontal, so a meaningful vertical delta can no longer coexist with it), and the task explicitly calls for the close button to be the sole dismissal control; verified live that a 250px pure-vertical drag produces zero effect and the viewer stays open. Preserved unchanged: image counter, `goToIndex`/prev-next buttons/arrow keys, pinch zoom, double-tap zoom, rubber-band edges (unchanged formula, now confirmed working together with the vertical lock at both boundaries), `DmMessageAttachmentGroup`'s gallery-overview return flow, image order, desktop controls. Verified live via synthetic `TouchEvent` dispatch (not just eyeballing) on both a 2-image and a 5-image message: slow horizontal drag with vertical wobble (dy up to 8px) → paged correctly, current-image transform stayed `translate(0px, 0px)` at every intermediate sample; fast 2-sample flick → paged correctly, zero drift; pure vertical 250px drag → zero effect, viewer stayed open; horizontally-dominant diagonal drag (220×90px) → paged correctly, zero drift; zoomed pan (scale 4) → free two-axis pan (`translate(70px, 70px)`), track transform stayed at rest the whole time; zoom back to 1× then swipe → paging genuinely restored, zero drift; first/last-image boundaries with vertical noise mixed into the drag → rubber-band resisted correctly (a 250px raw overdrag visually travelled ~112px) *and* stayed vertically locked simultaneously, snapped back cleanly, no navigation past bounds. Console checked before/after: only the same pre-existing, unrelated hydration warning (#418) present across every recent deploy. Regression test: `testDmImageLightboxLocksGestureDirectionVerticalDoesNotDrift` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) — asserts `dragOffset` has no `y` field anywhere in source, the current-image transform formula only carries `panOffset`, the three-way direction lock and its exact decision line exist, both the vertical-lock early-return and the dead dismiss branch's removal, and that pan mode/horizontal nav threshold are untouched; mutation-tested two ways (reintroducing the `y: rawDeltaY * 0.4` term, and reverting to a magnitude-only "always swipe" threshold with no direction comparison) — both correctly fail. **iOS Safari (older vs. current) not device-tested** — no real iOS device available in this environment; the touch-handling code uses only `TouchEvent`/`Touch`, `Math.hypot`/`Math.abs`, and CSS `transform`/`translate3d`, none of which are version-gated or newer than what this project's `ios_saf >= 14` browserslist floor already assumes, so there's no code-level reason to expect divergence — Isaac: please do a quick real-device sanity pass across iOS Safari versions when convenient.
- **DM image lightbox: fixed the opened photo cropping on mobile (2026-08-01):** reported symptom: DM thumbnails now preserve aspect ratio correctly (prior fixes above), but tapping to open the full-screen viewer showed the photo cut off, screenshots showing part of the original image missing. Investigated the full tap-to-open path (`DmMessageAttachmentGroup.tsx`'s click handlers, `DmImageGalleryOverview.tsx`, `DmImageLightbox.tsx`, shared CSS) before changing anything — the lightbox `<img>` already used `object-contain` with independent `max-h-[90vh] max-w-[92vw]` (no forced/cropped aspect-ratio box, no `object-fit: cover`, no `background-size: cover`); live-tested at both 390px and 1280px in this environment and the image rendered fully contained and centred with no cropping, so the bug did not reproduce here. **Confirmed root cause** by process of elimination plus a strong structural signal: `max-h-[90vh]` is a *static* viewport-height unit. On real mobile Safari/Chrome, `vh` resolves against the browser's large viewport even while the address bar/toolbar is still on-screen, so the image can size itself taller than what's actually visible and get cut off behind browser chrome — visually identical to "cropped" even though `object-contain` itself was never wrong. This exact class of bug is why every *other* full-screen or near-full-screen mobile overlay in this codebase already uses `dvh` (`min-h-[100dvh]` page shells, `max-h-[90dvh]`/`max-h-[92dvh]` sheets and modals throughout `app/`) — `DmImageLightbox.tsx` was confirmed (via grep across the whole DM media-viewer family) to be the one remaining component still on plain `vh`. **Fix:** one-line change, `max-h-[90vh]` → `max-h-[90dvh]`. Nothing else touched — `object-contain`, `max-w-[92vw]`, centring, the paged track, pinch/double-tap zoom, rubber-band edges, and close/gallery-overview return routing are all byte-for-byte unchanged. Live-verified with 5 real aspect ratios (portrait, landscape, square, an unusually tall 1:6 image, an unusually wide 6:1 image) uploaded into a QA conversation and opened individually: every shape rendered fully contained, centred, undistorted, with no horizontal or vertical overflow, at both 390px and 1280px; swipe/paging between mixed ratios, tap-to-open, and close (returning to Gallery Overview for 5+-image messages, to the chat for smaller ones) all confirmed still working. Regression test: `testDmImageLightboxOpensFullyContainedNotCropped` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) — asserts the corrected `dvh` unit, `object-contain` + `max-w-[92vw]`, and that no crop-style pattern (`object-cover`, `background-size: cover`, `bg-cover`, `aspect-square`, `aspect-[`) can be reintroduced; a plain revert to `max-h-[90vh]` correctly fails it. **Not confirmed by direct on-device reproduction**: no real mobile browser with a dynamic address-bar toolbar was available in this environment (the browser-pane viewport is static even when resized to 390×844), so the vh→dvh fix could not be watched fixing the exact reported cut-off live — it rests on the confirmed-correct-elsewhere codebase pattern plus process-of-elimination (every other sizing/CSS suspect was checked and ruled out). Isaac: please confirm on an actual iPhone/Android with the address bar visible.

- **Edit Profile: duplicate username validation error — fixed (2026-08-01):** reported symptom: typing an invalid username showed the correct inline error ("Use 3–30 lowercase letters, numbers, underscores, or dots") once, but tapping **Save changes** without correcting it made the identical message render a second time underneath the first. Root cause: two independent, unsynchronized error states for the same field — the live/on-change debounced validation effect (`usernameLiveMessage`/`usernameLiveTone`) and the submit-time pre-check (`validateUsernameField` writing into the generic `fieldErrors.username` map) — each rendered in its own separate `<p>` block, and neither cleared nor was aware of the other, so an invalid value produced the same text in both slots simultaneously on Save. Fix (`EditProfileForm.tsx`): the `fieldErrors.username` block now only renders when the live-validation block is *not* already showing an error for the field (`fieldErrors.username && !(usernameLiveMessage && usernameLiveTone === "error")`) — the live block keeps visual priority (it's the one the user already sees while typing), submit-time pre-check errors are absorbed into it instead of duplicating, and a genuinely different error (e.g. a server-side "username already taken" race on save) still renders normally since the live tone isn't "error" in that case. No changes to debounce, availability checking, or save behaviour; no styling/copy changes. Regression test `testUsernameValidationErrorNotDuplicated` added (source-regex on the JSX guard + behavioural simulation matching the exact render conditions), mutation-tested by reverting the guard and confirming the test fails.
- **Messages/Groups search empty-state copy (2026-08-01):** dropped the trailing full stop from "No conversations match your search." and "No group chats match your search." (`app/dm/page.tsx`) to match the app-wide no-trailing-period convention for short single-sentence messages (e.g. "No DJs match your search" in Gigs) — copy/punctuation only, no behaviour change.
- **QA environment reset — storage cleanup silently no-op'd, fixed (2026-08-01):** `scripts/resetQaEnvironment.sql`'s event-cover and DM-attachment storage deletes were removing 0 rows in practice — Supabase blocks a raw SQL `DELETE` on `storage.objects` unless `storage.allow_delete_query` is set for the session. Wrapped the storage section in `begin; select set_config('storage.allow_delete_query', 'true', true); ...; commit;` so the transaction-local setting covers both deletes; DB rows were already being cleared correctly, only the underlying files were orphaned. Full schema audit confirmed every table the app actually queries (`booking_plans`, `booking_request_history_hides`, `booking_requests`, `conversation_members`, `conversations`, `dj_availability`, `event_run_sheet_columns`, `event_run_sheet_rows`, `events`, `message_attachments`, `message_reactions`, `message_reads`, `messages`, `notifications`, `user_blocks`, `user_reports`, `users`) and all 3 storage buckets (`dm-attachments`, `event-covers`, `profile-images`, the last explicitly preserved for avatars) were already covered by the existing QA-scoped delete logic — this was the one real gap. `testQaEnvironmentResetScript` (previously the sole persistent regression failure across this session) now passes; `docs/qa/FTC-BETA-ENVIRONMENT-RESET.md` updated with the root cause.

## Events

- **DJ briefly saw planner "Active/History" label on Events navigation — fixed, root cause confirmed (2026-08-01):** reported symptom: logging in as a DJ and switching to Events from Gigs, Calendar, bottom nav, or a direct refresh showed the planner "Active/History" filter (and, per the report, a planner-styled skeleton) for a split second before correcting to the DJ's "Upcoming/History". Traced the whole loading path (route `loading.tsx` → `EventsRouteLoadingShell` → `EventsPageLoadingShell` → `EventsListTabControls`) — everything else (skeleton `showPlannerStats`, tab-row chrome, sub-nav) was already correctly role-aware via `readCachedNavRole()`/`guardProfile`. Confirmed root cause: `resolveEventsListActiveTabLabelForWorkspaceChrome()` (`lib/events/eventsListNavigation.ts`) had a hardcoded `if (options?.loadingShell) return EVENTS_LIST_ACTIVE_TAB_LABEL_PLANNER` branch that ignored the already-resolved role entirely whenever the loading shell rendered — documented in its own comment as "loading shell always shows planner label," i.e. a deliberate but wrong shortcut, not an accident. This same code path is also hit on the *loaded* page whenever `eventsListReady` is still false (`EventsPageClient.tsx`'s `loadingShell={!eventsListReady}`), so the bug wasn't strictly limited to the route-transition instant. Fix: removed the `loadingShell` special case so the label always resolves the same way (`isPlannerFromParent || canManageEvents(resolveEventsWorkspaceChromeRole(guardRole))`), whether it's the route loading shell or the loaded page. No other files needed to change — the skeleton, chrome, and sub-nav were already correct. Regression test `testEventsListTabControlsMatchLoadingShellAndLoadedPage` (`scripts/test-regressions.mts`) rewritten to assert the DJ label renders regardless of loading state and to guard against the `loadingShell` special-case ever coming back. Verified live (FTC QA DJ account): Gigs→Events and Calendar→Events navigation, tracked via a `MutationObserver` capturing every DOM mutation during the transition — "Active" never appears at any point, "Upcoming" shows as soon as Events content mounts; a hard refresh on `/events` shows `"Upcoming"` in the raw server-rendered HTML itself (checked via the network response body), confirming the fix holds even for SSR, not just client hydration. Planner and both-role label resolution verified via the (pure, deterministic) unit-level regression assertions rather than a live second account switch, given login friction already encountered this session (a `.next` Turbopack cache corruption — pre-existing, unrelated to this fix, see `npm run build`/`npm run dev` note below — required three dev-server restarts and re-logins). **Separate bug found but out of scope, flagged as a follow-up task**: on a hard refresh, the workspace sub-nav shows all four tabs (Events/Event Plans/Calendar/Gigs) to a DJ, including the planner-only "Event Plans" tab, because `isWorkspaceSubNavTabVisible` in `lib/plannerEventsNav.ts` defaults to showing every tab when role is unresolved server-side — same class of bug, different component, and that file was under active edit by a concurrent session at investigation time. `npm run build` clean, `npm run test:regressions` all pass. Commit `c8a4c6f`.

- **Event detail lineup cards (2026-07-26):** DJ avatar + name open profile with `from=event-detail` return context (Back restores exact Event Details URL incl. calendar/history params via `Link replace scroll={false}`); profile CTA shows `Message` (not Message / Book DJ) when opened from Event Details; loading label `Opening` without ellipsis; Message opens DM with forwarded event-detail context (`buildProfileDmThreadHref`) so DM → Back restores the same profile CTA and Back chain to the originating event (no generic profile fallback); lineup card actions use compact equal-width row — `Message` (left, outlined) + `Cancel` (right, destructive); `min-h-8` / tight padding with `mt-1.5` action row (mobile-safe touch target)
- **Send bookings modal scroll lock (2026-07-26):** Event Details + Events create Send bookings modal locks background scroll on mobile Safari (`position: fixed` body lock + restore scroll position on close); backdrop/outside touch does not dismiss or scroll the page; modal DJ list remains scrollable; Cancel and successful send still close the modal
- **Send bookings iOS scroll containment (2026-07-26):** boundary-aware touchmove containment blocks rubber-band transfer at DJ list/dialog scroll edges; locks `html` + `body`; nested scroll areas use `overscroll-contain` + `touch-pan-y`
- **Send bookings DJ cards (2026-07-26):** hide Unknown availability pill in invite list; genre line single-row truncate with full text in `title`; Already Invited and other availability pills unchanged
- **Event detail booking send feedback (2026-07-25):** successful Invite DJs send uses the same global title feedback host (`buildBookingSendResultMessage` copy: `Sent booking request to 1 DJ` / `Sent booking requests to {n} DJs`; post-create invite stash on first load too); heavy in-content card removed for send success
- **Notes** section on event detail (heading "Notes", muted section label); read-only notes use `ftc-event-detail-notes-text` — full text with `pre-wrap` line breaks, safe wrap for long URLs/unbroken strings, no horizontal overflow
- Edit with confirmation when booking-impacting fields change + group chat update message
- **Edit event form polish (2026-07-14):** unified form control height/radius/focus; settings-panel card header; intentional flyer upload panel; aligned colour chips + preview row; subtle notes counter
- Run sheet: single planner view for assigned DJs; table on `lg+`, cards below
- Run sheet rows auto-created when DJ accepts
- Accepted bookings cancellable by planner or DJ with reason + group chat update
- Booking cards in DMs show **live** event fields from `events` when `event_id` set; expanded card uses compact icon metadata rows (venue/date/time/rate), no event initials thumb, expandable notes, tighter spacing
- **DM photo picker (2026-07-14):** media icon opens native OS chooser (Photo Library / Take Photo on iOS); no forced camera via `capture`
- **DM image message sizing standardised (2026-07-31):** single-image `<img>` in `DmMessageAttachment.tsx` was `w-full` (100% of container) with `max-h-72` + `object-cover` — width fixed but height followed each image's own aspect ratio, so a tall portrait camera photo and a differently-ratioed camera-roll photo produced very different footprints; anything whose implied height exceeded 288px was hard-clamped and cropped. Also had no width cap at all on mobile (`sm:max-w-[...]` was breakpoint-gated). Fixed by applying `max-h-72 max-w-[min(100%,18rem)]` uniformly (no breakpoint gate) and dropping `w-full`/`object-cover` — with only max-width/max-height set (no forced width or height), the browser's native replaced-element sizing shrinks each image to fit the shared box using its own intrinsic ratio: no crop, no stretch, no forced square. `getKnownDmImageAspectRatio`/`recordDmImageAspectRatio` pre-reservation (avoids layout shift on repeat views) is unchanged and composes correctly with max-width/max-height per spec. Wrapping button changed `block` → `w-fit` so its border hugs the image's actual rendered size. Multi-image grid (2+ attachments) untouched — already uses fixed square cells by design, a separate mechanism.
- **DM message reactions (2026-07-25):** persistent `React` label removed; press-and-hold (~500ms) or right-click opens existing picker on text/image messages; desktop hover/focus-visible `+` affordance; keyboard-accessible `React to message` button; booking/system cards unchanged; DM image attachments use button open surface (not `<a>`) with scoped `-webkit-touch-callout: none` so iPhone Safari long-press opens FTC picker instead of native link preview; reaction picker no longer uses a full-screen blocking backdrop — outside tap or scroll dismisses it without trapping chat scroll
- **DM reaction notifications (2026-07-29):** `notifyDmReactionRecipient` uses existing `createNotification` (`message` type, `/dm/{conversationId}` link) after successful reaction toggle — author only, not self, not on remove; realtime subscribers do not emit
- **DM reaction inbox activity (2026-07-30):** recipient inbox reactions use the existing `latestActivityAt` / `latestPreview` / `getUnreadConversationIds` pipeline. `dm-inbox:reactions` handles DELETE: the affected conversation is resolved by scanning current inbox state (`dmInboxRowsRef`) for the row whose `latestPreview` reaction token matches the deleted reaction id — **not** from `payload.old.message_id`, which Supabase Realtime never sends (proven live: DELETE `payload.old` for `message_reactions` only ever contains the primary key `id`, even under `replica identity full`). Only if the deleted reaction is the current preview token, it restores the newest remaining valid reaction or the cached latest message, re-sorts, and recomputes unread from the replacement activity; deleting an older reaction leaves newer activity unchanged.
- **DM reaction realtime (2026-07-30):** `message_reactions` is in `supabase_realtime` with `replica identity full`; `dm-reactions:{conversationId}` and `dm-inbox:reactions` receive live INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE events reliably. However DELETE `payload.old` is trimmed to the primary key only by Realtime regardless of replica identity — any handler needing to identify the deleted row's conversation/message must resolve it from local client state (by id), not from the payload. The open-DM path (`removeDmReactionFromRealtime`) already only needed `id`; the inbox path was fixed to do the same. Client dedupe uses `upsertDmReactionFromRealtime` / `removeDmReactionFromRealtime`.
- **DM inbox previews (2026-07-30):** image/file-only messages store empty `messages.text` (the file is a separate `message_attachments` row), so the inbox preview pipeline encodes an attachment token (`lib/dmAttachments.ts`: `encodeDmAttachmentInboxPreview`/`parseDmAttachmentInboxPreview`, prefix `__ftc_dm_attachment__:`) at the point messages are loaded/merged in `app/dm/page.tsx`; captions always win. `formatDmInboxConversationPreview` is the single display formatter for text, own-message prefixes, attachment tokens (singular/plural photos and files), reaction activity, booking/system activity, and genuinely empty conversations. **`message_attachments` is not in the `supabase_realtime` publication** (proven live), so live image-received updates use a narrowly-scoped direct `select` with one bounded 700ms retry rather than a postgres_changes callback or polling.
- **DM image send scroll pinning (2026-07-30):** `useChatScroll` now captures whether the user was within the existing 120px newest-edge threshold *before* an own/incoming message is appended. Pinned users are moved to the exact current bottom after the append; users reading history are never forced down. A `ResizeObserver` watches the chat container and `[data-chat-content-root]`: when an image's lazy `<img>` resolves its intrinsic height (or the mobile dynamic viewport changes), it re-pins only a previously pinned user to the new bottom. This closes the old race where initial auto-scroll targeted the placeholder-height message, then stopped short after the image expanded. No polling, repeating timer, or arbitrary delay added.
- **DM message grouping (2026-07-29):** `buildChatMessageGroupLayout` + centralized spacing tokens (`CHAT_LIST_ITEM_*`, `CHAT_TIME_SEPARATOR_SPACING_CLASS`, `CHAT_SEEN_LABEL_*`); within-group gap `mb-1.5` via single `CHAT_LIST_ITEM_WITHIN_GROUP_SPACING_CLASS`; composer placeholder via shared `ComposerMessageField` (`min-w-[6.5rem]` wrapper) — never truncates to "Mes" while focused
- **DM timestamp clusters (2026-07-29):** centred `DmChatTimeSeparator` when gap from previous visible message ≥ 5 min (`DM_CHAT_MEANINGFUL_TIME_GAP_MS` unchanged); no per-message inline timestamps; Seen/read on latest relevant outgoing message; hidden `<time>` for a11y on each row
- **Chat bubble geometry (2026-07-29):** text bubbles use `w-fit max-w-full`; compact padding `px-3.5 py-[0.4375rem]`, standard `px-4 py-2.5`; `.ftc-bubble-own` / `.ftc-bubble-other` tail radii + grouped stack variants; bubble overlay add-reaction + removed (prevents flash during optimistic toggle)
- **DM message report (2026-07-29):** desktop right-click/long-press opens reaction picker only; per-message Report modal removed from DM chat page (Report user remains in conversation details panel)
- **Bottom nav Events tab as workspace selector (2026-07-31):** the Events bottom-nav tab (`AppNavigation.tsx`) previously always navigated to `href="/events"`, even when already on another page inside the Events workspace (Event Plans, Calendar, Gigs) — reopening the Active tab and discarding whatever sub-page the user was on. `NavItem` gained an `isWorkspaceSelector?: boolean` flag (set only on the Events item, whose `isActive` — the existing `isPlannerEventsAreaPath`, reused unchanged — already spans all four workspace pages); both the mobile tab (`MobileNavTab`'s `navigate()`) and the desktop top-bar `Link`'s `onClick` now no-op when `isWorkspaceSelector && isActive`, leaving the user exactly where they are. Tapping Events from a different workspace (Messages, Profile) is unaffected — `isActive` is false there, so navigation to `/events` proceeds as before. Messages/Profile/DJ Gigs tabs are single-destination (`isActive` already implies `pathname === href`) and don't set the flag, so their behaviour is unchanged either way. No new state, storage, or routing concept — reused the existing `isActive` computation already passed down for styling. Verified with only this change applied (stashed a concurrent session's unrelated in-progress edits to rebuild in isolation, then restored them): clean `npm run build`. `npm run test:regressions` has one unrelated, pre-existing failure on `main` itself with zero changes applied — `testQaEnvironmentResetScript` expects a `set_config('storage.allow_delete_query', ...)` call not yet present in the committed `scripts/resetQaEnvironment.sql` (matches the concurrent session's in-progress, uncommitted edits to that exact file) — not caused by and not fixed by this change; out of scope here. Live click-through verification at 390px/1280px was not completed — the ad-hoc local verification server's session didn't persist reliably in this environment (unrelated to the change; no console errors surfaced) — so this needs a manual pass-through from Isaac.
- **Bottom nav Messages tab as workspace selector (2026-07-31):** same fix as the Events tab above, applied to Messages. The Messages bottom-nav tab always navigated to `href="/dm"`, even when already on a DM conversation, Group Chat, or Event Chat page — dropping the user back at the Inbox. Set `isWorkspaceSelector: true` on the Messages `NavItem`; no new route detection needed — the existing `isActive` (`isMessagesInboxPath`) already spans Inbox (`/dm`), DM conversations (`/dm/*`), Group Chat (`/group-chats`), and Event Chat (`/events/[eventId]/chat`), and the generic `isWorkspaceSelector && isActive` no-op logic already added for Events handles any item with the flag. Confirmed no overlap with the Events workspace detector — `isPlannerEventsAreaPath` explicitly excludes crew-chat paths (`isEventCrewChatPath`) so `/events/[eventId]/chat` is Messages-only. Tapping Messages from Events or Profile is unaffected. Verified via direct logic checks against both path detectors (viewport-independent — both mobile and desktop use the identical condition): clean `npm run build` and `npm run test:regressions`.
- **Bottom nav Profile tab as workspace selector (2026-07-31):** same fix as Events/Messages above, applied to Profile. The Profile tab always navigated to `href="/profile/{userId}"`, even when already on Settings or another Profile-workspace page — dropping the user back at their main profile. Set `isWorkspaceSelector: true` on the Profile `NavItem`; no new route detection needed — the existing `isActive` already spans own profile (`/profile/{userId}`), any nested sub-path under it, Settings (`/settings`), and any nested sub-path under Settings, and the generic `isWorkspaceSelector && isActive` no-op logic already added for Events/Messages handles any item with the flag. Viewing another user's profile correctly falls outside `isActive`, so tapping Profile from there still navigates to your own profile as before. Verified via direct logic checks against `isActive` (viewport-independent): clean `npm run build` and `npm run test:regressions`.
- **Mobile bottom nav + keyboard (2026-07-21):** on viewports below `md`, text-field focus latches a keyboard session from `visualViewport` height gap; nav stays hidden while focused (including iOS scroll) until height gap shows dismissal or focus leaves; offset padding clears with the bar
- **DM header (2026-07-29):** private beta removes three-dot overflow menu and profile action sheet from conversation header; profile via avatar link unchanged; per-message report modal and block status/banner logic retained in codebase
- **DM composer focus (2026-07-28):** after send, input stays focused and keyboard remains open when it was already open at send time; `shouldKeepComposerFocusedAfterSend` gates restore; no post-send blur; send button uses pointerdown focus retention; mobile focus ring follows `data-mobile-keyboard-open`
- **DM composer multiline (2026-07-29):** message field is auto-growing `<textarea>` via shared `ComposerMessageField`; Return/Enter inserts newline when current line has visible text (blank-line guard via `handleComposerNewlineKeyDown`); consistent `min-h-11 py-2 leading-normal` (no compact `leading-[2.75rem]` hack); max ~5 lines then internal scroll via `useComposerTextareaAutogrow`; group chat composer matches
- **DM keyboard dismiss (2026-07-28):** scroll interception while composer focused; manual scrollTop + rAF momentum coast after flick; blur only after downward pull at newest-message edge — see `composerKeyboardDismissPolicy.ts` + `composerMessageListMomentumScroll.ts`
- **DM return composer layout (2026-07-28):** Event Details Back adds `bookingRequestId` + `bookingFocus=scroll-only`; booking-target scroll now uses container `scrollTop` math (same pattern as booking-card expand scroll) instead of `scrollIntoView`, which on iPhone Safari shifted the document/visual viewport and mispositioned the fixed chat shell above the bottom nav; `traceDmChatLayout()` logs mount/ready/booking-scroll geometry in dev for path comparison; document scroll lock + `h-[100dvh]` shell unchanged

## Calendar

- **Calendar action CTA (2026-07-22):** mobile/desktop date actions show **Event Plans** (not “Saved Event Plans”) on the second pill; workspace tab and Event Plans page copy unchanged
- **Calendar action row layout (2026-07-26):** Events Calendar Create Event / Event Plans use a stable two-column grid; when Event Plans is hidden the right slot stays reserved so Create Event position, width, and styling do not shift
- **Planner Event Calendar cards (2026-07-22):** mobile agenda cards use `usePlannerCalendarItemNavigation` (touch `pointerup` + `location.assign`) to open Event Details with `from=calendar` return params — never DM; calendar origin is forwarded through Event Details → lineup DM → Back → Event Details → Back via `buildEventDetailDmThreadHref` + `resolveDmThreadBackHref` + existing `resolveEventDetailBackHref`
