## Booking / invite UX

- **Booking card group chat is a nav row, not a third CTA (2026-08-03):** the expanded booking card wrapped its group-chat section in its own bordered box (`rounded-xl border … bg-ftc-bg-elevated p-3`) containing a full `ftc-btn-primary` "Open group chat" — a second primary button competing with **View event**, inside a box nested in a card that already has a border. Now: a muted `Group chat` section label plus the shared **`EventDetailSecondaryAction`** row (icon + title + chevron, `min-h-[3.25rem]`, `hover:border-ftc-border-strong`, focus ring). That component is already used standalone outside its own module (`EventsPageClient`, `events/[eventId]/page.tsx` both import `EventDetailPrimaryAction` from it), so this is reuse of the established FTC nav-row rather than a new one-off. **Action hierarchy is now explicit:** View event = primary, Cancel booking = destructive, Open group chat = navigation. The bordered wrapper is gone (the card is already a card) and the redundant `<>…</>` fragment + nested ternary collapsed into a single condition. **Label uses the card's own muted 10px convention** (the same class as its two "Booking request" labels) rather than `PlannerSectionLabel`, which is cyan and would have made this one label inconsistent with its siblings in the same card. Behaviour untouched: same `getBookingGroupChatAccess` states (`open` → href, `locked_pending` → "Group chat unlocks after you accept.", `hidden` → nothing), same destination. New `testBookingCardGroupChatIsANavigationRow` pins the reuse, the absence of `ftc-btn-primary` in that block, the removed box, the label convention and both access states; **mutation-tested** by restoring the primary button and by re-adding the bordered box — each caught. `tsc`, `npm run build`, full suite clean. **Live-verified** at 390px and 1280px in a real DM thread on an accepted booking: row is full-width and tappable, 52px tall, 2 SVGs (chat icon + chevron), href `/events/:id/chat`, no nested box wrapper (`parentClasses: "mt-2"`), no horizontal overflow.

- Default send button label mode: **Confirm N DJ(s)** (`SendBookingRequestsPanel`)
- Unavailable-DJ confirm modal uses Confirm wording
- Calendar-origin and standard create flows aligned on copy and validation
- **Calendar create workspace tab (2026-07-23):** Calendar Create Event / Event Plans open on `/calendar?create=…` via `EventsCalendarOriginCreateClient` (no intermediate `/events` list); legacy `/events?create=calendar` redirects to calendar; CALENDAR tab stays highlighted; cancel/save return unchanged
- **Calendar create workspace navigation (2026-07-26):** leaving `/calendar?create=…` uses `navigateAwayFromCalendarCreateWorkspace` (`location.assign` — App Router client nav is unreliable here); workspace routes prefetch on create open to warm the reload; intercept only blocks tabs while saving
- **Gigs History cards (2026-07-15):** `Fixed ·` / `Open offer` fee copy aligned with Incoming/Confirmed; tighter info-to-actions spacing; shorter View event (primary) + Open DM (subdued) buttons
- **Gigs Confirmed tab (2026-07-19):** received gigs reload after booking acceptance (`ftc-notifications-updated` + tab visibility) so accepted bookings appear in Confirmed without stale client state; gig date keys use shared `resolveEventDateKey` (legacy + ISO); `?tab=confirmed` URL alias maps to Confirmed
- **Gigs tab row (2026-07-23):** `DjGigsTabs` — Incoming/Confirmed use compact `ftc-gigs-tab-pill` (`0.375rem 0.5rem`, `min-height: 1.875rem`); counts cap at **99+** via `formatGigsTabCountDisplay`; label/count row and **`2.5ch`** count slot mount only when count &gt; 0 via shared **`shouldRenderGigsTabCount`**; label/count gap **`gap-1.5` (~6px)**; **History** reuses **`eventsListTabPillClass`**. **`gigsTabCountsCache`** + **`gigsListSnapshotPrefetch`** (from workspace sub-nav) warm tab counts before first Gigs visit.
- **Gigs History delete-selection row (2026-07-24):** bulk-delete toolbar reuses shared `HistorySelectionToolbar` (`tabRowEmbedded`, back/ALL/Delete) inline in `DjGigsTabRow` — History pill + trash swap to toolbar in the same row (`Incoming | Confirmed | ← ALL Delete`); Incoming/Confirmed stay tappable; leaving History (tab or workspace nav) exits selection and clears picks; no second toolbar row or card layout jump
- **Withdrawal Other reason field (2026-07-25):** DJ withdraw / planner cancel “Other” details — 80 chars max, 3 explicit `\n` lines; fixed ~3-row scroll textarea; DM booking card Reason uses `ftc-dm-booking-cancellation-reason-text` (3-line clamp + `max-height` fallback, `pre-line`, safe wrap); Event Details `Your booking` + lineup cards use `EventDetailBookingCancellationDetails` + `ftc-event-detail-cancellation-reason-text` (full reason, safe wrap, `min-w-0` flex children)
- **Cancelled DM booking cards (2026-07-25):** no View event action — cancelled bookings render as a completed receipt (event meta + cancellation info only); pending/accepted unchanged
- **Gigs sub-tab switching (2026-07-24):** Incoming/Confirmed/History pills use `history.pushState` + `bumpGigsListRouteRevision` (Events pattern) so active pill and list filter update immediately without waiting for Next `searchParams` or App Router navigation
- **Gigs list loading (2026-07-19):** Incoming/Confirmed/History show `ReceivedBookingsListSkeleton` while the initial gigs fetch runs (toolbar stays visible; no blank list gap); tab switches with cached data skip the skeleton
- **Gigs tab counts (2026-07-19):** Incoming/Confirmed counts derive from the received-bookings + hidden-id snapshot as soon as those requests complete; sender profile fetch no longer blocks counts; reserved count slots stay stable before numbers appear (no fake zero)
- **Workspace sub-nav (2026-07-19):** shared `(planner-workspace)` layout keeps Events / Event Plans / Calendar / Gigs tabs mounted across route transitions; loading shells render content only below the persistent tab row; mobile tabs use horizontal scroll + `router.push` (no full reload)
- **Profile (2026-07-19):** removed redundant Calendar/Gigs navigation card from DJ profile — availability and bookings stay in Gigs nav only
- **Profile header/nav (2026-07-19):** removed back button from own-profile header (top-level nav destination); mobile bottom nav Profile tab uses same touch `pointerup` routing as other tabs and resolves user id synchronously from session/cache
- **Profile back from DM (2026-07-26):** profiles opened via `buildProfileHref` (`from=chat&returnTo=…`) show top-left Back in `ProfilePageHeader`; Back uses `Link replace` to return directly to the encoded DM URL (preserves query params, no Messages detour); DM details panel View profile uses the same return context; Event Details lineup cards use `buildEventDetailProfileHref` (`from=event-detail&eventId=…`) for the same Back pattern to the originating event; DM back from event-detail profile uses `buildProfileDmThreadHref` / `resolveDmThreadBackHref` with `profileFrom=event-detail` so profile state survives DM round-trip (`Link replace scroll={false}` on DM header)
- **Profile from active DM (2026-07-29):** `isProfileOpenedFromDmConversation(from=chat, returnTo=/dm/…)` hides the bottom Message/Book DJ CTA; group-chat profile opens (`returnTo=/events/…/chat`) and Discover/event/booking entry points keep the CTA; DM saves/restores message-list `scrollTop` in sessionStorage when opening a profile and returning via Back
- **DM scroll restore gate (2026-07-30):** fixed a bug where a fresh/normal DM open (inbox → DM) could restore a stale saved scroll position instead of landing on the newest messages. Root cause: `useDmChatScrollRestoreOnProfileReturn` consumed `ftc-dm-chat-scroll:{conversationId}` from `sessionStorage` on every mount, with no signal distinguishing "returning from that user's profile" from "opening the conversation normally" — a profile-link click saves a snapshot, but if the user left the profile some other way (not Back), the snapshot stayed in storage (up to its 30 min max age) and got wrongly applied on the next ordinary open. Fix wires the previously-unused `lib/dm/dmChatScrollRestoration.ts` helpers (`DM_CHAT_SCROLL_RESTORE_PARAM`, `buildDmChatScrollRestoreHref`, `shouldRestoreDmChatScroll`): the DM page's `chatReturnTo` (passed as `returnTo` to `buildProfileHref` for the header/message avatar links) now marks itself with `?restoreScroll=1` via `buildDmChatScrollRestoreHref`; the hook only calls `consumeDmChatScrollPosition` when the DM page's own mount URL carries that param (`shouldRestoreScroll` prop, gated by `shouldRestoreDmChatScroll(searchParams.get(...))`). Normal inbox/DM-list opens never carry the param, so they always land on newest messages; only landing back on the DM via the profile page's `Link replace` Back (which passes the marked `returnTo` straight through) restores the prior position. No new storage key; crew chat's separate `buildChatReturnTo` usage is untouched.
- **DM initial-scroll long-conversation fix (2026-07-31):** separate, deeper bug found after the above ship — long DM conversations (enough history to overflow the viewport) could open scrolled to a position above the newest messages even on a genuine fresh inbox open (`restoreScroll` absent, `bookingRequestId` absent, `suppressAutoScrollRef` false, `scrollToBottom()` confirmed executing and `scrollTop` confirmed reaching and holding `maxScrollTop`). Root cause was structural, not timing: the DM message list rendered `reversedMessages` (`[...messages].reverse()`, newest-first) inside `CHAT_MESSAGE_LIST_CLASS`'s `flex-col-reverse` `<ul>`, while the scrollable ancestor (`scrollRef`, `CHAT_MESSAGE_SCROLLER_CLASS`) stayed non-reversed `flex-col` — a double-reversal that only becomes visually observable once a conversation is long enough to actually overflow (short conversations never expose it, since `maxScrollTop` is `0` under either interpretation, which is why FTC QA DJ always looked correct while Breaker Breaker didn't). Fix: added DM-only `DM_CHAT_MESSAGE_LIST_CLASS` (`lib/dm/chatMessageGroupLayout.ts`) — plain `flex-col justify-end` (no reversal; `justify-end` keeps short conversations resting at the visual bottom without needing reversed DOM order) — and the DM page (`app/dm/[conversationId]/page.tsx`) now maps `messages` directly in chronological order (removed `reversedMessages`/`reversedIndex`; `messageVisuallyBelow` is now `messages[index + 1]`). Crew/group chat (`app/events/[eventId]/chat/page.tsx`) still uses the original `CHAT_MESSAGE_LIST_CLASS` + its own separate `reversedMessages`, untouched — this fix is DM-only. `useChatScroll.ts` and `useDmChatScrollRestoreOnProfileReturn`/`dmChatScrollRestoration.ts` (the prior fix) needed no changes; the scroll-math and restore-gate logic were already correct — the bug was purely in which DOM order was fed into which container direction.
- **DM repeat-open scroll fix, attempt 2 (2026-07-31):** third bug in the same area — reopening the *same* DM conversation a second time (inbox → conversation → inbox → same conversation again) did not land on newest, even though the first open of that conversation had worked correctly and none of the known suppression paths (`restoreScroll`, `bookingRequestId`) were present. `useChatScroll`'s one-shot `needsInitialScrollRef` is only reset by its own `loading`-transition effect, and `loadConversationData`'s data-fetch effect only depends on `[conversationId]` — so a second entry into a conversation whose id hasn't changed has no guaranteed independent signal forcing a fresh initial-scroll evaluation if the surrounding page instance persists across the away-and-back navigation (App Router client-side transitions are not contractually guaranteed to give every route entry a brand-new component instance). Fix adds a fresh-per-click marker, independent of component/effect lifecycle: `buildDmChatFreshOpenHref` (`lib/dm/dmChatScrollRestoration.ts`) stamps `?openedAt=<token>` on the inbox's `openConversation` href; `useChatScroll` accepts an optional `freshOpenToken` and treats a token change (tracked via `lastScrolledFreshOpenTokenRef`, compared against the current token) as an additional, independent trigger for one fresh scroll-to-bottom evaluation, alongside (not replacing) the existing `loading`-based one-shot gate. `freshOpenToken` is optional and unused by crew chat (`app/events/[eventId]/chat/page.tsx` still calls `useChatScroll` without it) — omitting it reduces the new logic to the exact original behaviour, byte-for-byte. Does not touch `restoreScroll`/profile-return restore or `bookingRequestId`/booking-target-scroll suppression — both remain the sole authority they were before; `openedAt` never triggers a restore, only a fresh "land on newest" pass, and the existing `suppressAutoScrollRef` check still gates the actual `scrollToBottom()` call regardless of which path decided to attempt it.
- **DM repeat-open scroll — attempt 3, reverted (2026-07-31):** the theorized "async `pinnedToBottomRef` gap" fix (`pinnedToBottomRef.current = true` set synchronously in the `isFreshOpen` branch) was disproved by manual QA — FTC QA DJ still failed to reopen on newest with the fix applied. Later built a deterministic test harness (happy-dom + the real `useChatScroll` hook, `ResizeObserver`/`requestAnimationFrame` test doubles under full manual control) and used it to run the exact race the fix targeted — height growth landing in the gap between the `isFreshOpen` decision and its own pending `requestAnimationFrame`, both before and after that rAF fires. Every construction passed identically with and without the fix: `scrollToBottom()` always re-measures `getChatMaxScrollTop()` fresh at the moment its own (guaranteed-to-run) rAF fires, so it self-corrects for any height growth regardless of what the ResizeObserver decided earlier. The fix was real code but addressed a race that cannot actually produce the wrong scrollTop — reverted.
- **DM repeat-open scroll — regression test added (2026-07-31):** `scripts/test-dm-chat-reopen-scroll.ts` (wired into `test-regressions.mts`) drives the real `useChatScroll` hook through inbox → open → return → reopen (same component instance, not remounted) using happy-dom with manually-controlled `ResizeObserver`/`requestAnimationFrame` doubles — not source-regex assertions. It proves two things with the same harness: (a) a control run with no fresh-open signal genuinely stays stuck at the pre-leave scroll position (proves the gap `freshOpenToken` — attempt 2, `8c52557` — closes is real, not assumed; verified this control fails to reproduce the bug if `freshOpenToken` support is removed from the hook entirely, matching pre-`8c52557` behaviour), and (b) with the signal, first open, a repeat reopen, and an image-heavy reopen (list fits before an attachment decodes, then overflows) all land on the newest message. Kept attempt 1 (`891eb2e`, flex-col-reverse structural fix) and attempt 2 (`8c52557`, `freshOpenToken`) — both proven necessary/correct; reverted attempt 3 (`5b589e1`) as disproven.
- **DM repeat-open scroll — FTC QA DJ, image aspect-ratio fix insufficient (2026-07-31):** manual QA disproved the "resolved" claim below — FTC QA DJ still opened mid-conversation, landing on a specific older image, after the aspect-ratio fix shipped. Kept the fix anyway (it's independently correct: reserving learned image dimensions before decode is real, verified, harmless, and prevents unnecessary layout shift for images this tab has already seen — see the entry below for what it does and its test), but it does not explain the full symptom by itself, most likely because `loading="lazy"` means the specific older image involved may never have decoded during the first view at all (out of viewport, so nothing was learned for it to reuse). Investigated next: `messages` is fetched via `.order("created_at", { ascending: true })` with **no secondary sort key** (`app/dm/[conversationId]/page.tsx`, `loadConversationData`). SQL gives no ordering guarantee among rows with an identical `created_at` when there's no full tiebreaker — and Postgres's `now()` is stable for an entire transaction, so any conversation whose history was bulk-seeded in one script/transaction (exactly what a synthetic "FTC QA DJ" QA-account history looks like, as opposed to an organic conversation like Breaker Breaker where real, separately-sent messages get genuinely distinct timestamps) can have several messages — plausibly several of the seeded images — sharing one timestamp. Two executions of the identical query can then legitimately return a different relative order for those tied rows, meaning which message renders last ("newest") can differ between a first open and a reopen even though the scroll position itself (`scrollTop === maxScrollTop`) is correct — matching "lands on a *specific*, *consistent* older image" better than any scroll-timing theory tested across four prior rounds. Fix: added `.order("id", { ascending: true })` as a tiebreaker, making the fetch fully deterministic. **Not verified live** — no authenticated browser session is available in this environment (policy), and `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is empty in the local `.env.local`, so a read-only check of FTC QA DJ's actual timestamps wasn't possible either. This is the best-evidenced remaining hypothesis and an independently-correct fix (a query without a full tiebreaker is always a latent bug regardless), not a live-confirmed root cause. Test: `scripts/test-dm-message-order-determinism.ts` — uses the real `@supabase/supabase-js` query builder (not our source, not a mock) to assert the actual outgoing request has `order=created_at.asc,id.asc`; verified it fails against the pre-fix single-key query and passes with the fix.
- **DM repeat-open scroll — FTC QA DJ, resolved (2026-07-31):** [superseded by the entry above — the aspect-ratio fix alone did not resolve the reported behaviour; entry retained for the fix's own rationale and test.] confirmed root cause after `useChatScroll`-level testing (all three prior rounds) found nothing — the fault was in the image message component, not the scroll hook, which is exactly why the hook test kept passing while the real conversation kept failing. `DmMessageAttachment.tsx`'s single-image `<img>` (the common case; the 2+ image grid already uses fixed `aspect-square` cells and was never affected) has no `width`/`height` or `aspect-ratio` — only a `max-h-72` cap — so the browser can't know its rendered height until it decodes. On a genuinely fresh load this is already compensated for (`useChatScroll`'s `ResizeObserver` re-pins once each image's real height lands — documented 2026-07-30). On a reopen inside the same tab, every previously-seen image is now browser-cached and can decode in a tight, near-simultaneous burst instead of the network-staggered sequence a first load produces; this is consistent with landing "around a specific older image" rather than merely short of the bottom, and with `useChatScroll`'s own real-DOM re-pin behaviour (proven correct via the deterministic test harness) not being the thing failing — the layout is shifting under content that's no longer being individually tracked in that burst. Fix: `lib/dm/dmImageAttachmentDimensions.ts` — a module-scoped `Map<attachmentId, aspectRatio>` populated from each image's real `onLoad` (`naturalWidth`/`naturalHeight`), consulted on every render of `DmMessageAttachmentView` to set `style={{ aspectRatio }}` *before* the image starts loading whenever this tab has already decoded it once. First-ever view of an image is unaffected (nothing learned yet, falls back to the existing, already-correct first-load path); every subsequent render of that same image (reopen, scroll-away-and-back, anywhere) reserves the correct box up front, so there is no late layout shift left for any re-pin mechanism — JS-driven or native browser scroll anchoring — to race against. Regression test: `scripts/test-dm-image-attachment-dimensions.ts` renders the real `DmMessageAttachmentView` component, fires a real `load` event with known natural dimensions, unmounts/remounts for the same attachment id (mirrors reopen), and asserts the aspect-ratio is reserved on the second render before any load event — verified this fails against the pre-fix component and passes against the fix. Crew chat, booking cards, and the multi-image grid are untouched.
- **DM repeat-open scroll — FTC QA DJ, actual root cause found and fixed (2026-07-31):** the aspect-ratio and ordering-tiebreaker fixes above were both real and independently correct but did not resolve the reported symptom (confirmed by manual QA after each shipped). Root cause found by live-reproducing the bug in a real, authenticated Chrome tab (Claude in Chrome extension, user's own session — the sandboxed browser-pane tool was confirmed unusable for this: its `ResizeObserver` never fires at all). That reproduction also surfaced an environmental confound worth recording: automation-driven tabs report `document.visibilityState: "hidden"` even while genuinely being screenshotted/interacted with, which throttles `ResizeObserver`/`requestAnimationFrame`/sub-second `setTimeout` delivery almost to nothing — so raw "does the observer fire" timing captured that way is not trustworthy evidence and was discarded in favour of reading `useChatScroll.ts` directly with the reproduction's other findings (scrollTop staying fixed while scrollHeight grows) as a guide. Actual bug: `scrollToBottom()` sets `scrollTop` to the *current* `getChatMaxScrollTop()` and marks `pinnedToBottomRef.current = true`, then calls `container.scrollTo(...)` — the resulting native `'scroll'` event is dispatched *asynchronously* by the browser, never in the same task. If an attachment `<img>` finishes decoding in that gap, `scrollHeight` grows (this never itself fires a `'scroll'` event — appending content below the fold doesn't move `scrollTop`), so by the time the *stale* `'scroll'` event for that earlier `scrollTo()` call finally arrives, `handleScroll()`'s `isNearBottom()` check compares the now-stale `scrollTop` against the new, larger max, sees a gap past the 120px threshold, and wrongly concludes the user scrolled away — clearing `pinnedToBottomRef.current`. The `ResizeObserver` watching the same growth then fires too, but its own re-pin correction reads that same, just-cleared ref and skips correcting, permanently stranding the view short of the true bottom. This explains every symptom across all four rounds: image-heavy only (Breaker Breaker has no post-mount growth to race against), worse on reopen (cached images decode in a tighter burst, widening the race window), and the "visible glitch" reported even on first open (slower network-staggered loading gives more, but not zero, chances to hit the same race). Fix (`lib/useChatScroll.ts`): added `lastAutoScrollTopRef`, set to the target every time `scrollToBottom()` programmatically moves `scrollTop`; `handleScroll()` now returns early — without touching `pinnedToBottomRef` — whenever the event's `scrollTop` still equals that last self-set value, since that means nothing has actually moved and the event is a stale echo of the app's own prior scroll, not user intent. A scrollTop that has genuinely moved (real user scroll, or another effect's own `scrollTo`) is unaffected and still evaluated normally. Regression test: `scripts/test-dm-chat-growth-scroll-race.ts` (wired into `test-regressions.mts`) drives the real hook through exactly this interleaving — deferred `scrollTo` dispatch, growth in the gap, then the stale echo followed by the `ResizeObserver` firing — using a scroll-metrics test double that (unlike the shared harness's) models the browser's async event dispatch instead of firing it synchronously; verified it fails against the pre-fix hook (stuck at the pre-growth position) and passes with the fix (lands on the true post-growth max). Not yet confirmed by Isaac's manual QA — this is the first round in this bug's history verified via live root-cause reproduction rather than theory, but the required final check is still his.
- **DM → Event Details → Back returns to the same booking card (2026-08-01):** [handoff entry backfilled 2026-08-01 — shipped in `55644db` but the CURRENT-STATE.md update for it was missed at the time.] fixed a separate bug from the DM scroll rounds above — from a DM conversation, tapping "View event" on a specific booking request card, then Back, could land the DM back open "around older image messages" instead of the same card, even though the existing booking-target-scroll fallback (`useChatBookingTargetScroll`, `bookingRequestId`/`bookingFocus=scroll-only` params) was wired correctly end-to-end. Root cause: that fallback is a bounded DOM-query retry loop (12 attempts × 50ms ≈ 600ms) racing fully-sequential conversation data loading (`loadConversationData`: messages → bookings → missing-booking hydration → attachments/reactions → currentUserId, no pagination) plus real paint/layout timing — a budget that can plausibly be exceeded on a real device even though every link in the href-building chain was independently verified correct. Fix: extended the existing `restoreScroll` sessionStorage exact-position mechanism (previously DM→Profile→Back only, 2026-07-30 entry above) to also cover DM→Event Details→Back, with the precise restore now primary and `useChatBookingTargetScroll` demoted to fallback-only when no saved position exists. `buildEventDetailFromDmHref` (`lib/events/eventsListNavigation.ts`) now unconditionally stamps the "View event" href with `restoreScroll=1`; `resolveEventDetailBackHref`/`resolveDmThreadHrefOptionsFromEventDetailReturn`/`buildDmThreadHref` thread that flag through to the DM return href; `useDmChatScrollRestoreOnProfileReturn`'s save-trigger click listener also matches `a[href*='/events/']`, not just `/profile/`. New `hasSavedDmChatScrollPosition` (`lib/dm/dmChatScrollRestoration.ts`) is a non-destructive peek the DM page uses to null out the booking-target scroll when a precise restore is pending, so the two mechanisms never race. Normal inbox opens, DM→Profile→Back, and Gigs/Calendar booking deep links are unaffected. Regression test: `scripts/test-dm-booking-return-scroll.ts`.
- **Event Plans delete mode (2026-07-19):** trash and delete-selection toolbars share one fixed-height secondary row (`EVENT_PLANS_TOOLBAR_ROW_CLASS`, same 1.875rem / md:2.375rem rhythm as Events/Gigs tab rows) so plan cards no longer shift when entering or leaving selection mode; title-row Create button slot stays reserved on mobile; toolbar layers swap in place via `EventsListTabRow` + embedded `HistorySelectionToolbar` (no flex-wrap growth)
- **Event Plans empty-state alignment (2026-07-26):** removed legacy 3.125rem toolbar wrapper above empty state — Event Plans empty card now shares the same content baseline as Events (secondary band + tab-row height only)
- **Event Plans selection toolbar stability (2026-07-25):** `SavedEventPlansSectionHeader` always renders trash and Back/All/Delete inside the same `EventsListTabRow` + `EventsListTabPillWidthSpacer` slot — removes conditional alternate DOM that caused card list jump on mode toggle
- **History bulk selection (2026-07-19):** Events History and Gigs History delete-selection use full-card tap plus cyan ring only — no presentational checkboxes; cards expose `aria-pressed` and `aria-selected` for screen readers; cancel exits atomically with stable card shells (border stays on the list item, not a swapping inner button) to prevent white border flash
- **Event Plans selection cards (2026-07-25):** delete-selection uses full-card tap plus inset cyan ring only — no checkbox; hidden `Use plan` button (`EVENT_PLAN_USE_BUTTON_SELECTION_HIDDEN_CLASS`) preserves CTA footprint in mobile + desktop slots so content width and card height stay stable when selection mode opens
- **Event Plans Use plan placement (2026-07-25):** `Use plan` CTA matches Gigs `Open DM` placement — mobile inline beside event/venue meta row; desktop bottom-right row (`hidden sm:flex justify-end`); button styling and tap target unchanged

## Event Plans

- **Event/plan short text limits (2026-07-22):** Event name, venue, and plan name share **`PLANNER_EVENT_PLAN_SHORT_TEXT_MAX_LENGTH` (30)** via `eventFormFieldValidation` + `bookingPlanFormFieldValidation`; `PlannerFormField` uses `applyTextInputLimit` on single-line inputs; save blocked when over limit (legacy longer DB values display until shortened).
- **Event Plans load perf (2026-07-21):** session/local list cache + workspace prefetch (`ensureBookingPlansListPrefetched` from Events sub-nav) — stale-while-revalidate on mount; create/edit/delete update cache; avoids skeleton + duplicate wait on repeat Events ↔ Event Plans navigation
- **Mobile cards (2026-07-14):** compact layout below `sm` — title + vertically centred Use plan on one row; separate Event and Venue label/value rows with single-line ellipsis truncation so max-length unbroken strings cannot overlap the Use plan button; NOTES label only when notes exist; tighter card padding and list spacing; desktop grid unchanged
- **Event Plan save button copy (2026-07-26):** create/edit form submit shows `SAVING` while saving (no trailing ellipsis; `uppercase` button class)
- **Event Plans delete confirmation copy (2026-07-26):** bulk/single delete dialog body uses sentence case without trailing full stops; loading primary shows `DELETING` (no ellipsis)
- **Use Plan DJ confirm loading copy (2026-07-26):** confirmation step primary button shows `CONFIRMING` while sending (no trailing ellipsis; same on unavailable-DJ confirm modal in this flow)
- **Event Plans polish (2026-07-14):** removed redundant “Saved Event Plans” heading; stronger title/meta hierarchy; 2-line notes preview; weighted Use plan outline button; History-matched bulk delete rows (`FTC_SURFACE_ROW_CLASS`, checkbox, toolbar)
- **Use Plan flow polish (2026-07-14):** Event details step shows `Plan` label + plan name; Use Plan entry uses top-right Cancel (returns to Event Plans); header uses `ftc-form-card-header` spacing to match Create Event
- **Gigs "send booking requests" DJ list height (2026-07-31):** the DJ selection `<ul>` in `app/(planner-workspace)/bookings/page.tsx` (`select-djs` step) used a flat `max-h-[420px]`; once a DJ's fixed-offer/ask-for-rate controls expanded inline, the remaining budget only showed a sliver of the next card before Send summary. First pass changed mobile to `max-h-[65dvh]` (no floor, still shrank on short viewports); landed on `max-h-[clamp(480px,72dvh,640px)] sm:max-h-[420px]` — measured real row heights (collapsed 74px, expanded 200px), 480px floor guarantees expanded + 2 more, 640px cap for tall phones, desktop unchanged at 420px.
- **Create Event (Events page) DJ invite height, real fix (2026-07-31):** the above two rounds targeted the wrong screen — `app/(planner-workspace)/events/EventsPageClient.tsx`'s embedded "Invite DJs (optional)" section (in the single-step Create Event form, not the Gigs 2-step flow) uses a separate component, `SendBookingRequestsPanel` (`app/components/booking/SendBookingRequestsPanel.tsx`), whose list height is controlled entirely by its own `listMaxHeightClass` prop — untouched by the Gigs-flow edits. That call site passed `listMaxHeightClass="max-h-48"` (192px), smaller than one selected DJ's expanded rate-controls row (measured 196px), so selecting any DJ clipped everything else to almost nothing.
- **Create Event DJ invite — scroll-trap regression + fix (2026-07-31):** the `clamp(480px,72dvh,640px)` applied above overcorrected — on mobile it could reach ~640px, nearly the full viewport, for an *embedded* section of a longer form. Combined with the panel's own `overscroll-contain` + `touch-pan-y` (which intentionally block scroll chaining to the parent so a modal-style list doesn't scroll the page behind it), a box that tall trapped touch-scroll gestures inside it instead of reaching Send summary / Save event. Fixed with `max-h-80 sm:max-h-[420px]` (320px mobile — the component's own built-in default — 420px desktop, unchanged). Verified with a debug harness: page `scrollHeight` stays small and Save event scrolls fully into view with 0, 1, and 3 DJs selected+expanded; the list still scrolls independently when there are more results than fit in 320px. `SendBookingRequestsModal.tsx` (bottom-sheet variant, used from Event Details "send more invites" / Calendar-origin create) still uses the panel's own default `max-h-80` and was intentionally left alone — different context, own dialog-level scroll.
- **Event Plans DJ card visual parity (2026-07-31):** after the height fix above, Event Plans' DJ cards still looked like a different component — separate left checkbox, `md` avatar with no selected badge, an availability badge shown even for "unknown" status, denser padding. Extracted the card itself (avatar + small selected checkmark badge, name/genre typography, compact duplicate/availability badges with "unknown" status correctly hidden, selected-border styling, Fixed Offer/Ask for Rate placement) into an exported `DjInviteSelectionRow` component in `SendBookingRequestsPanel.tsx`. `SendBookingRequestsPanel` (Events) now renders it too, so the two flows are visually identical by construction, not by copying values. `bookings/page.tsx` keeps its own state/search/summary/buttons — only the per-DJ row moved.
- **Event Plans (Use Plan) DJ invite parity fix (2026-07-31):** Event Plans' "Use Plan" flow (`booking-plans` → `/bookings?planId=...`) never used the shared `SendBookingRequestsPanel`/`useSendBookingRequestsDraft` workflow — `bookings/page.tsx` hand-duplicates the same DJ search/select/rate-controls/Send-summary UI with its own local state (`searchQuery`, `selectedDjIds`, `djOffers`, etc.), so it kept the old near-viewport `clamp(480px,72dvh,640px)` height after Events was fixed — the two flows only ever shared *visual intent*, not the actual height rule. Extracted `DJ_INVITE_LIST_MAX_HEIGHT_CLASS = "max-h-80 sm:max-h-[420px]"` as an exported constant from `SendBookingRequestsPanel.tsx` (single source of truth) and pointed both call sites at it — `EventsPageClient.tsx`'s explicit prop and `bookings/page.tsx`'s own `<ul>` className. The two DJ list markups are still separate implementations (not merged into one component) — only the height rule that was actually reported broken is now shared. Verified via debug harness: mobile 320px / desktop 420px, matching Events exactly, page scroll stays the primary scroller with 3 DJs selected+expanded.
- **Use Plan workspace tabs (2026-07-25):** while planner booking create is open on `/bookings` (Event Plans → Use Plan), Gigs Incoming/Confirmed/History sub-tabs are not mounted (`plannerBookingCreateOpen` synced via `GigsWorkspaceChromeState` after deep link clears `planId` from URL); workspace sub-nav intercept closes create via `resetCreateFlowState` then navigates; Event Plans stays highlighted
- **Use Plan cancel on DJ step (2026-07-26):** Cancel on step 1 or 2 returns to Event Plans without resetting `createOpen` first — bookings create UI and Event Plans workspace highlight stay mounted until `router.replace("/booking-plans")`; no transient Gigs Incoming/Confirmed/History render
- **Use Plan event creation (2026-07-26):** sending booking requests from Event Plans → Use Plan now creates an `events` row first (same `createEvent` path as Events → Create event), links booking requests via `event_id`, prepends Events Active cache, and clears planner calendar cache so new events appear immediately in Events → Active and on Calendar; saved Event Plan unchanged

## Group chat

- Per-event crew chat for owner + accepted DJs
- Messages inbox Group tab with artwork tile, deduped by event_id
- **Messages inbox empty state (2026-07-26):** DM tab: `No messages` + `Your conversations will appear here`; Group tab: `No group chats` + `Event group chats will appear here`; no DM/GC badge icons above headings
- **Messages inbox search (2026-07-26):** placeholder `Search` (was `Search conversations...`)
- **Messages inbox tab spacing (2026-07-29):** Messages/Groups segmented control uses `mb-3` above the sticky header divider so the pill border does not merge with the page divider
- Event edit posts one update message to group chat (booking-impacting fields)
- Planner does not get unread from own group messages
- Group chat page: header only (duplicate context card removed)
- **Crew chat UI polish (2026-07-15):** header shows event artwork + name + `Crew chat • N members` with inline participant avatars; compact centred system-message pills with friendlier display copy; sender names above first message in a sequence (DM-style); understated empty state; View event button matches DM booking cards (calendar icon + shared secondary button class); presentation-only — no messaging/realtime/DB changes
- **Messaging UI polish (2026-07-15):** crew chat header — text-only centre (event name + `Crew chat • N members`), avatars on dedicated row below subtitle (no clip); lighter compact View event button; tighter content-hugging system pills; updated empty-state copy; mobile Messages nav badge anchored to icon top-right
- **Crew Chat "premium polish" pass (2026-08-02):** UX-only, no messaging architecture/DB/permission changes. **Header** now states only the event name (pinned, never collapses) and a tappable "N Crew Members" line + avatar stack, both opening a new member sheet — venue/date/View Event moved out, so nothing repeats. **Event-context card** (`GroupChatEventContextCard.tsx`) reintroduced beneath the header: venue · date · time + a restyled "View Event" action (soft-primary tint, no longer visually secondary), using the shared `AnimatedExpandPanel` primitive to collapse once the user scrolls into the conversation and reappear back at the live edge (a plain scroll listener against the existing `scrollRef`, rAF-throttled, no ResizeObserver). **This is not the same "duplicate context card" removed on 2026-07-15** (line above) — that one repeated the event name/venue/date already in the header; this one is deliberately non-overlapping with the (now slimmer) header content, and the event name is intentionally *not* repeated in the card since the header states it permanently one line above. **Member sheet** (`CrewMemberListSheet.tsx`, new): reuses `BookingSheetDialog` rather than forking its chrome; lists every crew member with avatar + name (both link to `/profile/:id`) + a role badge (👑 Promoter / 🎧 DJ) derived from `ownerId` comparison — no new "acceptance status" badge, since `get_event_crew_participant_ids` only ever returns the owner unioned with *accepted* DJs, so every listed DJ is definitionally accepted and a status chip would be redundant. **Read receipts**: "Seen by Promoter" / "Seen by {name}" / "Seen by N members" on the single latest message only (any historical-message version would be noise), derived from the existing `message_reads` per-(user, event) `last_read_at` high-water mark — no schema change — via a new realtime subscription generalising DM's existing 1:1 "Seen" pattern (`isMessageSeenByReader`, reused) to N crew members. **Spacing**: crew chat's own, slightly tighter rhythm (`mb-1`/`mb-2.5` vs DM's `mb-1.5`/`mb-3.5`), added as a `spacing` variant on the *shared* `resolveMessageGroupLiClass` rather than a forked copy — DM's direct calls never pass it, so its constants and default output are byte-for-byte unchanged (locked in by an existing regression assertion plus a new one, both mutation-tested). **Perf**: `GroupChatMessageBubble`/`GroupChatSystemNotice` wrapped in `React.memo`; the three reaction callbacks changed from per-row inline closures to stable, messageId-first handlers so memo is actually effective across hundreds of messages — no virtualisation library added (none existed; that stays a follow-up, not this pass). **Sender name is now a profile link** (previously avatar-only). **Announcement slot**: `CrewChatAnnouncementBand.tsx`, a real component positioned beneath the event card that renders `null` today — reserved structurally (per its own doc comment) rather than as a visible placeholder box, since actual announcement messaging needs a `kind`/`type` column `messages` doesn't have (a schema change, explicitly out of scope). **Deliberately not done**: image sharing (task scoped it out — Crew Chat has no attachment button/fields at all today, so building it would be new feature work, not polish); typing indicators (no supporting realtime infrastructure exists anywhere in the codebase). Verified at 390px and 1280px via a temporary debug route rendering the real components with synthetic data (removed before commit): collapse/expand, empty state, member sheet (mobile bottom sheet / desktop centred dialog), avatar+name profile links, seen label. `npm run build` clean, full `npm run test:regressions` pass including a new `testCrewChatPremiumPolish` (mutation-tested on the DM-spacing-isolation and stable-callback guarantees). **Not verified**: a real authenticated crew chat end-to-end (no live Supabase session in this environment) — the live page's data wiring (new `ownerId`/`eventSetTime` fields on `EventCrewChatAccess`, the read-receipts realtime channel, the scroll-collapse listener) is exercised by type-checking and the synthetic-data harness, not a real multi-DJ conversation.

**Crew Chat "premium polish" pass, part 2 — image sharing, date separators, empty-state (2026-08-02):** completes the four items the pass above deliberately deferred. **Image sharing**: `message_attachments` gains an `event_id` column (sibling to `conversation_id`, exactly-one-of enforced by a new check constraint) via `scripts/setupEventCrewChatAttachments.sql` — **written but not applied**; the page's attachment load/realtime already fail soft (matches the existing reactions pattern) so the chat works normally until Isaac runs it, then images start flowing with no further code change. Upload pipeline is new-but-thin (`lib/groupChatAttachments.ts`, `sendEventCrewChatMessageWithAttachments`) reusing DM's bucket, validators, and pending-photo staging verbatim (`lib/dmAttachments.ts`, `lib/dm/composerPendingAttachment.ts`) rather than forking them; rendering reuses `DmMessageAttachmentGroup`/the DM lightbox as-is — no second gallery implementation. `DmMessageAttachment`'s `conversation_id` widened to `| null` alongside a new `event_id` field (same type, now genuinely dual-purpose despite the DM-flavoured name, kept to avoid a rename ripple through every existing import). **Date/time separators**: new `lib/groupChatTimestampVisibility.ts` — same TODAY/YESTERDAY/day-label clustering as DM's (`formatDmDaySeparatorLabel` reused directly), but flat, with no booking-card classification DM needs and crew chat never has. Wired in as a *trailing* separator (`wrapWithTrailingTimeSeparator` in the page), the mirror image of DM's *leading* one — crew chat's message list is `flex-col-reverse` + newest-first array (for scroll-anchoring), DM's is not, so a separator attached "before" a message in that reversed DOM order has to render textually *after* it to land visually above it; verified by hand against the reversed flex stacking order, not assumed from DM's pattern. **Empty state**: DM's own inline empty-state JSX extracted into a new shared `ChatEmptyState` (`app/components/chat/`), crew chat's rebuilt on top of it too — same typography/spacing now on both, content (icon vs. none, copy) still per-surface. **`ChatBackButton` de-duplicated**: was defined identically-in-spirit twice (`DmConversationHeader.tsx`, `GroupChatHeader.tsx`); extracted to `app/components/chat/ChatBackButton.tsx` using DM's superset (keyboard-scroll-preserving `scroll={false}`, optional `replace`), both call sites now import it. **`ComposerIconButton`** (the photo-picker button DM already had) similarly extracted in the same pass and reused by crew chat's composer rather than rebuilt. Booking cards in crew chat were scoped **out** on Isaac's call — zero booking-negotiation concept exists in crew chat today, so building a card would be new feature work, not reuse. New regression coverage: `testCrewChatTimestampSeparators` (clustering + day-boundary-takes-precedence, mirroring `testDmChatDaySeparators`'s local-time-Date discipline so it can't flake by timezone), `testCrewChatImageAttachmentsWiring`, `testChatEmptyStateComponentized`. `npm run build` and full `npm run test:regressions` (including the pre-existing suite) both clean. **Live-verified** at 1280px and 390px against a real authenticated session (dev server on a spare port, logged in as the `FTC_QA_BOTH` test account) — header, event card, day/time separators (including a live-sent message correctly starting a new time cluster), consecutive-sender grouping, composer, and mobile nav offset all confirmed against real data; **image upload itself was not exercised end-to-end** since the migration above is intentionally unapplied in this environment.

**Crew Chat image attachments — migration type-cast fix, now applied (2026-08-03):** `scripts/setupEventCrewChatAttachments.sql` (part 2, above) failed on first run in the Supabase SQL Editor with `42883 operator does not exist: text = uuid`. Root cause, found by live-reproducing the exact INSERT against the real database rather than assumed: the `message_attachments_insert_uploader` policy's crew-chat branch compared `messages.event_id` directly against the newly-added `message_attachments.event_id` (`uuid`) — but `messages.event_id` is only conditionally `uuid` (see `setupEventCrewChat.sql`'s own text→uuid migration, which backs off to `text` if any historical value isn't a valid UUID), and in this database it's still `text`. Fixed by casting both sides to `text` for that one comparison — matching the `is_event_crew_member_for_message(text)` wrapper already used everywhere else in the same file for this exact ambiguity — rather than assuming either type. Because the SQL Editor runs a pasted multi-statement script as one implicit transaction, the first (failing) run had also rolled back the otherwise-successful earlier statements (the `event_id` column add, the constraint, the index), which is why `message_attachments.event_id` briefly appeared to not exist at all even after "running the migration" — confirmed directly via `information_schema.columns` before the fix, and again after: the corrected script has now been re-run clean, `event_id` exists, the new RLS policies (`message_attachments_select_member`/`_insert_uploader`, `dm_attachments_insert_member` on `storage.objects`) are live, and Crew Chat image upload works end-to-end. DM's own attachment permissions are untouched — every change here is additive (an OR-branch), never a replacement of the DM-scoped checks.

**Crew Chat event-context card: manual reveal toggle (2026-08-03):** the card (venue/date/time + View Event) still only auto-collapses/expands on scroll (per the 2026-08-02 entry above, untouched) — the only way back to it once collapsed was scrolling all the way to the live edge. Added a small "Details ▾ / Hide ▴" toggle to the existing countdown row ("Event starts in N days"), right-aligned, chevron rotating on state change (same `transition-transform duration-200` + path shape as `EventRunSheetSection`'s own accordion chevron, inlined rather than importing from that file since the chevron there is a local, unexported helper owned by a different, concurrently-edited section). The countdown row itself moved to sit directly *above* the card instead of below it, so the card now visually expands underneath the row that controls it. The toggle reuses the page's existing `eventCardCollapsed` boolean directly — no second/duplicate collapse state, and `GroupChatEventContextCard` itself is unchanged; a manual tap is just an additional writer of the same state the scroll listener already owns. **Found and fixed one real bug during verification, not present in the original ask but necessary for it to work at all:** tapping the toggle while pinned at the live edge (the chat's default resting state) caused the card's height change to shrink the message scroller's available space, which made the browser clamp `scrollTop` down to the new (smaller) max — firing a genuine `scroll` event that the existing auto-collapse listener then read as "still at the bottom," instantly reopening the card the tap had just closed. Fixed with a single ref (`eventCardManualToggleSuppressUntilRef`, a timestamp) that makes the scroll-listener's own evaluation skip itself for 400ms right after a manual toggle click — long enough to cover the card's collapse animation plus any trailing layout-driven scroll-clamp events, short enough that it has no effect on genuine scrolling at any other time; the automatic scroll-triggered behaviour itself is byte-for-byte the same otherwise. Verified live (390px and 1280px, real authenticated session): toggle reliably collapses-and-stays-collapsed / re-expands with no snap-back; `npm run build`, `tsc --noEmit`, and the full `npm run test:regressions` suite all clean.

**Event updates are now system cards, not sender bubbles (2026-08-03):** follow-up to the compact-field-list entry below, which improved the *contents* but left the container looking like a message someone typed. **Root cause of that:** `postEventGroupChatUpdate` posts through `sendEventCrewChatMessage` with the planner's `user_id`, so the row inherited sender styling — bright primary fill, right-aligned, for the planner; left-aligned *with the planner's avatar and name* for the crew. Both read as "a person wrote this". **Fix:** a `systemAuthored` branch in `GroupChatMessageBubble` that renders event updates as an app-authored notice regardless of who triggered them — always left-aligned, no avatar, no sender name, and off the primary fill entirely. New `resolveChatSystemCardShellClass` supplies a card rather than a bubble: uniform `rounded-xl` with **no tail corner** (every user message has an asymmetric corner pointing at its sender, so dropping it is what reads as "nobody said this"), `bg-surface-raised` (#1a2230 slate) with a low-alpha cyan hairline — both existing palette tokens, no new colour, no shadow. **Reactions, highlight and the seen label are deliberately preserved** by keeping `ChatMessageBubbleShell` and only swapping its class; `isOwnMessage={false}` is passed so the reaction pill and picker anchor to the left where the card actually sits. Restyling a message type must not quietly delete capabilities from it. **Contents:** heading became a 10px uppercase cyan accent label (FTC's established section-label treatment — the clearest "app-generated" signal short of an icon); field labels 10px uppercase muted; values 13px with the **old value on the muted token and the new value at full strength plus medium weight**, so the eye lands on what it is now; field gap `gap-1` → `gap-2.5`; row max-width `76%/62%` → `72%/56%`. The previous opacity-based de-emphasis was **removed** — it existed only because the same markup had to survive both a primary-filled and a surface bubble, and the card now owns one known background, so real colour tokens give better contrast control. `denseBody` (added in the entry below) is gone with it, since the card supplies its own padding. Generation, realtime, timestamps, ordering, persistence and notifications are untouched — this is still parse-at-render over unchanged stored text. Regression assertions rewritten for the new invariants (no primary fill or shadow on the card, left-aligned branch ordered *before* the own/incoming split, left-anchored reactions, old-muted/new-emphasised, DM unaffected); **mutation-tested** by disabling `systemAuthored` and by moving the branch after the own/incoming split — each caught. `tsc`, `npm run build`, full suite clean. **Live-verified** at 390px and 1280px: card `rgb(26,34,48)` vs user bubble `rgb(79,209,255)`, radius 12px, `box-shadow: none`, left offset 12px *while signed in as the planner who sent it*, no avatar, timestamp intact, 68% width. *(Third collision this session: the concurrent agent stashed the working tree twice more mid-verification. Everything round-tripped, but one near-miss is worth recording — at one point this file's working copy was based on a pre-their-commit revision, so committing it would have silently reverted their `testHorizontalOverscrollDoesNotPanRevealAdjacentContent`. Caught by diffing before staging; the fix was `git checkout HEAD -- scripts/test-regressions.mts` and re-applying only my own hunk. **Check `git diff <file> | grep "^@@"` before staging any shared file while two agents share this tree.**)*

**Event update messages restyled as compact field lists (2026-08-03):** these are posted by `postEventGroupChatUpdate` when a planner saves booking-impacting edits. **They are not system-notice pills** — `isGroupChatSystemUpdateMessage` only matches the `Booking update:` prefix and the crew-opened notice, so an event update falls through to an ordinary `GroupChatMessageBubble` (blue/right for the planner who sent it, surface/left with avatar for the crew). That is why it read as a large chat message rather than a notification. **Approach: parse at render time, never touch what's written.** `messages.text` still stores the exact same `Event details updated:\n• Label: from → to` string — notification previews, ordering, realtime and history all read it — and a new pure module `lib/events/eventGroupChatUpdateMessage.ts` parses it back into rows for display. Two consequences worth knowing: every message *already in the database* picks up the new layout, and requirement-6 ("don't change generation") is satisfied literally rather than approximately. The only edit to the generator is that its literal prefix now references the shared `EVENT_GROUP_CHAT_UPDATE_PREFIX` constant so format and parse cannot drift; output is byte-identical. **Presentation:** heading displays "Event updated" (13px semibold) instead of the stored "Event details updated:"; each changed field becomes a two-line row — label, then `from → to` — at 12px; bubble padding `px-4 py-2.5` → `px-3 py-2` via a new additive `denseBody` option on `resolveChatMessageBubbleShellClass` (DM never sets it, so DM output is unchanged); row max-width `85%/72%` → `76%/62%`. "Event name" displays as just "Name" since the heading already says Event — display-only, the stored label and the change detection are untouched. **De-emphasis is opacity-based, not `text-ftc-text-muted`**, because the identical markup renders on a primary-coloured bubble for the planner and a surface bubble for the crew; a fixed muted token would lose contrast on the blue. Parsing is lenient — a non-update, an empty body, a malformed row or a `Booking update:` notice all return `null` and fall back to the plain-text bubble, so nothing can render as an empty card. New `testEventUpdateMessagePresentation` round-trips the real generator output through the parser (including a value containing colons and en dashes, which naive splitting breaks), asserts the stored prefix is unchanged, covers the four fallback cases, and pins the typography/padding/width; **mutation-tested** by changing the stored prefix and by breaking the label override — each caught, then reverted. `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, full suite clean. **Live-verified** at 390px and 1280px: heading "Event updated" at 13px/600, rows `Name / Venue / Set time` (only changed fields), bubble padding `8px 12px` (was `10px 16px`), width 278/390 ≈ 71% on mobile and 388/672 ≈ 58% on desktop. *(Mid-task the concurrent session ran `git stash` to isolate its own overscroll fix, which temporarily removed this work from disk; it returned intact on their pop and they committed as `2fb420b`. Nothing was lost, but it is the second time this session that two agents in one working tree have collided — worth staggering if it keeps happening.)*

**Incoming avatar timestamps removed from Crew Chat (2026-08-03):** incoming rows showed a small time under every sender avatar, duplicating what the day separators ("TODAY"/"YESTERDAY") and the grouped time separators between clusters already say. **Scope turned out to be Crew Chat only** — `DmIncomingMessageLayout` had already dropped its visible timestamp (its `<time>` is `hidden` in both branches), so only `IncomingChatMessageLayout` still rendered one. The fix therefore just brings group chat to the pattern DM already established, rather than inventing anything: the cluster-end branch's visible `<time>` becomes `<time … hidden>` (kept in the DOM as machine-readable markup, exactly like DM's), and `CHAT_INCOMING_ROW_GRID_CLUSTER_END_CLASS` (`grid-rows-[auto_auto] gap-y-0.5`) is dropped from the wrapper — that second grid row and its `gap-y` existed *only* to hold the timestamp, so removing them is what naturally closes the gap rather than leaving a 2px dead band. Three things this orphaned were deleted as dead code: `CHAT_INCOMING_TIMESTAMP_CELL_CLASS`, `CHAT_INCOMING_ROW_GRID_CLUSTER_END_CLASS`, and the `showTimestamp` prop on the layout (plus its pass-through, and the `showTimestamp?: boolean` field on `resolveIncomingGroupLiClass`'s options — which was declared but never destructured, i.e. already a no-op). **Not changed:** the 3rem avatar column width (narrowing it would shift every bubble, including DM's, which shares `CHAT_INCOMING_ROW_GRID_CLASS`), avatar positioning, sender names, day/time separators, and the **outgoing** timestamp under own-message bubbles — the task scoped explicitly to incoming avatars, so outgoing was left alone; this does mean incoming and outgoing are now asymmetric, flagged for a product call rather than decided unilaterally. New `testIncomingChatMessagesHaveNoAvatarTimestamp` asserts every `<time>` in *both* incoming layouts is `hidden`, the timestamp cell/row constants stay deleted, and the avatar cell / sender slot / separator wiring survive; **mutation-tested** by restoring a visible timestamp and by re-adding the reserved grid row — each caught, then reverted. `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` and the full suite all clean. **Verified against real rendered HTML** rather than only source text: `renderToStaticMarkup` on the component across all four group positions (`first`/`middle`/`last`/`standalone`) confirmed zero visible `<time>`, the hidden one intact, sender name present, avatar present on cluster ends and the spacer slot otherwise, and no `grid-rows-[auto_auto]`. *(A browser check wasn't possible this session: another chat's `next dev` holds this directory — Next refuses a second dev server in the same dir — and that session's browser is signed in as the QA planner account, which has no group chats. Not killed, deliberately. The static render check above covers the same ground more durably.)*

**Crew Chat opens with event details collapsed (2026-08-03):** the card previously opened expanded, so entering a chat showed less of the conversation than it could. It now starts collapsed (`useState(true)`) — the conversation is the reason anyone opens this screen, and venue/date/time stay one tap away on **Details**. State is deliberately **not persisted**: plain local `useState` already resets to collapsed on every mount, and a matching `setEventCardCollapsed(true)` was added to `loadAccess`'s existing per-event reset block (beside `setMessages([])`/`setMemberSheetOpen(false)`) so switching directly between two events' chats also starts collapsed even when React reuses the component instance instead of remounting. Simplicity over persistence, per the task. No layout, styling, animation, or scroll-anchoring change; **View Event** is untouched (it remains conditional on `showViewEventAction={openedFromMessages}`, i.e. it only renders when the chat was opened from the Messages inbox — verified present with the correct href in both collapsed and expanded states via `?from=dm`). Regression coverage gained three assertions: the `useState(true)` default, the per-event reset, and the call-site count tightened from 2 to exactly 3 (declaration + toggle + reset) so a fourth writer still trips it. **Both new assertions mutation-tested** (reverting the default to `false`, and deleting the reset — each caught, then reverted). **Live-verified** at 390px and 1280px against a real authenticated session: entry state collapsed (`aria-expanded="false"`, `grid-template-rows: 0fr`), tapping Details expands (`1fr`), tapping Hide collapses again, scrolling to top and bottom leaves the label unchanged, 13 messages and the composer render normally on both viewports. *(The card's 200ms animation itself still can't be observed in the in-app Browser pane — `document.hidden === true`, rAF frozen — but the collapsed-on-entry default is initial render state, not animation, so it verifies deterministically there regardless.)*

**Crew Chat event card is now toggle-only — scroll never changes it (2026-08-03):** the card's original behaviour (added 2026-08-02) collapsed it once the reader scrolled off the live edge and re-expanded it on return. Once the explicit **Details/Hide** toggle shipped, that automatic behaviour became both redundant and actively bad: crossing the 24px threshold re-opened the card, and a small scroll back up closed it again, so the surface rearranged itself while the reader was simply reading. Isaac's call, and the right one — a chat that moves under you takes control away from the user. **Removed:** the rAF-throttled scroll listener that drove `setEventCardCollapsed`, the `EVENT_CARD_COLLAPSE_THRESHOLD_PX` constant, and `eventCardManualToggleSuppressUntilRef` (which existed *only* to stop that listener fighting a manual tap — dead once the listener went). `setEventCardCollapsed` now appears exactly twice in the page: its `useState` declaration and the toggle's own `onClick`. **Kept:** the scroll-position compensation from the entry below — tapping the toggle still resizes the card, so the conversation would still jump without it. Also kept is that compensation's deferral to `useChatScroll`'s re-pin when already at the live edge; that observer is independent of the removed listener. Nothing else changed: sending, reactions, image viewing, unread, auto-scroll-on-new-message, and all spacing/typography/animation/card styling are untouched. The regression test gained three invariants — `setEventCardCollapsed` occurring exactly twice, the threshold constant staying gone, and no `addEventListener("scroll")` on this page — and the first was **mutation-tested** by reintroducing a scroll-driven listener, which it caught. `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` and the full suite all clean. **Still not visually verified** for the same reason as below (Browser pane reports `document.hidden === true`, rAF frozen, so CSS transitions never advance) — worth a real-device check that the card now holds its state through a full scroll up and back.

**Crew Chat: conversation no longer jumps when Details expands/collapses (2026-08-03):** follow-up to the toggle entry above. **Layout cause (traced, not assumed):** the page's outer wrapper is `fixed inset-0 … overflow-hidden`, so the window never scrolls — the scrolling element is the internal `scrollRef` div, and the event card is a *sibling above it* inside a fixed-height flex column. The scroller is `flex-1`, so it absorbs the card's height change entirely: growing the card by H shrinks the scroller's `clientHeight` by H and pushes its top edge down by H, while `scrollHeight` (the messages) is untouched. With `scrollTop` left alone the whole conversation therefore slides down by H — the reported jump — and slides back up on collapse. **Fix:** capture distance-from-the-live-edge *before* the toggle flips state, then hold it constant across the resize via a new shared pure helper `resolveScrollTopPreservingDistanceFromBottom` in `lib/useChatScroll.ts` (sits beside the existing `getChatMaxScrollTop`/`shouldKeepChatPinnedAfterLayoutChange` rather than starting a new module). Holding that distance *is* the compensation — `clientHeight` shrinking by H raises `maxScrollTop` by H, so `scrollTop` moves by the same H and the two cancel — and deriving it from live `scrollHeight`/`clientHeight` stays correct even if content height changes in the same frame, which a raw "add the measured H" would not. Because `AnimatedExpandPanel` animates `grid-template-rows` over 200ms rather than resizing in one step, the correction is applied by a **`ResizeObserver`** on every frame the height actually changes (a one-shot correction at click time would only catch the first frame), torn down after 320ms and on unmount. **Deliberately skipped when already at the live edge** (`distanceFromBottom <= CHAT_NEAR_BOTTOM_THRESHOLD_PX`): `useChatScroll`'s own ResizeObserver already re-pins to the bottom there, which is the same visual outcome, so writing `scrollTop` from both places would have them fight. No spacing, typography, animation or card styling changed (verified by grepping the diff for `className`/`style=`/spacing/duration lines — only comments matched). The scroll-*driven* auto collapse/expand was left untouched at this point — **it has since been removed entirely, see the entry directly above.** New `testCrewChatEventCardToggleScrollCompensation` covers the arithmetic (expand/collapse symmetry, pinned case, negative/short-content clamping) plus the wiring invariants; both the ordering assertion and the arithmetic assertion were **mutation-tested** (each fails against a deliberately broken implementation, then reverted). `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` and the full regression suite all clean. **Not visually verified:** the in-app Browser pane reports `document.hidden === true` and `requestAnimationFrame` never fires there, so CSS transitions never advance and neither the card resize nor the ResizeObserver correction can be exercised — an early "0px drift" reading was discarded as meaningless once that was found. Needs a real-device/desktop-browser confirmation that expand and collapse are visually still. *(Also worth knowing for future sessions: running `npm run build` while the dev server is running clobbers the shared `.next` directory and makes every route 404 — stop the dev server before building, or `rm -rf .next` after.)*

## Copy / UX polish (2026-07)

- "Create event" sentence case (not "Create Event")
- No trailing periods on several confirmation dialogs and empty states
- **Finish-time validation copy (2026-07-31):** `EVENT_FINISH_NOT_AFTER_START_ERROR` (`lib/bookingDateTime.ts`) dropped its trailing full stop — "Finish time must be later than the start time"; validation logic unchanged
- **SoundCloud field moved to Links section (2026-07-31):** `EditProfileForm.tsx`'s SoundCloud `ProfileFormField` now renders inside the "Links" fieldset (after TikTok) instead of "DJ / Artist details" — pure JSX relocation, still gated by the same `showDjFields` check so non-DJ profiles see it exactly as before (not shown). Validation (`normalizeSoundCloudInput`), save mapping, placeholder text, and error display untouched. "DJ / Artist details" now holds just the genre picker; "Links" holds Instagram, TikTok, and SoundCloud together.
- **Profile photo viewer close button removed (2026-07-31):** `ProfilePhotoViewer.tsx`'s floating ✕ button was redundant — tap-outside (`handleBackdropClick`) and Escape already dismiss the enlarged photo overlay. Removed only the button's JSX; backdrop click, Escape, drag detection, and all animations/transitions unchanged.
- **Copy-consistency pass, validation/error messages (2026-07-31):** dropped trailing full stops from ~90 single-sentence validation/error strings across auth (`login`, `signup`), profile (`EditProfileForm.tsx`, `lib/user/currentUser.ts`, `lib/user/profileFormUtils.ts` — Instagram/TikTok/SoundCloud link validation, username checks), events (`lib/events.ts`, `lib/events/eventCoverImage.ts`, `lib/eventCrewChat.ts`), bookings (`lib/bookingRequests.ts`, `lib/bookings/sendBookingRequestsFlow.ts`, `EventsPageClient.tsx`, `bookings/page.tsx`), DM (`lib/userBlocks.ts`, `lib/userReports.ts`, `DmReportFormModal.tsx`), and `lib/client/generate-event-plan.ts` — matching the no-trailing-period style already used elsewhere. Multi-sentence messages ("X. Y.") and DM chat system-message copy were deliberately left untouched (different category, out of scope). No logic/behaviour changed; two `test-regressions.mts` assertions and one test fixture string that hardcoded the old copy were updated to match.
- **Copy-consistency pass 2, app-wide audit (2026-07-31):** full follow-up sweep (validation/error/warning/loading, not just validation/error) confirmed the pass above was complete for its own scope and found ~18 remaining single-sentence stragglers it hadn't covered — mostly one-line success/notice toasts, which share the exact same transient-feedback UI slot as error messages (confirmed against the existing canonical no-period examples in `lib/design/inlineTabFeedback.ts`: `"Event updated"`, `"Booking request cancelled"`, `"N events removed from history"`) but had been out of the prior pass's narrower "validation/error" scope. Dropped trailing periods in `EventDeleteCancelButton.tsx` (delete-event body), `DeleteAccountSection.tsx` + `app/api/account/delete/route.ts` (both genuinely user-facing — the route's `error` JSON is thrown and rendered, unlike `app/api/generate-event/route.ts`'s, which is discarded by its only caller and was correctly left alone), `SendBookingRequestsPanel.tsx`, `CancelAcceptedBookingButton.tsx`, `BookingRateModeField.tsx`, `EditProfileForm.tsx` (genre-limit field error), `events/[eventId]/page.tsx` (5 booking-action success notices), and `bookings/page.tsx` (3 success notices, incl. one with a pluralised count). Also fixed one missing terminator: `EventDeleteCancelButton.tsx`'s cancel-event body is 3 sentences but its 3rd was missing a full stop while the first 2 had one — added it, since multi-sentence messages get periods throughout, not just single ones. `events/[eventId]/page.tsx`'s cancelled-booking notice concatenates an optional warning sentence onto the base message (`${baseMessage} ${warning}`); moved the separating period into the template literal (`${baseMessage}. ${warning}`) instead of the string literal, so the standalone case loses its period while the concatenated case's grammar is byte-for-byte unchanged. Loading-button "…" wording: audited ~30 instances and found it's a genuine, long-standing 4-way mix (`"Verb-ing…"` with ellipsis, ~25 instances — the de facto dominant convention; `"VERB-ING"` uppercase no-ellipsis on a couple of specific dated CTAs from 2026-07-26; `"Save event"` no-ellipsis descriptive-phrase on the event edit-form save button; even mixed within buttons sharing the exact same uppercase `EVENT_DETAIL_BTN_PRIMARY(_WIDE)` class) — not something a copy-only pass can safely converge on without picking a direction that amounts to a design decision, so left it as-is except for the one unambiguous, provably-wrong case: `SendBookingRequestsPanel.tsx`'s own send-button label mixed `"Confirming"` (no ellipsis) and `"Sending…"` (ellipsis) on the *same* button across its two modes — normalised to `"Sending"` to match its sibling and the file's existing no-ellipsis intent. Capitalisation audit (grep for lowercase-starting quoted strings matching validation/error keywords) found no genuine user-facing offenders — the only lowercase-starting matches were dev-only React-context invariant errors (`"useX must be used within Y"`), which are not UI copy. Verified: clean `npm run build`, full `npm run test:regressions` pass, and a targeted before/after grep diff confirming only the intended strings changed. No logic/behaviour changed.
- **Loading-button ellipsis, standardised app-wide (2026-07-31):** the ellipsis-variance question left open above was resolved by an explicit decision — no ellipsis on any loading-button label, ever, keeping each button's own existing text-case convention (a button that already renders `SAVING` via a CSS `uppercase` class keeps doing so automatically; a normal-case button's loading label stays normal-case — no styling touched, since forcing literal all-caps text into non-uppercase-styled buttons would create a case mismatch against that same button's own resting-state label, which the decision explicitly ruled out). Removed the trailing `"..."` from 30 loading-button labels across 23 files — every `loading`/`saving`/`sending`/`submitting`/`deleting`/`removing`/`archiving`/`busy`/`updating`/`creating`/`confirming`/`blocking`/`unblocking`/`hiding`/`starting`/`retrying`/`messaging`/`signingOut`/`resettingPassword`-style boolean-gated button label in the app, found via exhaustive grep (three-dot ellipsis, the real `…` character, and boolean-ternary pattern matching, cross-checked against each other) — see commit for the full file list. Also updated one test fixture (`scripts/test-history-remove-confirm.ts`) that hardcoded the old `"Deleting..."` value as an arbitrary prop, even though the test never asserts on that literal text, for full repo-wide consistency. Left untouched: `EditProfileForm.tsx`'s `"Checking…"` — a live-region *status* message next to the username field while an async check runs, not a button label, so out of this pass's explicit button-only scope; validation/error/success/toast copy (a separate, already-completed pass — see the two entries above); DM/chat system messages; input placeholders ending in `...` (e.g. `"Message..."`, `"Search events, DJs, venues..."`). Verified zero remaining ellipsis on any loading-button label via repo-wide grep. No behaviour, loading-state, disabled-logic, or styling changed — confirmed via diff review (every changed line is a pure string-literal edit).
- Desktop planner UX brought into parity with mobile (wording, validation, calendar cards, today/selected styling) without copying mobile layout

- **Event detail page (2026-07-14):** shorter hero (~25%), icon-led event summary block, compact lineup booking cards aligned with DM cards, Invite DJs action label, cancel event moved below bookings, flat action cards, dashed run sheet empty state

- **Calendar day selection:** desktop grid highlight tied to open day panel (`actionDate`); closing the panel clears the outline instantly (no transition fade) and blurs focus; Today styling unchanged
- **Page load speed:** optimistic auth in `OnboardingGuard` (cached session renders immediately); profile + nav role persisted to localStorage (userId-scoped) and seeded at module load; profile fetch starts immediately when session exists; auth guard runs profile fetch in parallel with session check; events list + group inbox caches survive hard refresh via localStorage; profile cache + deduped fetches in `lib/user/currentUser.ts`; nav skips redundant profile load when guard profile exists

- `1b0b074` — fixed nested scroll-trap regression from `be1ffff`: `clamp(...,640px)` was nearly full-viewport on mobile and, combined with the panel's `overscroll-contain`/`touch-pan-y`, trapped touch-scroll inside the DJ list. Switched to `max-h-80 sm:max-h-[420px]` (moderate, non-viewport-scaling)
